The addresses are then read afresh (a read begun after the change; an older read that answers late is let go, `app/company-mail.ts`), and where they can't be read the line goes by what the routines said, so it never shows what was before the change. While a change is on its way the chip names the agents as they will be and waits; what could not be done is said under the address (no model account that can pay, or the cloud's own reason). An address nobody answers shows "Choose who answers". The form that adds an address has the chip too (one agent, or nobody), so a new address goes to its agent as it is made. Every member sees the addresses and who answers them; only an owner or admin changes them (`views/mailbox.ts`; `views/settings-hooks.ts` gets the card and form slots; `cloud/routines.ts` saves a new routine that starts on a mailbox; a menu row may be a tick box, `ui/context-menu.ts`).
- The Mailbox answers email as a mail app does: Reply, Reply all and Forward under a thread (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "don't like the answering flow in the mailbox - you should get similar answer/reply buttons as in the real gmail/outlook app, not the chatcomposer to tell other agents what to reply"). The line at a thread's foot that told the handling agent what to answer is gone. One of the three buttons opens the answer in their place, with the member's picture beside it: who it goes to as chips (for Reply where the email asks its answers to go, its Reply-To, else its sender; everyone else the message went to as well for Reply all, in To or Cc as the message had them, the company's own addresses left out; nobody yet for Forward), its text, which grows as it is written, Send and a bin that lets it go. Send sends it as the member's own email from the thread's address, in the thread (`replyTo`, migration `20260930740000`); that address is the answer's from the moment it is opened, and no other stands in when it is paused meanwhile (the cloud says so): it stands there with their picture, and the sender's mail program threads it under the email it answers. A reply to an email the company sent follows it up, to who that went to. Reply all shows only when it goes to more people than Reply. A forward is a new email with the message's sender, date, subject, recipients and text under the member's own words. The answer's Writer chip gives a reply to an agent instead, as the Write window's does: the member then writes what it should say, and the words go to that agent as their own message with the email's subject and sender as quotes. An answer is kept with its thread while the member reads elsewhere; one the cloud did not take is shown again in its thread with the reason, and sent again as it stands it is the same email. Account mail and viewers get no answer buttons. The Write window and the answer share their lines, chips, sending and Writer menu (`views/mailbox.ts`). Needs migration `20260930740000` live first.
- An app's card in a message stands under the words, not in their bubble, and shows the app as the drawer does (Jonas, 13 Nov 2026, on a card in a chat: "don't like this visual, we can do better"). Before, the card was a white box in the bubble's grey, with a small black emblem and the app's whole `about` in three lines of small print that ended in "· App". Now a message's cards are a row of their own (`.app-cards`, a part of its own from `markdownBubbles`), drawn on the thread under the words' bubble; a card is 360 px at most and shows the app's emblem at the drawer's size in the drawer's colour for it (the member's own icon and colour, or its group's: `appLook` in `views/shell.ts`, which the rail fills as it draws), the name, one line of `about` (whole on a hover; "App" where the app has none or is not read yet) and a chevron. On a hover the border darkens, the emblem grows a little and the chevron steps right; no lift and no shadow. A sentence that names the same app twice gets one card, and the app's name in the sentence takes the words' colour, a little heavier, in place of the link blue, as its card stands right under it. A card in a list's item or a table's cell stays where it is, in the bubble. The wall's chip and its opened card follow the new parts. The line "App · opens on its own page" is gone.
- Every figure now starts in a colour the picker offers (Jonas, 13 Nov 2026: "the planets default color is not an available color from the palette - shouldn't we change the default colors to fit the ones we offer?"). The Planet's own shade moves from the retired teal (#5ec0b5) to Sky, the Heart's (#fb672a) to Red and the Flame's (#fa752e) to Orange, so its swatch is ringed when it is chosen; the artwork is unchanged, and `avatar-appearance.js` keeps the three drawn shades only to find the eyes. An agent whose file names no colour follows its figure's new shade; a colour an `agent.md` names is unchanged. A test now holds every figure's own shade to the palette.
- A card with its answer reads as the exchange it was, in a thread (Jonas, 13 Nov 2026, on an answered question after the app card's redraw: "same with answered questions - could we improve that visually too?"). Before, an answered card kept its frame, its label row ("QUESTION · Co-Founder · 04:01 AM") and an "Answered" badge, with the title greyed and the answer in a grey pill inside the frame over "You · 04:02 AM". Now what the agent asked is the agent's bubble (the title in bold, the body under it, a long one still behind Show more), and a question's answer is the member's own bubble on their side; another member's answer stands on the left with their name under it, and an answer on its way has "Sending" under it. Anything else done with a card is one quiet line under the question with a decided approval's mark: "Saved to the company vault", "Handed back", "Sent", "Skipped"; a card past its time has the line with no mark. A visual choice keeps its pictures under the question, the chosen one marked. There is no frame and no badge; the label row stays for a screen reader, and each part tells its time on a hover as a message does (`data-sent-at`). When the member answers, their bubble rises into place as a sent message does and a line's mark pops. A question or form passed with Skip now says "Skipped", not "Declined", which read as the answer being no. A card that waits keeps its frame and controls, and the questions dock draws the card as before (`inThread` on the card's context, set by the conversation only).
- A coding session whose change was refused at saving time for its files says so to members (own code storage, the import's follow-ups: the cross-check's follow-up of seam 8; migration `20260930743000`). For the coding executor's landing codes, from the failed turn's code or the code at the end of its summary, the session's note reads "The change was not saved: it holds file names that Qorpera cannot keep. The agent has been told to rename them and try again." (Danish "Ændringen blev ikke gemt: den har filnavne, som Qorpera ikke kan gemme. Agenten har fået besked om at omdøbe dem og prøve igen.") for `CODING_LAND_NAMES_REFUSED`, and "The change was not saved: it holds a file that is too large for Qorpera to keep. The agent has been told to take it out and try again." ("Ændringen blev ikke gemt: den har en fil, der er for stor til, at Qorpera kan gemme den. Agenten har fået besked om at tage den ud og prøve igen.") for `CODING_LAND_FILE_TOO_LARGE`, where it read "The session stopped because of an internal error …". The code decides before any word of the summary is read, so a refused path named like a login or a limit picks no other sentence; a workspace refused for the same names still says the session could not start. Every other failure reads as before, and a landing from an executor before the codes too (`ui/run-failure.ts`).
- A coding session whose workspace could not be made for its files says so to members (own code storage, the import's follow-ups, part N; the lead's call 8). When the failed turn's code, or the code at the end of its summary, is `CODE_HOST_NAMES_FOLDER_REFUSES`, `CODE_HOST_NAMES_NOT_UTF8`, `CODE_HOST_NAMES_NOT_NFC` or `CODE_HOST_NAMES_DIFFER_BY_CASE`, the session's note reads "The session could not start: the code holds file names that Qorpera cannot keep. Trying again will not help. The agent knows what to do next." (Danish "Sessionen kunne ikke starte: koden har filnavne, som Qorpera ikke kan gemme. Det hjælper ikke at prøve igen. Agenten ved, hvad der skal ske nu."), and for `CODE_HOST_FILE_TOO_LARGE` "The session could not start: the code holds a file that is too large for Qorpera to keep. Trying again will not help. The agent knows what to do next." ("Sessionen kunne ikke starte: koden har en fil, der er for stor til, at Qorpera kan gemme den. Det hjælper ikke at prøve igen. Agenten ved, hvad der skal ske nu."), where it read "The session stopped because of an internal error … a follow-up continues it." The code decides before any word of the agent's summary is read, so a refused path named like a login or a limit no longer picks another sentence. A usage limit still comes first; every other failure, a landing refused for names among them, reads as before (`ui/run-failure.ts`).
- Storage's done line says the work that came along outside the main version (own code storage, step 6, the import's follow-ups, item 1: `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP6-FOLLOW-UPS-PLAN.md` section 1, as `STEP6-BUILD.md`'s last section fixes the words; migration `20260930741000`). The code list's `otherWork: {lines, changes}` and the repository's `changes` are read leniently (anything not as expected is left out alone). After the earlier-changes sentence the line says "It also brought {lines} other line(s) of work, with {changes} change(s) that were not in the main version." (Danish "Den havde også … arbejdsspor med, med … ændring(er), som ikke var i hovedversionen."), then "An agent can bring that work into the main version." ("En agent kan føje det arbejde til hovedversionen.") only while the place's last change is change 1. With no `otherWork` (every list before 741000, and imports with no other work) the line reads exactly as before. No word of git, a branch, a commit, a ref or a merge.
- A group forming in the apps drawer: held over another app's middle, a plate in that app's colour springs up behind it and breathes, the app steps back onto a second card as a group's front card would, and the dragged app shrinks and hides its name; over a group, its cards part a little. Drawer tiles are never text-selected by a drag (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "make an animation too when hovering a group creation").
- Dragging in the apps drawer is decided where it is let go, and dropping one app on another makes a group at once (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "you can ungroup but not group. if i drag interfaces over each other and let go it should create a group. dragging is not fully working in general for the interafaces"). Before, a drop did only what a wait had set up (450 ms resting on another app's middle to group, 120 ms on an edge to move), so a quick drag did nothing, and a drop past the last app did nothing. Now letting go over another app's middle puts the dragged one into it (a group: into it; an app: a new group of the two, named from their shared first word or "Group", opened, renamed from its menu; no name dialog), over another place takes that place, and past the last app makes it last (`ui/app-grid-drag.ts`). While dragging, the others still make way after 120 ms on an edge and the app under the pointer is marked after 150 ms.
- The company's group colours: `apps/groups.md` may hold one note line `<!-- group colours: <id>=<colour>, … -->` (`@qorpera/cloud-client/app-groups`), which the drawer reads. A group and everything in it are drawn in its company colour unless the member picked their own (`appColours`: the member's, the company's, the group it stands in's, its first app's).
- Storage shows what a place holds while code is brought into it (own code storage, step 6, the import's follow-ups, item 2: `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP6-FOLLOW-UPS-PLAN.md` section 2; seen live 13 Nov 2026 00:11Z, where the folder showed only `restoring.json` under the done line until it was opened again). When the company's code list shows that the import of a place came, moved a step or ended (in, refused or failed), or shows for the first time how it stands (an import can end before Storage's first read of the list), Storage reads that folder again at once if the member is in it or below it (`CodeImportHost.changed`), whether or not the folder's hint said so; a change that comes while another read runs is kept for after it, a folder the step took away goes up to the place, and a read that finds what is shown draws nothing again (no rows fade in twice, focus stays). While Recents shows, the read waits until Documents shows again, so Recents is never emptied by it. The place's mark `code/<owner>/<name>/restoring.json` is left out of the folder (and its count) exactly while the list's `bringing` names that place; at every other time it shows, for the operator's runbook. One item is counted as "1 item" ("1 element"), no longer "1 items". Web only, no backend change.
- An import a member watches in Storage says its end as the page says it after a reload (review of #1710, F4): a file's import that ends too large says the file's 256 MB, not GitHub's 1 GB; the name is the list's (GitHub's spelling as first asked, or the place's for a file) once a read shows it; an ask whose answer was lost and whose import ended refused since says so, while one that only finds an older failure still says it may not have started; and a "not brought in" goes once the code is in after all or is being brought in again (`app/views/code-import.ts`).
- "Bring code in" in Storage (own code storage, step 6, the import: `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP6-IMPORT-BUILD.md` section 6 "Members", as `STEP6-BUILD.md`'s last section changes it; migration `20260930736000`). In `code/` and each owner's folder under it, an owner or admin has the button "Bring code in" (Danish "Hent kode ind"), drawn as "Download the code". It asks one question, "Where is the code?", with one field for a GitHub address (`github.com/acme/shop` with or without `https://` or `www.`, with `.git`, a trailing slash or a page of the project after it, git's `git@github.com:acme/shop.git`, or `acme/shop` alone; anything else is "That is not a GitHub address.") and "Qorpera copies it with its history. Nothing changes on GitHub."; the project lands at `code/<owner>/<name>` in lower case, and the member is never asked for a path. On the folder of a GitHub copy the button is "Bring it into Qorpera" and asks only "Bring {name} into Qorpera?". While it runs, the line under the top line (above the download's) says "Bringing in {name}:" with one of five steps (Getting it from GitHub… · Reading the file… · Checking it… · Waiting for the agents to finish what they are doing… · Putting it in place…), read from the list's `bringing` again every 5 seconds while the member is in the code's folders, so it outlives a reload; the code's folders then say "{name} is brought in." with Open, and its own folder "Brought in from GitHub on {date}. {count} earlier changes came with it. The copy on GitHub stays as it is." (or from a file). One not brought in says "{name} was not brought in: {why}" with one of twelve reasons (the design's ten, "agents are still working on it on GitHub; try again when they are done" and "its history has old git objects Qorpera can't take in yet"), in the question when the ask was refused and in the line from the list's `lastImport`, to owners and admins only; a reason this version does not know gets "it could not be brought in just now; try again", never its code. An ask whose answer never came (no network, the 15 s deadline, a gateway's error, an answer that is not as expected) claims no outcome: "Checking whether {name} started…" until a read asked after it shows it on its way or in, else "{name} may not have started." with Try again (asked again, the open import is answered), and after two minutes of failed reads "Qorpera could not be reached, so it is not known whether {name} started. Open this folder again in a moment." On a cloud whose list has no `bringing` key (or one this version cannot read) nothing of it shows and the code's folders ask the list once, not again; a cloud without `qorpera_request_code_import` hides the button for good (`app/views/code-import.ts`, `cloud/code-downloads.ts`; the GitHub copy's line moved there from `code-download.ts`, whose list read is now shared).
- Storage's download line says the file is ready, and a GitHub project's working copy says what it is (own code storage, step 6, the follow-ups: `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP6-IMPORT-BUILD.md` sections 3.4 and 6; seen in the founder's own company, where the line stayed "Downloading…" after the browser had saved the file). Once a download of the code, or of a file in Storage's window, is handed to the browser, the line reads "{name} is ready: your browser saves it in your downloads." (Danish "{name} er klar: din browser gemmer den i dine overførsler."): the page cannot know when the browser has saved it. Every other state of the download is as it was. On the folder of a GitHub copy (`code/<owner>/<name>` and below, a folder that holds a GitHub project's working copy and is not code kept in Qorpera) the same line under the top line reads "Copy of the GitHub project {name}" (Danish "Kopi af GitHub-projektet {name}"), with the project's name as GitHub spells it, for every member; no button yet. It comes from the new `githubCopies` key of `qorpera_list_code_repositories` (migration `20260930735000`), `[{owner, name, github}]`, where `github` is `Owner/Name` by GitHub's rule and is the folder's owner/name in lower case. A cloud without the key, or a key that is not as expected in any part, names no copy and leaves the rest of the list as it was, so the web can ship first (`cloud/code-downloads.ts`, `app/views/code-download.ts`, `app/views/company-folder.ts`).
- Icon and colour for each app in the drawer, and Generate icon (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "change the icons of the interfaces and have a generate icon button too for them to create a new costum one based on it's name or contents", then "still missing a selection of colors from a pallette"; design page 6gSTTCcz3D8AjBpV3GmrH6; migration `20260930732000`, SQL test 1225). An app's menu has **Icon and colour** (a panel beside the menu, `ui/app-look-picker.ts`, a new `panel` entry of `ui/context-menu.ts`): the palette row of the figure picker (Default and the 11 colours; for an app in a group it sets the group's colour), **Made for this app** (the app's own icons, `apps/<name>/icons/<id>.svg`, read when the panel opens or one is picked), **All icons** (the 24 built-in ones, 14 new since today, drawn in `ui/interface-emblem.ts`), **Generate icon** for an agent's app and **Back to the app's own icon**. A group's **Colour** is the same palette row. A pick is the member's own (`emblems` in their apps layout, left out when empty; Reset sidebar counts and clears it) and is drawn on the tile in the app's colour; a custom icon is drawn by `appIconEmblem` from the shared reader `@qorpera/cloud-client/app-icons`, so a file it cannot read is never drawn. Generate icon sends the app's agent (its manifest's `owner`, else the Co-Founder) "Please draw 3 new icons for <name> (apps/<name>), from its name and what it shows." in its thread (`CompanyController.requestAppIcons`); the tile breathes and the panel shows three placeholders until a new icon is read (every 10 s, at most 10 minutes). The Colour submenu of a loose app is gone (the panel has it).
- Apps take their group's colour in the drawer, and a member picks colours (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "I would like for all the icons to share the same color as their group", "you should also be able to change their colors" and "the icons shouldn't turn white when selected"; migration `20260930731000`, SQL test 1224). A group is drawn in its first app's colour, a group in a group in its group's, and every app in a group in the group's (`appColours` in cloud/app-groups.ts). Right-click a group or a loose app: **Colour** ▸ Default, then the eleven palette colours by the figure picker's names (Charcoal, Brown, Red, Orange, Gold, Green, Sky, Blue, Purple, Pink, Slate), each with its swatch and the chosen one checked; an app in a group offers none. The choice is the member's own, in their apps layout (`colours`, left out when there are none, so a write without colours is what an older cloud takes); Reset sidebar counts and takes it away, and Ungroup takes the group's colour with it. The open page's tile is now a soft wash of its own colour with its emblem kept in that colour, no longer white on the selection colour. New icon `palette`.
- The Sites page is redone as the Mailbox was (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "the sites page needs a redo/polish similar to what we did with the mailbox. needs to feel easy, minimalistic and pleasing"). No grey cards: the page's title and one line ("Share a site with its link and password.", or for a member who is no owner or admin "Only founders and admins can see passwords or take sites offline."), then one row per site on a hairline: the Sites globe in the app's blue (grey when the site is off), the site's title, a dot and Live (Publishing… or Off), and for a site made from code "Version 3 · What's new" with the link to What's new (the version is read with What's new: `siteVersions` in the company's state). Under it the link as a pill (`sites.qorpera.com/<name>`; a press copies it, the arrow opens the site in a new tab) and, for an owner or admin, the password as a pill, hidden as dots, with an eye to show it; a press on the pill copies it, a hidden one too, read there and then and handed to the clipboard as it is read (a ClipboardItem of a promise, so Safari copies it within the press). The two pills stand one over the other, as wide as each other, and side by side where the page is 48em or wider. What is done rarely is in the row's "⋯" menu (the shared context menu): Change password, which opens a question with the field, Make a new password, Cancel and Save password, and says there why a password was refused, keeping what was typed; and Take offline, in red, with today's questions. What just happened is said in a small dark toast at the foot ("The link is copied.", "The password is changed.", "Wristband shop is offline."); when the browser will not copy, the text is shown whole in its pill, selected, and the toast says "Press ⌘C to copy." (Ctrl+C elsewhere). No file counts, sizes, folders, machines or ports any more; when and by whom a site was published is the tip of its state. No site yet: the Co-Founder, "No sites yet" and "Ask an agent for a website and it shows up here." Loading shows the list's grey shapes. A row new on the page moves in, its globe lifts under the pointer, a row taken offline slides away; reduced motion keeps all of it still. The page lays itself out by its own width (`@container site-rows`, in em), from a phone to a wide window with the sidebar open or compact; this replaces the card rule of the fix below. What's new's "Live now" card is the same row (`styles/sites.css`): the globe, the version and who said go, where the password is, and Open the site and Go back as pill buttons; what it says and does is unchanged. An answer that comes back late (a password read, a copy, a change, a take-offline, a read of the list, the version from What's new) acts only on the page it was asked from (review r1 of #1693, F1 and F2): it carries the moment it left, its site and the generation of what the page knew of that site's password, and acts only while the page is mounted, the site listed and the generation the same; a password reaches the screen only past a check of the member's authority and the list's `passwordChangedAt` (a password changed elsewhere leaves the screen at the next read); and a draw moves the focus only while it is on the page or on nothing and no question, confirmation or menu is open over it. So a password hidden or changed while its copy was out never comes back, and a read that lands while a question is open never pulls the focus behind it. Previews before and after: `lanes/own-code-storage/sites-redo/`.
- A member starts a desktop computer for an agent that has none (Jonas, 12 Nov 2026: "opening the computer still produces this window with the message implying I cant manually start a desktop computer, instead of just starting one"; migration `20260930730000`). The computer window's states with no computer ("{name} has no computer yet.") and with only machines without a screen ("{name} is working on a machine without a screen.") offer Start one to owners, admins and contributors; a viewer reads "Someone with edit access can start one." One press makes one computer as the agent's own machine_start with desktop true would (or answers the agent's own one, woken when it sleeps), then carries the wish through to a live picture as Wake it does: "The computer is starting…" with the lines for waiting for room or computer hours, then "The screen is turning on…". The wish is the page's, for that computer, and ends at its picture, a refusal, a close, a switch, or after 3 minutes. Nothing is started on opening the window. Refusals in plain words, with Try again where trying again can help: no server can run one now, none large enough, one started for the agent a moment ago, the agent no longer on the team, or it could not be started. Danish in `ui/danish/workspace.ts`.
- A site's card on the Sites page and the live card on What's new keep their text readable at every width (seen live 12 Nov 2026, the first real site: at a 1024 px window with the sidebar open the card's text column was 0 px wide and its words ran down the card a letter a line; What's new's live card did the same with "Open the site" and "Go back to version 1" beside it). The cards' lists are their cards' container, so the cards follow their own width, with the sidebar open or not, not the window's breakpoints. The text keeps at least 10em beside the buttons, and a card narrower than 46em (too narrow for the text beside a site's Danish buttons) puts its buttons under the text. Measured in headless Chrome: a 449 px card (1024 px with the sidebar) now shows a 377 px text column with the buttons under it; a wide card keeps them beside. The shared card of Settings and the Mailbox does not change.
- What's new: a platform page in the apps drawer where a member puts a project's website live (own code storage, step 4, "released and live": `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP4-RELEASES-PLAN.md` section 9, as corrected by `STEP4-BUILD.md` section 5; migration `20260930729000`). For each of the company's projects kept in Qorpera that makes a website, the page shows what is live ("Version 3 · since 14:10 · Ann said go", Open the site, where its password is, and Go back to the version before, asked once), the changes ready to go live as a picture with one line each and how many more are behind the scenes, and Put it live (Put the first version live before the first) and Not yet. Owners and admins see the buttons; every other member reads "Founders and admins put changes live." The page sends back exactly what it drew; when the project moved meanwhile it says "Something changed while you looked. Here is what is ready now." and reads it again, and a press that did not go through says why and that nothing changed. Not yet opens the conversation with the agent of the newest ready change (the Co-Founder when there is none, or it left) with "Not yet: " in its composer, sends nothing, and quiets the entry's badge in this browser until something new is ready. Founders never read a change number, a commit, a check, a branch or a repository path. The entry (key `whats-new`, after Sites, a new spark emblem in orange) shows while the read (`qorpera_list_code_releases`, read when the company opens and when the window gets focus) lists a project, its badge every ready change; a cloud without the read shows none, and while it shows an agent's own app named `whats-new` stays out of the drawer. It takes its place among the drawer's groups as Sites does: loose after the company's apps, or in the group `apps/groups.md` or the member's own layout names (`whats-new`, which `isAppDrawerKey` now takes), a group's badge counting its changes. The Sites page calls a project's site "Goes live from What's new", says what taking one offline does to it (its earlier versions go, and the next change makes it again with a new password), and its lead now reads "The company's websites." A coding turn whose site the checks could not hand out (`CODING_CHECKS_SITE_REFUSED`) reads as a change that did not pass its tests. A press whose answer never came claims neither outcome: "It may not have gone live: Qorpera could not be reached. Checking…", then, while reads fail too, that it is not known and to open the page again in a moment; the first read that succeeds settles it ("Version N is live.", or the line goes and the ready changes are there again), and no outcome line outlives a later read. A version Qorpera stopped trying to prepare (five failures, the read's `givenUp`) says the next change that is saved gets a new try, never that Qorpera is trying again. Danish: the page is "Nyheder" (Jonas has the last word on the name).
- An agent whose work waits on the member says so, and what for (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "I would like the agents to be better at indicating when they need the users input and it's really stopped at you. it's hard to tell right now."). The company reads `qorpera_list_member_waiting_work` (migration `20260930723000`; new `cloud/waiting-work.ts`, `CloudApi.listWaitingWork`) where it read the waiting approval cards, on the same hints (a room's new events, at most every 5 s) and at once after the member answers a card or an approval, and keeps it by agent (`waitingOnMe`; `CompanyController.waitingFor`). An agent with a card raised for the member (a question, a secret, a takeover, an email draft) or an approval card they answer is "decision" whatever run it lists, unless a run of its own works or writes. Its rail line then reads "Stopped: {what}" (Danish "Stoppet: {what}"), the card's question or the approval's action, in a warning colour (light and dark tokens, the selected row keeps its white; "Needs you" for a decision with no words takes the same colour), and a small raised hand (Lucide's hand, in the warning colour) sits on the figure's top-right of the row, the pinned card and the Co-Founder's card, also while the agent works on something else; a mention's "@" beside it moves to the top-left. The link's name says "Waiting for you: {what}". The browser tab's title counts the agents that wait on the member in the open company, "(2) Qorpera", and is the page's own title again at none and outside a company (new `app/tab-title.ts`). The home page's "{name} needs you" row says what for, with the item's time. A database before the migration answers the read as missing: the approval cards alone are read, as before, and an agent with one reads "Needs you" with the hand. Tests: `test/stopped-at-you.test.ts`.
- The computer window takes a member's input only in its large view (v234 in production, 11 Nov 2026: in the small window a press on a held picture did nothing on the computer, as the window's own drag takes every press there, while the picture said it took input; a 1600x1000 desktop cannot be driven at 400x225 anyway). Take over pressed in the small window, or on the agent's take-over card, enters the large view in the thread by itself once the hold is the page's and a picture shows (never full screen: only the member's own press on Enter asks for that). Back in the small window while the page holds the desktop, the picture is watched only (role img, no keys) and the words under it say "You are using the computer. Choose Enter to click and type." with Hand back; a press there drags the window as before, and keys there never reach the computer. Enter gives the picture the input again. Only in a thread: leaving the thread cancels an entry still pending, a take-over answered out of one enters nothing (back in the thread the member has the small window, the hold and those words), and a window that comes back to a thread entered covers it as entering does (review r1 F1 of #1685). No change to the drag code. Danish in `ui/danish/workspace.ts`.
- A coding session's change to code kept in Qorpera is checked before it goes in, its tests run when the repository has them, and its card says so in plain words (own code storage, step 3, "checked and reviewed", live in release v233: `lanes/own-code-storage/STEP3-CHECKS-PLAN.md` 11.5). While a change is checked and saved, with or without tests of its own (a repository with no checks file, or with checks switched off, goes in at once), the card says "Checking the change before saving it" instead of "Saving the change" (the coding executor's step "Checking and saving the change"; an executor from before reads as before). A turn whose change failed its tests, or whose checks file is gone or broken (`CODING_CHECKS_FAILED`, `CODING_CHECKS_REFUSED`), reads "Not saved yet: the change did not pass its tests, so the code is as it was."; a turn whose tests could not run, or ran while the code moved twice (`CODING_CHECKS_DID_NOT_RUN`, `CODING_CHECKS_MAIN_MOVED`), reads "Not saved yet: Qorpera could not test the change just now. Nothing was lost." Both in Danish too. Until now these read "Something on Qorpera's side refused one of the session's steps". The failed turn's code decides, never its words, which name the check, its command and its output for the agents. A cloud that sends none of these codes reads as before. No migration.
- Groups of apps in the drawer, and every emblem in its app's colour (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "the same way we have categories and groups for agents, we should have so for the interfaces as well … the agents should be able to organize the interfaces for you natively"; then, on the design page: groups "more like a group of apps", opening "into the flat view" with no popup and no highlighted background, a stacked-cards emblem, and "having them all be black only is the wrong idea"; migration `20260930728000`, SQL test 1221). The company's groups are `apps/groups.md`, which agents keep; a member's moves, new groups, renames and ungroups are their own (a document per member in the cloud), and Reset sidebar takes them away with the rest. A group is one tile; a click opens it into the grid (its apps grow in after it, the rest glide) and folds it again; open groups are kept per device. Holding an app over another app's middle makes a group of the two; over a group's, puts it in; an edge swaps, as before. Right-click: Move to for an app, Rename group and Ungroup for a group, both with Reset sidebar.
- Each member's own sidebar, and Reset sidebar (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "company's on creation. but when a member changes something it's specific to that member, like with any other change to the sidepanel. we should really add a reset sidepanel button too"; migration `20260930727000`, SQL test 1220). On a cloud that says what the member changed (`own`), making, renaming and deleting sections and moving agents and group chats change only the member's own sidebar, never the company's sections file, and any member may do it; the rest keeps following the company's. Reset sidebar… at the foot of the empty space's menu and a section's menu says how many changes are the member's (greyed out with none), asks with a list of what goes (the apps' order on this device too), and the rail then offers Undo. Deleting a section says whose sidebar changes. New section has a new icon: a title over two agents' rows (Jonas: "the new section buttons icon is bad - redo it"; he picked it on the design page).
- The line under an idle agent's name shows its wall's Now line, where its work stands, after "Didn't finish", "Resting until" and "Waits on you" and before a planned run or the newest done item (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: the done headlines "suck as headlines"). A wall written before the Now line still shows its newest done item. Agents write the line from the next runtime cut on (`update_wall`'s `now`).
- The browser keeps the company file bytes a member has read, so opening an app, a page or a picture again downloads nothing, also after a reload or on another day (Jonas, 18 Nov 2026: "Yes, build step 2", step 2 of the slow-loads design; told that company files then stay on the member's disk until they sign out). Measured before it: every reload read every file again through the office line (about 11 MB/s, shared by all members), since the bytes were only in the tab's memory. `cloud/kept-objects.ts` (`KeptObjects`) is an IndexedDB database of the folder's content-named objects, a file's manifest and its pieces of at most 4 MiB each, under their SHA-256. The folder client takes an object from it only for a read that read the file's entry live as the member just before (an app's files, whole or in pieces, the company's colours, the reading, a program page), so the folder still decides every time what the member may read; a file reference kept from an earlier listing (Storage's previews, a download) asks the gateway as before and keeps nothing. Every object taken is hashed again before it is used, so bytes changed on disk are asked for again. At most 1 GiB, the least recently used going first (a running total and an index by last use, so keeping one object costs the same beside 50,000 as beside none); an object over 8 MiB is not kept. Objects go in steps of 64, each a transaction of its own that starts where the last stopped, so reads go on beside an eviction. A read waits at most 4 s for the database to open and 4 s for its answer; a database that does not open in that time is left alone for the rest of the tab, and a slow answer is one miss, so reads go to the folder as before. The database is deleted on every sign-out path (`forgetMemberFiles`: the member's sign-out, a session the cloud refuses, Auth's own signed-out event, in this tab and, through the browser, in every other tab), and a database another member left is emptied before the new member's first read. It is not encrypted: it is as readable to someone with the browser's profile as the member's session is. A browser without IndexedDB (a private window in some browsers) keeps nothing and reads as before. Reading ahead now costs the line nothing for files already kept. Real Chromium checks: `scripts/test-web-kept-objects-browser.mjs` (11).
- An app's page asks the folder about its files together, in one call each time it finds more of them, instead of one call a file (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "Yes, do it", on the measured open of Fjeldtur's brand app: 448 to 966 ms the first time, about 200 ms of it 25 entry reads in three rounds of up to eight). Putting a page together (and reading it ahead) now reads the entry with one `readMany` call, the files it names with one more, and the pictures their stylesheets name with a third, each file still read as the member just before its bytes; the bytes still come a few at once, from the member's file cache when they are there. Where the database has no `qorpera_member_folder_read_many` yet (it answers 404), each path is read alone as before, for that folder from then on; a call that fails otherwise reads its paths alone that time, so each answers or fails as its own read would. No change to what a page may read.
- An agent's row in the sidebar always has a line under its name, not only while it works (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "there is nothing below it if it's not working"; he picked "the most useful thing first" over the last message). Working, writing and "Needs you" keep their lines. Otherwise the first of these that holds is shown: "Didn't finish" in red for a run that did not finish (until now only the red mark said so); "Resting until 14:00" while a run waits at its login's usage limit; "Waits on you: …" with the first item of the agent's wall under Waits on you; "Next: Market check · 9:00" for a routine due within 12 hours; the newest Done item of its wall with its day ("Made the new logo · today"); a later routine; and else "Hired Sep 2". The walls (`QorperaOS/agents/<handle>/wall.md`) and the routine list (`qorpera_list_cloud_routines`, its `next_run_at`) are read with the member's own access when the company opens, again after each change hint of the company's folder (at most every 15 s) and when the window comes back, and the routines once more after the soonest next run has passed (`app/agent-notes.ts`, `app/agent-line.ts`). Danish too. No migration, no gateway or runtime change.
- The read-ahead of an app's page takes its short videos too (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "Yes, measure it", on the read-ahead of every file of an app's page). Measured live on Fjeldtur after that went out: the brand app still took 1.6 to 2.8 s on its first open, since its page shows three clips of 110 to 180 KiB and the read-ahead left every video for the open, one of them a 960 ms object fetch. Now a video of up to 2 MiB is read ahead as a picture is, within the round's 24 MiB; a longer one still waits for the open. No migration.
- An app with pictures opens quickly the first time too (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026, on the measured result "Fjeldtur brand still took 2.2 s on its first open": "Yes, do it"). Measured on Fjeldtur after the cache went live: an app whose page is one file opened in about 180 ms, its page read ahead; the brand app, about 19 files most of them pictures, took 1.1 to 2.2 s the first time, since only its entry page was read ahead and 24 object fetches were left for the open. Now the read-ahead reads each app's page with every stylesheet, script and picture it names, as putting the page together reads them (`readPage`, shared with `assembleApp`), only to put their bytes in the member's file cache: no videos, which wait for the open, and at most 24 MiB a round (the cache keeps 64 MiB), the most recently changed apps first, two apps and four files of each at once so it stays out of the way of what the member opens. Each file's entry is still read as the member first. No migration, no gateway or runtime change.
- A member turns an agent's desktop computer on (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "manually starting an agents desktop computer still doesn't work"; migration `20260930714000`). The window's screen that is off says "The computer is on, but its screen is off." with Turn it on (a viewer reads "Someone with edit access can turn it on."); Wake it now turns the screen on too once the machine runs, with the same press. The wish is the page's, for one machine, and ends at its picture, a switch of machine, a close, a refusal or after 3 minutes. While it works: "The computer is starting…", then "The screen is turning on…"; then the window looks at once. A machine too small for a screen says so with no button; a start that failed says "The screen could not be turned on. Try again." with Try again, until pressed again; a start that answered but whose next look finds the screen off is that failure, never started again by itself, and a look that began before a slow start answered starts nothing more (review r1 F3 of #1681). A member's own hold never stops their start (review r1 F1): a member who took the desktop over while its screen was off presses Turn it on and gets the picture. While no picture shows, the words and button of the controls under the picture stand in the state's place for this page's own hold and its hand-back (review r2 F1 of #1681), with the same words, label and state: "You are using the computer…" with Hand back (beside Turn it on when the screen is off), "Giving the computer back…" with Hand back disabled, and "The computer could not be given back yet. It goes back by itself within 5 minutes." with Try again, so a hand-back that failed without a picture is said too. Danish strings in `ui/danish/workspace.ts`.
- The computer window sends a member's input in the order it was made (v233 release review r1, F31 of #1661): a click waiting for its second, typed text and a wheel turn are each gathered a moment, and when input of another kind begins, what is gathered goes first (a click and then a wheel turn no longer scrolls before it clicks).
- A member takes over an agent's desktop computer and hands it back (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "Yes, build take-over"; migration `20260930713000`, stage 1b). Owners, admins and contributors get Take over under the picture; it takes the desktop for them (`takeDesktop`) and the picture takes their clicks, double and right clicks, drags, the wheel, keys that are not text by their X names (Return, Tab, ctrl+l: shortcuts by the key's place) and typed text gathered for 150 ms into one action, all at the screen's own pixels (the picture's natural size, wherever it is drawn), and what they paste (ctrl+v or cmd+v types their own clipboard). Escape is the computer's while the picture has the keyboard; shift+Escape leaves it. Actions go one at a time (`/v1/member-machine-screens/<company>/<machine>/act` through the member bridge, `screens.act`); a desktop busy with the agent's last step says "Waiting for {name} to finish a step…" and the action waits up to 15 s. While they hold it the window says "You are using the computer. {name} gets no pictures of the screen while you use it." with Hand back, renews the hold every minute while a thread shows it, and gives it back when the window closes, the member picks another machine, or the page goes (a keepalive request with the page's last token). Another member sees "{member} is using the computer. The screen is hidden meanwhile." and no picture, until a hint or the hold's time. The agent's take-over card's Take over opens the window and takes the desktop; I'm done hands it back before it answers. A hand-back is done only once the database answered it (review r1 F4, r2, r3): until then the window says "The computer could not be given back yet. It goes back by itself within 5 minutes." with Try again, and the card's I'm done answers nothing and says so. What the member may hold is kept by the company's computer windows (`ComputerPanels`, a `DesktopHoldLedger` in `desktop-holds.ts`), not by a window, for the page's lifetime, per machine with the member's hand-back epoch (review r5): a counter only the database moves; a take-over is sent at the epoch the page knows (from the list when it knows none) and accepted only at the current one; a hand-back at it moves it on and gives back the lease taken under it, so its answer is the confirmation for everything taken or sent at that epoch or before, whatever the network did, with no clock and no list read. A release of a machine (Hand back, I'm done, a closed window, a change of the window's machine, the page hidden, leaving the company) is marked at once, before anything is awaited: the window takes no input, sends no heartbeat and ignores any take-over answer for it; then one hand-back at the known epoch is sent, asked again after 2, 4, 8 and 15 s until answered, window or not; its answer's epoch is the one a later take-over uses. A take-over that must wait for the machine's hand-back waits for its answer and is never sent once called off. One rule discharges what the page owes (reviews r6, r7, r10): a take-over sent at an epoch (from the moment it leaves the page), a hold the window took up there, and an own hold a list or a hand-back's answer showed there (on a machine this page gave back, or while I'm done works on its agent) are owed at that epoch, and only the page learning from the database that the member's epoch is above it (a hand-back's answer, a TAKE_CALLED_OFF, a list) discharges them; no answer or read at that epoch, whatever it says about holding, and no refusal, timeout, close, switch or ended heartbeat does. While something is owed, every release of the machine hands back at the epoch the page knows (a close or a switch what the page took, Hand back and I'm done also what it saw held), and Hand back and I'm done hand back again, a few rounds while reads keep showing a hold, then say no. What the window shows and uses (held, may be held) may still end early on a refusal or an ended heartbeat; that is apart from what is owed. A take-over the database overtook (another tab gave the machine back before it arrived: TAKE_CALLED_OFF with a newer epoch, or the epoch moved on while it was on its way) is said under the picture ("The computer could not be taken over. Try again.") and the list is read again; the member's next press goes out at the new epoch, and nothing is retried by itself (review r11 F18). One this page called off itself (a release) is answered to no one, and a take-over asked after that release is a new one, never joined to it (review r11). In the window each press of Take over (its own button or the agent's take-over card) is its own attempt, of the machine chosen at the press, and only the latest is the window's: an older one's answer, whatever it says, never clears a newer wish to take over, ends a newer attempt or shows or hides its note. A press joins a take-over on its way only for the same machine and while no release called it off (the ledger's `calledOff`); otherwise it starts a new one, and the button offers Take over again for the machine shown once the earlier machine's take-over was called off by a switch (review r12 F19). A take-over request leaves the page only because of a press that no release has followed (review r13 F20): every release (Hand back, I'm done, a closed window, a switch away, pagehide, leaving the company, and the hand-backs the ledger asks itself) moves a counter on, per machine, per agent and for the page; a press records where it stood, and a waiting press or a take-over whose press came before a release is dropped without a word, before anything is sent and whenever the window's loop looks at it, so nothing the window remembers from before a release can take the desktop over after it. A take-over not sent because the machine's earlier hand-back is still not answered is now said under the picture too ("The computer could not be taken over. Try again."), found by a second schedule explorer, `test/computer-explorer.test.ts`, which drives the real window (its button and the take-over card's Take over, Hand back, I'm done, close, reopen, a switch, pagehide, the page coming back, a list hint, a key, time passing) against the same model of the database, 200 seeded schedules of 30 steps, and checks I1, I3, I4 (a press no release followed comes to something the member sees), I5 (a take-over leaves only after such a press) and I6 (no heartbeat and no input after a release until such a press). A hold of the member's is never taken up from a list any more (review r14 F21): input and heartbeats on a page only follow a Take over pressed on that page that no release followed. A hold the list shows from another window, or from this page before it was loaded again, is shown as it is: the picture (it is the member's own session), "You are using this computer in another window.", Take over (a take-over at the current epoch, which takes their own lease over for this page, in a new seat) and Hand back (at the list's epoch, naming the list's seat); no input is taken and no heartbeat sent in that state. After a reload the member presses Take over once to go on; without that the old lease ends by itself. A take-over of this page's whose answer was lost is still used once a list shows it held (the press was this page's). A take-over called off while it waited for an earlier hand-back answers called off, never "not sent", so a release that called it off is never followed by "could not be taken over" (review r14 F22). Hand back on a hold shown from another window (and the card's I'm done while the window shows one) makes that hold owed from the click, at the epoch the list gave, so its hand-back goes at once and is asked again until answered; when the list read after it fails, or keeps showing a hold, the window says "The computer could not be given back yet. It goes back by itself within 5 minutes." with Try again until a Hand back or a list read confirms it, and nothing owed is dropped (review r15 F23). Each action now goes with the hand-back epoch and the seat of the hold its key, click or text was captured under (`screens.act(machine, action, {epoch, seat}, signal)`, body `{action, epoch, seat}`; release review r1 of #1667, F24 and F26), and SQL runs it only under that hold. A hold is named by its seat as well as its epoch (F26): two holds of the member's can live under one epoch (a lease that ran out and was taken again, another tab's take-over of their own lease), so every take-over carries a press id the page mints (`takeDesktop(machine, epoch, press, signal)`) and answers the seat it was given; the heartbeat names it (`keepDesktop(machine, epoch, seat, signal)`), so a stale tab's heartbeat renews nothing and that tab lets go at its next heartbeat or action; a take-over whose answer was lost is held only when a list shows the hold seated by that very press (`control.seat`, `control.press`), never another tab's; and a hand-back has two forms (`handBackDesktop(machine, reason, epoch, seat | null, signal)`; F27, F28): EXACT by a seat when every take-over the page sent for the machine at that epoch was answered (a close, a switch, pagehide and leaving the page by the seat the page's own take-over got, nothing for a hold only seen; Hand back and I'm done by the highest of that and the seat seen held), so closing a stale tab never ends another tab's hold; VOID while a take-over of the page's has no answer, which moves the epoch and so voids that take-over whenever it arrives (and ends another tab's hold under that epoch: the one race where a closing tab does). Before a new take-over while an earlier one of the page's has no answer, VOID goes first and is answered, so no two take-overs of a page are ever alive and none is forgotten. What the page owes is discharged by an epoch above it, by an EXACT answer for the seat (or a higher one), by a heartbeat or an action answered `ended` for the seat, and, for an unanswered take-over, by its own answer or a list showing the hold it seated. The queue of actions and their sending belong to one hold of the page (F25): a hold is a new object at every take-over that succeeds, with its own queue and an abort signal that its release fires (an action on its way is cut off on the page at once); its sending loop ends after every wait, touching nothing of the window, once the hold is not the window's any more, so a new hold's typing goes at once even while an older hold's action is unanswered, and whatever that older action answers (it ran, the hold ended, another member, busy, a failure) changes nothing of the new hold. The heartbeat's answer is fenced by the same hold. A look's answer is dropped when the member chose another machine while it was on its way. The window explorer now holds action requests as it holds take-overs and hand-backs, records the page hold of every key, checks the action's epoch and seat in its model of the database as SQL does, and checks I7 (an action takes effect, and a heartbeat renews, only while the model's own count of seatings is at the seat that page hold was given: review r18), I8 (an older hold's answer changes nothing of the window) and I9 (a hold's input leaves the page, in order, while the hold stands), in typing schedules of their own (100 seeds of 40 steps in the normal run). Both explorers also check I10 (once a page released a machine with no press since, everything it sent was settled and no hand-back of it is unanswered, no lease a take-over of that page's seated is live) and I11 (a page that knows the end of every take-over it sent for a machine at an epoch hands it back EXACT), and I1 now counts what was owed by seat, not epoch (review r19); the window explorer answers refusals as SQL does (before, a refusal's null fields made it look lost to the page). In Danish too. What the page knows is ordered by the database's own counter, never by the page's clock (review r8): for a machine, a list read whose epoch is below what the page knows says nothing; at that epoch or above it is used (a hold of the member's it shows is real there; an epoch above ends what the page had under the older one); a list never ends a hold at the same epoch by not showing it (the heartbeat's `ended` does). I'm done and Hand back finish only on a list read asked after every one of their hand-backs was answered that shows no hold of the member's on the agent's machines, with nothing owed; a hold it shows is handed back at its epoch and the list read again, a few rounds, then no. A completion read counts only when none of its machines is below the epoch the ledger knows, and then the ledger alone decides (review r9): I'm done answers once, after a read that counts, nothing of the agent's machines is owed; a hold that read shows at the current epoch is owed like any other (review r10). A release marks the machine given back whether or not anything was owed there, and Hand back, I'm done and a closed window give back the agent's machines the page learns of only later too: a hold a list shows there is owed and never taken up by the window, until a take-over on purpose (review r10, both found by a schedule explorer: `test/desktop-holds-explorer.test.ts` drives the ledger as the window does, two pages, another tab and another member over a model of the database's take-over rules, every request's effect and answer apart, 8,000 seeded schedules of 60 steps, and checks that I'm done leaves nothing taken, sent or seen held, that nothing is used after its release, that no take-over holds after a hand-back past it, and, since review r11, that a take-over the page did not call off is never answered with silence and none goes out below an epoch a refusal named). A take-over the member starts from another tab after that read was taken is a new take-over, outside this answer; the agent then hears `DESKTOP_MEMBER_IN_CONTROL`, as for any take-over without a card. No page can exclude what the member does a moment later. Hand back and I'm done also read the member's list and hand back the agent's machines it shows the member holding that the page has nothing of (another tab, an earlier page), and again at the epoch a hand-back answers the member holds it now, at most twice; I'm done asks the ledger (`releaseAgent`), so closing the window first no longer lets the card answer early. Whenever the window's machine changes, at the choice itself, every other machine of the agent's in the ledger is given back, and each take-over takes the machine chosen at the click. Through pagehide the ledger keeps what it gave back until a hand-back is answered, should the page come back from the browser's cache. The window follows its hold by the held machine's own id, so a switch during a list read still gives it back (F6). Two fixes to the window: the state text under a picture is hidden while the picture shows (a screen reader read its last sentence under the live picture), and the window looks every 450 ms while the picture changes or the member acts (a look takes about 0.2 s), every second after three unchanged pictures, every 2 s after six and every 3 s after thirteen. In Danish too.
- Interface apps open faster, and an app opened again opens at once (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026: "they are currently very slow and don't think they even cache either"). Measured on Fjeldtur before: 700 to 900 ms to put an app's page on screen, the same on every open, about 25 requests, each file's bytes one ~100 ms object fetch after another. Now: the member's file bytes are kept in a cache for the tab, by version (`CloudMemberFolderFileCache`, cleared at sign-out and for another member), so only a file that changed is fetched again; each read still asks the folder for the entry as the member. An app's page reads its stylesheets, scripts and pictures side by side (eight at once) instead of one after another; the company's colours are read beside the app list, not after it; and a moment after the company's apps are read, each app's entry page and the colours are read ahead, so a first open finds them too. No migration, no gateway or runtime change.
- The time on a hovered message shows at once, and only the time (Jonas, 11 Nov 2026, on the label from 10 Nov: "the hover should be instant, it's pretty slow, and should only show the timestamp, not date, be centered to the message, and have no composer around it"). It appears the moment the pointer is on a message, with no wait and no fade, and reads the time of day alone ("10:04", "2:18 PM"), never the day or the date; the thread's own time lines still give the day. It is plain small muted text, with no border, background or shadow, beside the message and centred on its height (on the bubble under the pointer when a message has several, such as its words, a table and its files; on the part in view for a long one), on the side away from the thread's middle, or on the other side when that has no room. Where neither side has room (a narrow window, the agents' panel), it stands centred just above the message, or just below at the thread's top edge, on the thread's own colour so it reads. A chip of a face alone shows the time alone too (its name was before it). `conversationClockLabel` gives the time of day as the thread's time lines write it. No migration.
- An agent's computer window shows the desktop computer the agent uses, live (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "Jonas cannot see any of it"; migration `20260930712000`, stage 1a of lanes/desktop-computers-20261110/LIVE-VIEW-PLAN.md). The window reads the agent's machines (`qorpera_list_agent_machines`) and shows the first running desktop it drove last as a picture that refreshes about once a second, with a Live mark; after 3 unchanged pictures in a row it looks every 2 s, after 10 every 3 s, and a changed picture, a click in the window or a `machines` hint brings it back to 1 s. Pictures come through the member bridge (`/v1/member-machine-screens/`, `cloud/machine-screens.ts`) with the member's own token and the etag of the picture they have, so an unchanged screen costs a 304. It looks only while a thread shows it and its tab is visible; after 30 minutes without a touch on the page it pauses ("Paused to save power. Still watching?" Keep watching). Without a picture it says, in plain words and in Danish too: the agent is not using a computer, works on a machine without a screen, the computer is asleep (Wake it, for owners, admins and contributors; a viewer is told who can), it is starting (with the room and hours lines), its screen is off, or it is hidden because it may show files the member cannot open; a held picture stays up marked Updating…. A switcher in the window's corner (the app's menu) picks among the agent's desktops when it has more than one; in full screen its menu opens inside the full-screen frame (`openContextMenu` takes a `container`), a switch keeps full screen and the last picture, marked, until the next machine's comes, and the first look at each machine chosen says again that the member watches. The office paths (offices, catalog, starts, office screens) are gone from the window; the old viewer's modules stay for the stream that comes later. No take-over yet. The `machines` hint joins the company's live hints.
- Agents, group chats and member contacts in the rail make way while one is dragged, as the apps drawer's icons do (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "when dragging agents, they don't reorder the way I want. instead it highlights a line of where it would move to if you let go. I prefer the style used when reordering the interfaces with immediate orderswitching"). The dragged row lifts off as an image under the pointer, and an empty space of its shape stands where it would land: over another row, the rows glide apart at once to open the space there, and the section it left closes up. Letting go drops it into the space (the image glides into it), so the drop itself moves nothing. The shaded section and the thin line are gone. In the pinned row the space is a card's size. An empty pinned row ("Drop here to pin") no longer opens the moment a row is picked up, which pushed the list down under the pointer; it opens when the pointer comes up to the top of the list, and closes again past it. Dragged off the rail, the space goes back to where the row started, and letting go there changes nothing; Escape puts everything back. While a row is lifted the rail is not drawn afresh; what changed meanwhile is drawn when it is let go. Pinning, unpinning, moving between sections, dragging a contact back to Members and showing a hidden agent again by dragging work as before. No migration.
- An agent's figure picker stays inside the window (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "the agent detail popup windows can hide into the browsers bottom if it's low enough on the page"). The picker hangs below the card, and a card opened from an agent low in the rail kept only itself inside the window, so the picker's lower figures and colours went under the bottom edge. Now the card moves up while the picker shows, leaving the picker's bottom 12 px above the window's, and goes back level with its row when the picker is put away. A press in the card holds it still until it is let go (a press outside the picker puts the picker away), so a button pressed there, such as Close, is still under the pointer to take the click. `SurfaceDialog` takes such a hanging part with `overhang()`. No migration.
- An agent's app has no header (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026, on the slim bar over an app's page: "I would remove any headers as they add nothing but bad visual design"). The app is the whole page: the back arrow, the emblem and name, "Made by … · Qorpera never asks for passwords here", Reload and Show in Storage are gone from it. The rail shows which app is open and leads everywhere else, and a press on the open app's icon there reads it afresh (a modified click still opens a new tab). When an app can't be shown, the line that says why has buttons under it: the way back when the app is gone, Reload, and Show in Storage. A web link the browser stops from opening is offered in a line at the top of the page. The page beside a chat keeps its bar with Close, and the Concepts viewer its side panel, both still with the maker and the password line; an app's own page no longer says either (RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md §6, H2).
- Hovering a message shows when it was sent (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "Whenever I hover any message in a chatthread (for group chats, single agents or member contacts or agent-agent messages) I would like a hover showing the time the message was sent"). A small label in the menus' style appears beside the message after the pointer rests on it for a moment ("Today 10:04", "Yesterday 22:10", "Mon, Nov 3 14:02", in the chosen language and time zone); moving on to the next message shows its time at once. It stands on the side away from the thread's middle (right of an agent's message, left of your own), at the pointer's height; where there is no room beside (a narrow window, the agents' panel), it sits just above the pointer. It works in an agent's chat, a group chat, a chat with another member (sent messages and ones on their way), the transcript of two agents talking, and on the chips in a thread that link to such a talk (a chip of a face alone also names the agent). It replaces the browser's own tooltip on those messages, which took a second or more to appear; a screen reader still hears the time from the message's label. It hides on a press, a scroll, a key or when the pointer leaves the message, and it never shows for touch or while text is being selected. New: `ui/sent-time-hover.ts`. No migration.
- Member contacts stand at the foot of the rail and behave like agents (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026, the day member chats went live: "not sure I like the member contacts at the top, undraggable and un-hideable"; migration `20260930717000`, SQL test 1207). The other members no longer stand in a block under the Co-Founder. Each contact is a row like an agent's, in a Members group after everything the company arranges, and from there you drag it where you want it: into the pinned row (it becomes a card with the face and first name), into any section, or among what is in no section, at any place between the agents. Alt with an arrow moves the focused contact one place. A right-click (a long press on a phone) offers Pin or Unpin and Hide from sidebar; a hidden contact folds into Hidden at the foot of the list with the hidden agents, where Show in sidebar or a drag brings it back to where it stood (a pinned one loses its pin when hidden, as an agent does, and comes back to the Members group). The pinned row holds 24 cards, agents and contacts together. A hidden contact can still write to you: the count of their waiting messages stands on the Hidden fold. Where a contact stands is your own, as your pins are: nobody else's rail changes, the company's sections stay the agents', and a viewer can arrange their contacts too. A contact stands after the agent you dropped it behind and stays there when the company reorders its agents; if that agent leaves the section, the contact goes to the section's end, and if the section is deleted, back to the Members group. A bigger team is rows too (the cards for more than three members are gone: at the foot of the list they push nothing down). On a database without the migration the contacts stand in the Members group and cannot be moved or hidden.
- A member sends an email themself from the Mailbox, with no agent (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "Let a member send an email with no agent", and on the live window: "ehm, can I actually send any mails though? and is it selecting/locking any recievers when i type the mail of the person I want to email"). With "You" as the writer the Write window is a mail window: To, Subject and the text, and the button reads "Send" (was "Choose an agent", since mail left only through an agent). Send calls `qorpera_send_member_mail` (runtime changelog; migration `20260930716000`); the email leaves from the company's address through the sender an agent's email uses, and it shows at once in the list and under Sent as the member's own: their picture where an agent's figure stands ("You sent it", or "{name} sent it" for another member), and "You" with that picture in the open thread. Every member sees it; no mail is private to a member. Each address typed in To becomes a chip, as in Gmail: at the space after an address, and at a comma, a semicolon, Enter or when the field is left for anything typed; a name's words stay one chip ("Nordlys Energi"); a chip that is no email address is marked and says so, and Send names it instead of sending. Backspace in the empty field takes the last chip away, each chip has its own remove button, and a pasted list ("Anna Holm <anna@acme.dk>, bo@acme.dk") becomes a chip for each of its entries: the name beside an address is left out, and an entry that is no address is a marked chip, never dropped. An entry with no address in it is kept whole, as it stands, with what tells it from another ("Anna (Acme)" and "Anna (Beta)" are two chips, and so are "Anna <Acme>" and "Bo <Acme>"), typed or pasted. An entry that points at its address goes to that address alone, pasted or typed, also when the name beside it is an address itself ("old@acme.dk <anna@acme.dk>" goes to anna@acme.dk only). A quoted name and a remark in round brackets are read whole: a comma in one ends no entry, and an address in one is never a recipient ('"Former <old@acme.dk>" <anna@acme.dk>' and "Anna <anna@acme.dk> (formerly, old@acme.dk)" go to anna@acme.dk only), also when the quoted text is all of its entry ('"old@acme.dk"' is a marked chip). An entry that points at two addresses, at what is no address ("Carl <carl@gmaill>"), or has one more after the one it points at, does not say who it goes to: it is one marked chip, as it stands. Nothing is sent before the email has who it goes to (at most 10), a subject and its text; what is missing is said in the window, with the keyboard on that field. A send the cloud refuses (sending switched off, a paused mailbox, one of the company's own mailboxes as recipient, a limit) says why in the window and keeps everything typed; Send again sends the same email, never a second one, until it is changed in any part (who it goes to, where from, its subject or its text), also when an agent had it meanwhile. While an email is being sent, or its words are on their way to an agent, nothing in the window can be changed, only the window shut: what leaves the window when it has gone is what was sent. A window shut meanwhile comes back with the email and the reason when it could not go. When an earlier try of the same email reached the cloud after all and then failed or was cancelled, the window says so and keeps the email, and the next Send is a new one. When the company has several addresses in use, a From chip picks which one it goes from. Giving the email to an agent from the Writer chip ("Ask {name} to write it") leaves To as it stands: the row is the same for both writers, with the same chips and what is typed after them, and no chip is ever read again, so one that is no address is still one when the member takes the email back. With an agent as the writer a chip may be a name ("Name or email address"), so none is marked there, and the agent is told the chips and what is typed after them as who it goes to. A list pasted there is read as if it were typed, so no name in it is left out: the name beside an address is a chip of its own ("Nordlys Energi bo@acme.dk" is both), and so is one typed before what is pasted. Enter in To ends an address, Enter in Subject goes on to the text, and Cmd or Ctrl with Enter sends. Not in this change: Cc, attachments, and a member's own reply inside a thread (a reply still goes through an agent). Danish for every new phrase.
- An agent's card is a tenth wider and about a tenth taller, and it draws the agent's responsibilities as the Markdown they are written in (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "I would like the agent info windows to be 10% wider and 10% taller, adding 1 or 2 more lines to the descriptions max height", and "the description isn't rendering the markdown designs they are written in. I would like to fix that too, so it becomes more readable"). The card is 374 px wide (was 340), and the description shows up to seven lines before it scrolls (was five), which makes a card with a long description 373 px tall (was 335). Headings, bold, lists, code and quotes are drawn as in the chat, at the card's size, with headings and bold in the full text colour; a link reads as its text. A click on the drawn text opens the text itself for editing, as before, with the caret where the click fell, and leaving the field draws it again. Tab reaches the field as before. A member who can't edit keeps the drawn text, and so does a drag over it, so the text can be selected and copied. The group chat's card shares the frame: it is as wide, and its description as tall. No migration.
- A mouse wheel glides through a thread instead of jumping (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "scrolling through a thread in safari, it feels very laggy as it just flicks up, 0 smoothing. I would like us to add it ourselves"). Safari moves a scroller by a whole wheel step at once; now each click eases over about a quarter of a second, the same distance as before, and fast clicks add up into one glide. It applies to every scroller without a bar: the thread, a coding session's log, a pair's transcript and the rail. A trackpad or a Magic Mouse keeps the browser's own scrolling (it already glides), as do Firefox's line-based wheel (Firefox eases it itself, by a distance it measures from the font), pinch zoom, sideways scrolls, a code block or card that scrolls itself, and reduced motion. The glide keeps to the content when the thread moves by itself (a new message pinned at the bottom, earlier messages put in above), and a key, a touch or a trackpad ends it where it is.
- Links in an agent's interface app open, and every app starts from one base style (the review of 10 Nov 2026: seven agent-made apps held 68 links, all dead, and six of the seven were one generic green report, each agent having made up a look). A link the member clicks in an app's page is stopped in the frame and handed to the app over the port the page's data comes on; the app opens what it allows: `app:<name>` opens that app, `folder:<path>` opens the file in Storage, and a web link opens a new tab, as a link in a message does. Anything else opens nothing, and the frame itself never leaves its page. Only a click the member made counts; each is checked on the app's side with the path rules of a read and spends one call of the app's budget. When the browser stops the new tab, Qorpera offers the link in a line under the app's header. Every app's page now starts from a small stylesheet that the page's own CSS overrides rule by rule (it sits in a cascade layer): the system font, a centred column up to 800 px wide, plain headings, links, tables and code, and colour variables (`--q-bg`, `--q-text`, `--q-accent` and more). The variables carry the company's colours when `QorperaOS/brand/brand.toml` gives them (`accent`, `background`, `text`, and again under `[dark]`), and only where text stays readable. A page follows the app's light or dark when it says it supports both (`<meta name="color-scheme" content="light dark">`); otherwise it is shown light. A concept and a Company File page are shown as written. `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md` §2, §4, §5 and §6 have the rules; `scripts/test-web-app-frame-browser.mjs` proves the clicks in Chromium.
- Settings no longer has Company connections (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "the goal is fully qorpera hosted companies. we will only bring it back if it's actually needed"). The tab with Gmail, Google Drive and GitHub and their Connect buttons (and "Use a personal access token instead") is gone, with its view (`views/settings-services.ts`) and its Danish texts; Settings shows General, Company, Leaderboard and AI subscriptions. Searching Settings for "gmail" or "github" finds nothing. A provider sign-in coming back to the app no longer opens Settings; the Bank page's sign-in is unchanged, and its client (`cloud/connectors.ts`) keeps its tests, now in `test/connectors.test.ts`. Connections a company already made stay in the vault and keep working for its agents; nothing in the cloud changed. No migration.
- A member can act on the company from an agent's app, with their yes each time (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "agents lets you interact with your employees — interfaces let's you interact with your company directly"; he picked the first two actions the same day; no migration). Until now an app could only be looked at. An app's page can now ask for two things, and only what its `app.toml` lists in `writes`: an answer to an agent's open question that points at the app (`qorpera.answer(id, value)`, one of the card's own options), and a change to one row of a company table or a new row (`qorpera.setRow(table, key, cells)`, `qorpera.addRow(table, cells)`). The page only asks. Qorpera puts each request to the member on a bar above the app's frame, which it pushes down, so the page can neither draw the bar nor press it: "This app wants to send your answer to Bob" with the question and the answer in the card's own words, or "This app wants to change a row in a company table" with the table, the row and each value as it is and as it will be.
