"Not now" has the focus; the yes button ("Send answer", "Change row", "Add row") wakes a second after the bar is on screen and takes only a press that began after that. One bar at a time; after three "Not now" in a row the app asks no more until the member reloads it; the page's Escape is held while a bar is open. The bar then says "Sent to Bob", "Saved", or why not. An answer goes the way a tap on the card does (`qorpera_answer_interaction`, the member's own model account for that thread) and leaves the questions dock at once. A row is saved as the member's own change through the member commit route, over the version that was read, and only if the row still says what the bar showed; the folder core checks the table's write rule and the rows as for any save. A page never changes a key, a row's holder or any cell that names a member or an agent, never removes a row, and never touches the table `areas`. A page can also read the open questions that point at it (`qorpera.questions()`, `questions` in `reads`). New: `app/app-actions.ts`, `app/views/app-ask-bar.ts`; the bridge, the frame document and the app panel take the three calls. Danish: "Appen vil sende dit svar til Bob", "Ikke nu", "Send svar", "Ændr række".
- A new email in the Mailbox starts with no agent, and its window is bigger (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "It should have no agent owning it by default when the user clicks write manually - that indicates he is writing it until an agent is assigned", and "click the writer notch and change who writes the mail"). The Writer row is one chip: the member's own picture and "You" until they give the email to an agent. A click on the chip opens a menu of who writes it, "You" and every agent at work with the Co-Founder first (before, only the agents answering an address and the Co-Founder), each row with its picture and the current one with a check; a second click on the chip shuts it; the picked agent's figure and name take the chip, and the foot says which address it goes from. Mail still leaves the company only through an agent, so the button reads "Choose an agent" until one has it, and pressing it opens the same menu; after the pick the keyboard is on "Ask {name} to write it". An email that was started keeps its writer when the window is shut and opened again; an empty one is nobody's. The window is 560 px wide (was 500) with a taller text. Addresses is drawn in the page's own lines instead of grey boxes (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "only thing I would polish is the boxes showing your mailboxes and when you want to add a new one"): each mailbox and Digital Post is a row on a hairline, as a thread is, with quiet pill buttons (Pause, Remove) at its right; "Add mailbox" is an outlined pill; its form asks for the mailbox's name alone (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "no need for the description of why the mailbox is added"), in a soft rounded field that lifts on focus, as the search bar does, with a filled Add. The kernel still wants a purpose for a mailbox, so the page sends the name as it, and a mailbox's row shows a purpose only when it says more than the name (one an agent wrote). A webhook's form, under an agent's Routines, still asks what it is for. In the shared menu (`ui/context-menu.ts`) a checked row that starts with a picture carries its check at the row's end. No migration.
- Twelve agent figures and a clearer colour row (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "add some more avatars for the agents, and polish the colors a bit. some are too similar"; then "I really liked the puzzle", "keep the moon, but remove the ghost", "12"). Star, Cloud, Bolt, Mushroom, Moon and Puzzle join Planet, Acorn, Flame, Rocket, Crown and Heart; the Ghost is no longer offered, and an agent saved as a ghost is drawn as the Moon (the legal expert's figure is the Moon too). The six new figures are drawn in the same soft clay as the others, with the edge softness of the approved art (`status-2d-v1/README.md`). In the colour row, Red, Sky and Slate take the places of Coral, Teal and Gray (Jonas on Teal: "should be replaced with something else entirely"), and Green, Purple and Pink moved apart, so no two colours read alike on a figure; a light gray was tried and dropped, because on the dark theme it looks like Charcoal. An `agent.md` keeps its colour names (`coral`, `teal` and `gray` are now painted as Red, Sky and Slate), so nothing saved changes. The first-run picker shows the figures in two rows of six, the agent picker in three rows of four. A company without a logo that wore the ghost wears the moon. Needs migration `20260930709000` and the gateway and runtime that read the new figure names before it.
- The Members page is pictures only (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "redo the design for the members interface so it's easier to use and understand", then "dont want any textbased sections, pure visual", "you can get rid of the containers too" and, of the bar with the page's title, "get rid of the pages header though, it's pointless"). The header bar, the share bar, the tinted boxes, the headings, the suggestion card and the lines of text are gone. Each member has one line: their face, their name and a small ChatGPT meter (the mark, a bar and the per cent used; amber with the time it has room again when it is full; a broken line when there is none or it needs a new sign-in), then the figures of the agents that work for them, each with its name. An agent moves by a drag onto another member's line, or by a tap and a choice from its menu ("Move to Sofie" beside Sofie's face, with "Sofie says yes first." under it); a second tap closes the menu. While the other side has not answered, the agent stands twice: where it works, with the face of the member it would go to on its figure, and faded in that member's line. The member who answers has a yes and a no under the one in their own line, and the one who asked can take it back from the agent's menu. The agents of a member whose ChatGPT is full or needs a new sign-in are grey: their work waits. The Co-Founder stands in a line of its own, "Everyone", by the members' faces; agents that work for nobody stand in a last line, "Nobody". With more than three members the figures are smaller. On your own line a missing ChatGPT is a "Connect ChatGPT" button and one that needs a new sign-in a "Sign in again" button; both open Settings at the subscriptions. A tooltip says what each picture means, and each line says its count to a screen reader ("2 agents work for you"). The page has no header, so it no longer carries the live-updates note ("connecting"), as the Mailbox and the Bank page don't; the rail still says when live updates are degraded. For this, a menu row can carry a face in the icon's place and a second, quieter line, a menu can hold a line of plain text, a menu tells its opener which press closed it, and a board's drag works on a page that is not a board. In Danish: "Flyt til Sofie", "Sofie siger ja først.", "Træk tilbage", "Log ind igen". No migration.
- The pill above an agent's composer no longer shows what the agent is working on (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "the notch still shows "working" despite the agent not working... get rid of it too. it's a bad notch"). It no longer says "3 working" with a spinner, and open, it has no Working on list. The pill is there only while something waits on you ("1 waits on you", each with its Answer); with nothing waiting, there is no pill. The agent's wall.md keeps its Working on part; the app just doesn't show it.
- Members of a company can message each other (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "for multiplayer companies, I should also have a contact for the members I work with that's a direct way to message and communicate with my friends im building with"; migration `20260930715000`, SQL test 1205). In a company with more than one member, the rail lists the other members under the Co-Founder, each a contact with their face, a green dot while they are online and, under the name, whether they are online or when they last were. A contact opens your chat with that member: bubbles like an agent's chat, the times between them, a field and a send button, and nothing an agent's chat has that a person does not need (no model, no login, no run). A message shows the moment it is sent; "Seen" stands under your newest one once the other member has looked; a web address is a link; one that could not be sent stays with "Try again" and "Delete"; a right-click on a message of your own takes it back for both of you. Messages that wait show as a count on the contact, with the newest one under the name, and arrive live. With more than three other members the contacts are cards with faces and first names, so the agents stay in sight. A chat belongs to its company (the same two people have another in another company), wakes no agent and uses no login or computer hours; a viewer can write too. On a database without the migration the rail shows no members, as before.
- The Mailbox is laid out like Gmail (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "redo it so it feels more like just using Gmail inside of qorpera. the UX should feel natural and clean"). Write and the folders (Inbox, Sent, Addresses) sit at the side, with the count of sends waiting beside Inbox; the search field is a rounded bar across the top; each thread is one line (who, subject, the start of the text, the handling agent, the time) that lifts under the pointer, with a dot and bold for unread. A send waiting for an answer shows who it goes to and its Send / Don't send on a second line. An opened thread takes the list's place with a back arrow, messages with round pictures, earlier messages folded to one line, a waiting send as a reply box with Send on it, and the line to the handling agent as a rounded field at the foot. Write opens a new-message window docked at the foot (on a short page, such as a phone with its keyboard up, its text gives way first and then the window scrolls), and what just happened is said in a small dark bar at the foot left. The page lays itself out by its own width (a container query, in em): a drawer when wide, a rail of icons when narrower, lines stacked as a phone draws them when narrower still, and a phone's mail app when narrow (folders as chips, Write floating over the corner). Redrawing the page already shown (a letter typed in search, an answer on its way) no longer sets every line in motion again (`settle` in `views/mailbox.ts`). A redraw also leaves whatever scrolls where the member left it: the list, the Write window, its text, and the caret in the text (a read of the mail every 20 s once sent each back to its top, and the window's button out of view). That was so on every interface page in a real browser: `show` in `views/interface.ts` read the old page's scroll after taking it off the screen, where a browser says 0; it now reads every `data-scroll-key` scroller first, puts each back by its key, and keeps a text area's caret as it kept an input's. Typing in search starts the list from its top, and a picked writer keeps the keyboard focus. What the Mailbox does is unchanged: agents write the mail, and members answer with Send / Don't send or a line to the agent. No migration.
- The pill above an agent's composer no longer shows what the agent has done (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "showing this notch of what's been done is pointless"). It no longer says "6 done" or "1 just done", and open, it has no Done list. It shows what waits on you and what is being worked on; with neither, there is no pill. The agent's wall.md keeps its Done part; the app just doesn't show it.
- A coding session on code the company keeps in Qorpera no longer speaks of a pull request (the audit of 10 Nov 2026: founders see outcomes only, and such code has no pull request). While its finished work goes in, the card says "Saving the change" instead of "Opening the pull request", and its top line shows the project's name without the session's branch. A session on a GitHub repository reads as before.
- Agents show a name and their responsibilities, and no title (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "get rid of the subtitles for the agents. a name and responsibility is enough"), and their card no longer has "Latest notes" (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "there will be too much happening for you to care"). The card shows the figure, the name, the responsibilities, then Computer and Routines. The title is gone from the rail too: an idle agent's row shows only its name, and a working one still says what it is doing. The group chat details call an agent "Agent", the Members page shows the name only, a hire proposal reads "Hire “Sales”" without "as …", and the Create agent form has no Title box (a new agent's agent.md takes its name as its role, which still must be filled in). Searching the rail matches names only. The wall above an agent's composer stays as it was; only the card's way into it and its "n new" count went. The agent's role stays in its agent.md, unchanged.
- A question whose options all show the same app links that app once under its choices, not once per option (seen live 10 Nov 2026: a Co-Founder's two headlines in one concept listed `saml-concepts` twice).
- The chat composer looks more like Messages, and takes speech (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "the + on the side, the thinner composer and add voice to text/microphone input"). The menu button is a round "+" beside the field instead of "..." inside it, the field is a thinner capsule (36 px instead of 44 px), and a microphone beside the send button turns speech into text with the browser's own speech recognition (Safari and Chrome; hidden where the browser has none, such as Firefox). The words appear at the caret while they are spoken; typing, sending or a second click ends the listening. It listens in the language chosen for Qorpera, else the browser's. The new-conversation page has the microphone too.
- The Bank page's setup steps find the company's finance owner from `finance/bank-setup.json` (asked by the runtime session, 10 Nov 2026). Agents hired through the company folder carry no template, so "Hire a finance agent" could never complete: on production, 28 hired agents have none. Only the finance owner keeps the file, so a file at all now completes step 1, alongside an agent from the `financial-officer` template. The file's new optional `agent` field names the owner as the company shows it. The page matches it to the team by name, in any case, for the figure, "{agent} is on it" and the thread buttons. With no agent of that name, it shows the name and a coloured acorn, with no thread to open.
- Founders answer an agent's questions right inside the app the question is about (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: agents engage the founder in their work's decisions where the work is, and it should be fun; migration `20260930706000`, SQL test 1195). An app's page and the Concepts viewer show a dock over their corner, drawn by Qorpera outside the app's frame: the agent's figure waiting, "Bob asks you", and the question card as the chat draws it, with a link to the chat. A question points at an app when one of its options previews `app:<name>` or a picture inside `apps/<name>/`, or its text links `app:<name>`; the Concepts viewer shows those about any of its concepts, so a choice between two directions shows once. One tap answers it the way the chat does (`qorpera_answer_interaction`, on the member's own model account for that thread, with the same checks): the chosen box pops, the figure cheers, "Sent to Bob", and after a moment the card flies off and the next comes up ("1 of 2"). A refused answer opens the card again and says why. The dock folds to a pill with the count, kept per browser, and a new question nudges it. The list comes from the new `qorpera_list_member_open_questions`: the open question cards raised for the member in the agent threads they are in, not answered and not past their time, at most 20 a thread and 50 in all, through two new small partial indexes; viewers get none. Apps still only read: nothing in an app can answer or send. Danish: "Bob spørger dig", "Sendt til Bob".
- Styles the app gives an element as `style` now take effect on app.qorpera.com (found checking the new Bank setup screen live, 10 Nov 2026: its progress bar showed full on step 1). The site's Content-Security-Policy (`style-src 'self'`) drops a style attribute set from script, so `h()` now sets `style` through the CSSOM (`element.style.cssText`), which the policy allows, as `_headers` already assumed. This fixes the Bank setup screen's progress bar and its staggered steps, the Bank page's chart bars, axis labels and category swatches, and the Members page's seat colours and rings, none of which drew as intended on the live site.
- The Bank page, before a bank is connected, is about getting the company one from inside Qorpera (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "assume that 95% of bank accounts and CVR registrations will be set up from within qorpera"). Instead of "No bank yet" and one button, it shows "Let's get the company a bank account" with four steps the founder can watch move: Hire a finance agent (the Co-Founder hires one; done once an agent from the financial-officer template is on the team), Register the company (CVR on virk.dk), Open a business account, and Connect it here. The finance agent does the paperwork and the founder only signs with MitID. Each step's button opens the right thread with a message waiting in its composer ("Ask the Co-Founder to hire one", "Ask {agent} to start", "Open {agent}'s thread"); nothing is sent until the founder sends it. Steps two and three follow `finance/bank-setup.json` (`qorpera.bank-setup/v1`), which the finance agent keeps: each part's status (todo, working, your_turn, done), the CVR number and the bank, and a "Your turn" line for what the founder must sign. A file that isn't that schema reads as none. The last step is Connect the bank. While no bank is connected, an owner or admin who already has an account connects it from a small "Already have a bank account?" in the page's top right corner. A connected bank's page is unchanged. No migration.
- Threads have a slim scrollbar again, drawn like Claude Code's (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "we should add a stylish and clean scroll bar for the threads", "it should look just like the scroll bar in claude code"). It is a thin grey pill with no track, inset from the window's edge, that runs down past the composer and darkens under the pointer; dark mode draws it light. Conversations and the pair transcript show it; other quiet scrollers (the rail, coding logs) still hide theirs.
- The leaderboard and Friends are calmer (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026). A person's own picture as champion of the week stays still (a company's figure still cheers); your own row in the lead just says "You", not "You · You're in the lead."; and when a friend was last online reads in minutes and then hours up to a day, then days, then weeks, and "Last online 2+ weeks ago" for anything older (no more "yesterday", which could mean a few hours ago, and no dates). Danish "Sidst online for over 2 uger siden".
- The new-company page has a "Company name" field over the idea box (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: a company started alone never sees the table, so it could not be named). It is optional: left empty, the company starts with no name yet (the newest entry). Enter in the field goes on to the idea. The typed name is used by the arrow and by Invite friends, and both fields empty once the company opens. No migration.
- The rail shows where the other members are in the company now (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "a visual in the sidepanel that indicates what agent/interface the user is inside of, like a little notch"). A member who is online and in the company shows as a small picture on the agent, group chat, page or app they are in: at a row's right edge, against a card's figure (the Co-Founder, a pinned agent), on an app's tile, and on the apps button while the drawer is shut; three pictures and "+n", ringed in the colour under them, with "Sofie Lund is here" in the row's description. Each tab now tracks its conversation and, after "@", its place in the rail (`agent:`, `room:`, `page:`, `app:`, `concepts`, `sites`; app/presence-place.ts), since each member has a conversation of their own with an agent; the conversation's header still finds who has it open, and an older tab's bare conversation id still reads. Nothing is stored and no SQL changed: it rides the company's existing presence channel.
- Working figures stop decoding their recorded frames once they have played them (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: keep going on smoothness). A recorded frame's eyes and coloured strokes are worked out from the recording pixel by pixel, the largest remaining part of a moving figure's cost; the result never changes and the clips repeat (Working is one 10.8 s loop). Each prepared frame is now kept, cropped to its eyes and strokes (about 10 MB for every frame of every clip, 32 MB at most), so after a first pass a figure only draws. Every recorded frame draws the same pixels as before: identical in Chromium, within 2/255 as composited in WebKit. On node02, Chromium's main thread on a still conversation with three working figures went from 13 % to 7 % busy after a loop; headless WebKit from 44 to 56 frames per second still and from 34 to 45 while scrolling.
- Figures come alive about a second after the app opens in Safari, instead of 2.4-3.6 s (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "yes, do that"). Each figure waits for its artwork's light field, and Safari's JavaScript engine ran that function 30-40 times slower than Chromium's: 1.5-1.7 s per 512px plate in Safari 18.6 on a Mac, on the page or in a worker. `light-field.js` now does the same arithmetic in the same order (the field is bit for bit the same; a test keeps the first version as its reference) in a shape Safari runs in 54 ms per plate; it is also faster in Chromium (144 to 105 ms) and WebKit on Linux (95 to 77 ms). Measured in real Safari on the offline harness: first moving figure 0.58-1.2 s, every light field done by 0.86-1.4 s.
- The company's logo and name at the top of Settings → Company sit better together (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: the logo smaller, the name bigger and bottom aligned). The logo is 52 px with an 18 px gap, as the member pictures below it (was 88; 46 on a phone, was 64), with a smaller camera badge that hangs further off its corner. The name is 28 px (was 20; 24 on a phone), stands on the logo's bottom edge instead of being centred beside it, and so starts in line with the members' names.
- Settings → Company no longer shows a "Brand" title over the company's logo and name (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "remove the 'brand' title"); the logo and the name stand at the top of the tab on their own. Screen readers still hear it as Brand.
- Work stopped at a login's usage limit now really goes on by itself at the reset (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: make "Work will go on by itself when it resets" true; migration `20260930703000`). An agent's run in Agent notebooks mode used to end there and wait for review, though the notice said it would go on. Now it waits on the same login and goes on from where it stopped when the limit resets. The agent's thread says "{agent} is waiting to go on", with the reset time. The member whose login pays sees a "Continue with <email>" button for each of their other logins that can pay now. One click moves that same run to the chosen login, and it goes on there at once, so it never also comes back on the old login. The button is offered only where the move can be made (not for a routine's run, never onto a ChatGPT plan). While it waits, the agent shows no work, and the empty send button still stops the run. Nothing moves by itself. Other members read who can continue it. A run that still cannot wait (for example a coding session's turn) says that its work stopped and does not go on by itself, in its card, its agent's words and the notice.
- Settings → Company opens with the company's Brand: its name and logo (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "under the company tab there should be a company name and logo that can be changed and assigned the same way you can change profile picture"; migration `20260930696000`, SQL test 1181). An owner or admin chooses a logo the way they choose their own picture, fitted to a rounded square, or removes it. The name is shown, not changed: renaming is deferred (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026), since a new name also has to reach the business registry, mail and the rest. The official logo is the company folder's `QorperaOS/brand/logo.svg` (a chosen picture is wrapped in an SVG); colours, fonts and the brand guide live beside it in `QorperaOS/brand`, and the section shows only the name and logo (no note or files button). A company's logo now shows wherever the company does (the hub, invitations, Friends and the leaderboard), over its figure, which stays for a company without one. Others see it through a PNG drawn from the SVG in a private bucket: members, people invited to the company and friends of its members. Any member who may write the folder keeps that PNG in step with the SVG when they open the company, and the cloud takes a PNG only if it was drawn from the folder's current logo.svg.
- Work that stopped at a login's usage limit can go on now with another of your own logins (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: Engineering's run stopped on one ChatGPT login while another had 21% left, and its card offered only Dismiss). The card in the agent's thread offers the member whose login paid a "Continue with <email>" button for each of their other logins that can pay now. One click makes that login the thread's, by their own pick as the Login menu makes it, and sends the agent a short message to go on, which runs on that login. A message they have already started stays for them to send. Nothing moves by itself, and other members read who can continue it. The thread's own offer ("Or use another of your logins now") never showed here, since it only follows the thread's own login, and the thread already paid with the login that had room.
- The app draws sooner after it opens (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "the load times are pretty long too - especially on safari"). Before the rail could draw, the page prepared every figure's artwork at once: a grain-free light field for each body and face plate, about 30 million weighted samples per 512px plate, which held the main thread for about half a second (410 of 835 ms in a Chromium profile of the offline harness). The field now runs in workers (`assets/avatars/light-field.js`, the same file as page script and worker, so a field is identical wherever it is computed; on the page itself when no worker can run), and figures show their still artwork until theirs is ready. On node02 the rail drew at 0.30 s instead of 0.78 s in headless Chromium and 0.82 s instead of 1.51 s in headless WebKit; figures look byte for byte the same.
- Moving figures cost the browser a fraction of what they did, the second step of making the app smooth in Safari (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "we should make a great effort out of smoothing out the app as much as possible"). Recorded eyes are now drawn, halved and cut on canvases the size of the box they cover (about a tenth of the 512px layer); each figure keeps its own boxes, because WebKit can run a draw from part of a canvas after later calls have changed it. The recorded clip keeps its last six decoded frames instead of one, so views of one agent decode its frame once, and a frame's decode copies and reads back only the eyes' and strokes' boxes of the recording. Every recorded frame (Idle, Waiting, Working: 1,151) draws the same pixels as before in Chromium and WebKit; figures on screen differ by at most 8/255 at edges. Chromium's main thread on a still conversation with three working figures went from about 43 % to 11-15 % busy (65 % before #1588); headless WebKit from 32-35 to 39-48 frames per second (57-62 with the figures held still).
- Settings → Company no longer has the Memory section (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: Shared context never went live or was finished, so the choice goes). Every company keeps Agent notebooks, the intended memory: each agent writes what it learns to its own notebook in the company's folder. Checked on the live database first: no company had chosen Shared context, none was offered it, and all 544 runs so far used notebooks. The web no longer reads or sets `qorpera_company_memory_mode`; the RPCs and the runtime's gate stay. No migration.
- A company you start alone never shows the founding table (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: the page without friends "should just use the clean setup page", with no Ready for one founder). The new-company box's arrow opens the company and starts it by itself: the box says "Starting {name}", then the company's page shows only its name and "Starting {name}" until the start is through, then the usual "{name} is starting" page, which opens the company. When your ChatGPT account is not connected yet, that page shows only the ChatGPT step ("Connect your ChatGPT account, and {name} starts.") with a red Cancel, and the company starts once it is connected; closing the step shows the table instead. Invite friends still opens the table. Someone else at the table, an idea that could not be saved, or a start that failed also shows the table, with what went wrong. Kept through a reload (the tab's founding intent gains `start`). No migration.
- The Members page shows the Co-Founder's asks: the agents it asks you to take for one area are one card ("Kaya, your Co-Founder, asks you to take Clara and Astrid.") with its reason underneath, answered with one Yes or No.
- Agents' figures cost the browser far less while they move, so the app stays smooth in Safari (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "on Safari it seems more laggy and slow"). A smoothness test on the offline harness showed the moving figures as the cause: with three working figures on screen, Safari on a 165 Hz screen missed about 19 % of frames, and 0.1 % with the figures held still; the frosted glass and the scroll fades made no difference. Each moving figure cut its eyes to the body on a full 512px layer every frame and halved that layer with the costliest resampling. The eyes are now cut after halving, at the figure's own size, and the exact 2:1 halving uses plain sampling, which is the same 2x2 average (pixel-identical in Chromium; in WebKit it now matches that average too). The recorded clip's read-back canvases keep their pixels in memory (`willReadFrequently`), and a figure no longer rewrites its `hidden` attributes every frame. Chromium's main thread on a still conversation went from about 65 % to 33 % busy; headless WebKit from 22–26 to 33–36 frames per second.
- Hovering no longer pops up a link address or a tooltip (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "whenever I hover anything I see link popups ... can I get rid of this?"). Links inside the app (the rail's agents, chats and pins, the apps drawer, Settings, back links) no longer carry an `href`, so the browser has no address to show in the corner; they keep their place in `data-href`, and one listener on the document follows them (ui/dom.ts `h()`): a click or Enter opens the place, Cmd/Ctrl/Shift-click or a middle click opens it in a new tab, and a handler that prevents the click still stops it. They stay links to the keyboard and screen readers (`role="link"`, `tabindex="0"`) and keep the pointer cursor. The browser's own right-click "Open link in new tab" is gone with the href. Links that leave the app (websites, invoices, links in messages) keep their `href`, so the browser still shows where they go. The rail's rows, pins, the Co-Founder card and the apps drawer's icons no longer have hover tooltips: they repeated the name and role already shown; the name, role and state stay in the link's accessible name. Icon-only buttons keep their tooltips.
- Closing Settings while Storage is open no longer flashes a bare, unstyled list of huge black folder icons (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026). Settings tore down Storage's folder browser before fading out, and that took the browser's styling off with it, so the fade showed the raw list for a moment. The browser in Settings now keeps its look until Settings is gone; the standalone Company File page still resets its page when it leaves.
- The sidebar matches Safari's bar again with "Show color in tab bar" off, Safari's default (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: after #1582 "it just no longer matches"). Safari ignores `theme-color` with that setting off, so the Safari greys of #1578 are back: on a light page on a light Mac the sidebar takes Safari's toolbar grey, lighter while the window is in front (#f6f6f7) and darker behind it (#e9eaeb). `theme-color` still carries the sidebar's colour, so with the setting on Safari paints its bar in the same grey; either way the two match. Also fixed: the bar colour was read before the app drew its first screen and not again, so it stayed on the page's colour; it now reads again when the app puts a screen or dialog into its root, and when the theme or Safari's in-front mark changes. Dark Safari greys are not measured yet, so dark keeps the design's grey.
- The browser's bar takes the sidebar's colour, instead of the sidebar guessing Safari's (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: match the bar of whatever browser is used). A page cannot read a browser's toolbar colour, so the page now tells the browser its own with `theme-color`: the colour of its top-left corner, which is the sidebar where one shows and the page elsewhere, kept current across routes, light and dark, window size and the sidebar hiding (src/ui/browser-bar.ts). The corner is found from the boxes' positions, not hit testing, so the sidebar still counts while Settings makes it inert; see-through layers such as a dialog's dimming are looked through. Browsers that honour it paint their bar to match in every theme: Safari with "Show color in tab bar" on, the app installed as a web app, and phone browsers; desktop Chrome, Edge and Firefox tabs keep their own bar. The Safari-only greys from the entry this replaces (#1578) are gone, so the sidebar is always the design's own grey again.
- The new-company table is simpler (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026). The company's name is a labelled field ("Company name" over it, an edge round it and a pencil in it) instead of a heading that did not look editable. The line under it ("Your first message to the Co-Founder. Write it together.") and each founder's own role line in the box ("What will you do?") are gone; roles a table already has still show after Start. The Invite friends popup drops "They join as co-founders.". The owner's "Close the table" link under the page is now a red Cancel button beside Ready and Start; with others still at the table it is greyed out with "To cancel, remove the other seats first." A founder who is not the owner keeps Leave the table. The idea box here and the box on the new-company page no longer draw a second rectangle round their text while you type in them; the box's own ring shows focus. No migration.
- In Safari on a Mac the sidebar takes the toolbar's own grey, so the two read as one surface (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: the sidebar should always match Safari's header). Safari draws its toolbar lighter while the window is in front (#f6f6f7) and darker behind it (#e9eaeb); the rail follows the window, and its hover and search greys follow the rail. Focus inside an embedded frame still counts as in front, also when the window comes back with focus in the frame. A page cannot tell its window going behind from focus moving into Safari's address bar (Cmd-L), so the sidebar also turns darker while an address is typed. Only while the page and the Mac are both light; dark, other browsers, iPads and an installed web app keep the design's own sidebar grey. The check runs when the app starts, not in the head script (its 3.5 KB budget), so the loading screen keeps the old grey for a moment.
- The Members page shows who each agent works for, in big pictures (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "this interface should help clear up and delegate responsibility/ownership between members, help you re-organize supervision and subscription commitments etc through simple and big visuals"). Up to three members get a mat each, side by side: their face, how much of their ChatGPT is used, and the agents that work for them, grouped by area. With more members (Jonas: "let's say there were 10"), every member is a small tile (a ring that fills with their usage, their agents' faces, and a yellow line when their ChatGPT is full or needs a new sign-in) and one member opens big below. Tap an agent and send it to another member; the other side says yes first, and the asks to answer show at the top. A suggestion offers a split when one member carries the whole crew, or a hand-over when someone's ChatGPT is full. Agents that work for nobody sit in an open pile. The Board and People tabs are gone. Reads `qorpera_read_works_for` (migration 20260930700000); Danish throughout.
- The other menus now look like the account menu (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: yes, match the right-click menus): the company menu at the top of the rail, the right-click menus on agents, sections, chats and company tiles, and their submenus. They use the system's own UI face at a regular weight and 15 px (was Source Sans 13.5 px at 500), 18 px icons in the text colour (were 16 px and pale), a clearly drawn thin edge (the stronger border colour), 10 px corners (was 12) and a lighter shadow. A submenu's first row still lines up with the row that opened it.
- The Friends window opens over Settings and leaves them open behind it; closing it gives the focus back to Settings' Friends button (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: opening the friends list closed Settings).
- Settings are tidier (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026). Appearance is part of General now, under Language & region, and its tab is gone (an old link to it opens General). The agent rules moved from General to the Company tab, under Good will, with an "Agent rules" heading. The Company tab no longer has the Storage card (Storage has its own place in the header; what it uses shows under Storage's navigation) or the Pages switches. Instead an owner or admin right-clicks Mailbox, Members or Bank in the rail's apps drawer and picks Hide from sidebar, as on an agent; a long press does the same on a touch screen. It is the same company-wide switch as before (`qorpera_set_interface_page`), so it hides the page for everyone; nothing the page showed is removed. A hidden page waits in the rail's Hidden fold, "Hidden for everyone", where a press or right-click offers Show in sidebar. Members, still off at first, shows up there once the company has a second member. Other members see no menu and no hidden page. No migration.
- The account menu's options read like a native menu (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "the fonts for the options is really bad, and slightly unreadable", like Claude's menus). The menu uses the system's own UI face (San Francisco on a Mac, Segoe UI on Windows) instead of Source Sans, at a regular weight (was 550) and 15 px (was 14), with a little more line height; the icons are 18 px in the text colour (were 16 px and pale), and Sign out is no longer greyed. The name above the options is a quiet 13 px grey caption.
- More out of Settings (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026). The Mailbox's Addresses tab, and its page while the company has no address, now hold the company's mail addresses (add, pause, remove) and the Digital Post card, for owners and admins; a new address or a connected Digital Post shows at once. Webhooks are listed under each agent's Routines (owners and admins add, pause and remove them). Sites is a page of its own, `#/c/<id>/sites`, reached from a globe "Sites" entry in the apps drawer while the company has any; the sites are read when the company opens and when the window comes back. Settings → Company connections keeps only the company's accounts; its Digital Post, webhooks and mailboxes and the Sites tab are gone. Coding values (environment variables a coding session gets, such as a token for private packages) are no longer shown on AI subscriptions or Company connections (Jonas: not something to put in front of founders); the cloud keeps them and coding sessions still get them, and a way for an agent to ask for one in chat is to follow.
- Hiding a page from the rail is each member's own, as hiding an agent is (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "hiding/unhiding interfaces should not be company wide, but userbased"; migration `20260930697000`). Any member right-clicks Mailbox, Members or Bank in the apps drawer to hide it from their own rail, and shows it again from the Hidden fold ("Hidden for you"). A page shows unless the member hid it; Members shows once the company has a second member. The pages answer gains `hidden`; a cloud without it keeps the company's switch and offers no menu.
- Settings are tidier (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026). Appearance is part of General now, under Language & region, and its tab is gone (an old link to it opens General). The agent rules moved from General to the Company tab, under Good will, with an "Agent rules" heading. The Company tab no longer has the Storage card (Storage has its own place in the header; what it uses shows under Storage's navigation) or the Pages switches. Instead a right-click on Mailbox, Members or Bank in the rail's apps drawer hides it (a long press on a touch screen), and the rail's Hidden fold shows it again; see the per-member entry above.
- The leaderboard shows people with their own profile picture (initials until they choose one), never a company's figure; companies keep their logo, which is still their figure until companies get logos of their own (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026).
- The account menu at the foot of the rail has a thin, clearly drawn border and a fixed width (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "it should have thin borders and a max width that doesn't scale with the sidepanels width"). It is 232 design pixels wide from the rail's left edge whatever width the rail is dragged to (before, it stretched across the whole rail; the folded rail's menu was 260), its edge uses the stronger border colour, its corners are a little tighter (10 px, was 14) and its shadow is lighter, so the border, not a soft glow, gives it its shape. The top bar's account menu gets the same edge.
- The apps drawer's icons can be dragged to reorder them (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "I would like for the interfaces to be draggable too, so you can reorder them"). Press an icon and move it a few pixels: it lifts and follows the pointer, the others glide out of its way, and letting go keeps the new order. A press that never moves still opens the page, and the click that ends a drag opens nothing. Escape puts everything back. Alt with an arrow moves the focused icon one place. Touch scrolls as before. The order is the member's own, kept per company on this device in `localStorage` (`qorpera.web.rail-apps-order:<company>`), like whether the drawer is open. A page or app that joins later lands at the end. Mailbox, Members, Bank, agents' apps and Concepts all move. No migration.
- The Members emblem no longer shows a white rim around one of its two people (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "the members interface has a white border for one of the people that should be removed"). An emblem's cuts are now holes in a mask instead of strokes painted in the page colour, so the rail, the page header and the open tile show through them; before, the cut around the front person was painted white and showed against the tinted header and the rail. The calendar emblem's rings are notches for the same reason.
- Opening a file in Settings → Storage feels quicker (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "opening files feels slightly laggy"). The window opens at once with the file's name and breathing lines while the file is read, then the file fades in; before, nothing showed until the read was done. The window grows out of the file's icon and shrinks back into it on close (Escape, Close or a click beside it), and an icon gives a little under a press. Nothing moves under reduced motion. The list behind the window no longer makes room for a side preview it does not have, so it stays still while a file is open.
- The page no longer scrolls past the app (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "scrolling down, there is a blank section below the actual app"). The app's frame now clips anything inside it that reaches past the bottom of the window, so the page is always exactly the window's height and the browser has nothing to scroll to; before, such a part made the whole page scrollable and it scrolled, leaving an empty band under the app and hiding its top. Menus and dialogs, which sit over the page, are not clipped. Sign-in still scrolls on a short window.
- A coding value card (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: coding values happen behind the scenes; migration `20260930693000`). When an agent's coding sessions need an environment variable only a person can give, the agent raises a secret card for it in its thread. The card says where it goes ("Coding sessions in acme/web get it as NPM_TOKEN; it never shows in the chat") instead of "the agent never sees it". An owner or admin who saves it saves it for the company and gives it to the card's repository at once; anyone else, or a card with no repository, saves it as their own, which reaches their coding sessions in every repository, and the card says so ("Saved as your own ... every repository"). A value saved but not given to the repository keeps the card open and says so.
- A file opened in Settings → Storage opens in its own window over Settings, the way a picture is viewed, instead of a narrow panel beside the files (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "it should open as it's own window over the settings window"). The window is large (up to 960 by 900 px, the whole screen on a phone), a document reads at a comfortable width, a picture fits the window, and an HTML page fills it; Download and History stay at its foot, and History opens in the same window. Close, Escape or a click beside it closes it and puts focus back on the file; Settings stays open under it. The standalone Company File page keeps its preview beside the files.
- Storage's foot and the Storage card in Settings show only how much the company stores in total ("25.0 MiB in total"), with no bar against a limit (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026). Danish "i alt".
- The company rail (the side panel) is a tenth wider by default, and can be dragged a tenth wider too (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "I would like the default width of the sidepanel to be 20% wider", "the max width of the sidepanel should be 20% wider too", then "that was a bit much. maybe instead of 20% we just did 10%?"). Its default is 406 design pixels (was 369; Small: 502, was 456) and its cap is 44% of the window (was 40%). A width dragged before this is not carried over, so everyone starts once from the new default.
- The thread's foot has no background of its own (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "it should be transparant, with the widgets possibly being more glassy a-la apple style"). Notices, the agent's wall, the coding pill and the composer stand over the messages' last stretch, and the messages scroll on behind them; the view keeps room under the last message for the foot's measured height (`--thread-foot`). The controls are frosted glass (a translucent surface over an 18px blur, a hairline edge and a soft shadow, in both themes), the open wall is a frosted sheet, and a note or the Answering strip gets a glass chip to be read on. Only the controls take the pointer, so a wheel or click between them reaches the messages.
- The coding sessions' pill stands on the agent wall's line, level with its button, instead of a line of its own above it (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026). Unfolded, the pill keeps its usual width over the button. While coding sessions go, a single teammate's draft keeps its own place above the composer instead of the line's left. Under 680px of thread width the pill and the wall's button stack, with the pill under the button. A Codex session wears its prompt mark, ">_", instead of OpenAI's knot, which read as ChatGPT and at badge size as a blur; the badge on a figure is a little bigger, so the mark reads. CSS and marks only.
- Every interface emblem is black or white against the member's theme, instead of in its own colour (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026: "simplify the interfaces, by making them all either black or white, contrasting the users color theme"). Mailbox, Members, Bank, agents' apps, concepts and app cards draw the solid shape in the text colour with cuts in the page colour: black on the light theme, white on the dark. An open page's tile in the apps drawer keeps a white emblem with cuts in the selection colour. A file's or app.toml's `colour` is still read and kept, but no longer tints the emblem.
- Friends and a leaderboard (Jonas, 9 Nov 2026; migration `20260930690000`). Friends open as a window of their own, out of Settings: from a friends button left of the account in the top bar (a dot while a company invitation waits), from the account menu, and inside a company from a plain **Friends** button left of Settings | Storage in Settings' header. Each accepted friend shows when they were last online ("Online now" with a green dot on their picture, else "Last online 3 hours ago"); the app marks the account online while a tab is in front (`qorpera_mark_online`, every four minutes). Settings → **Leaderboard** takes the Friends tab's place (Jonas: direction C, "Champion of the week"): four wide tabs across the page, **Busiest friends** and **Busiest companies** (tasks done this week, a round from Monday 00:00 Danish time; a task is an agent's work saved to a company) and **Top-earning friends** and **Top-earning companies** (all sales so far, without VAT). The leader gets a banner with their figure cheering in confetti, how far ahead they are, and how long the week has left; the ranking shows who moved up or down since last week, and your own row says how far it is to the next place. A friend's figure is their busiest company's. A switch, "Show me on my friends' leaderboards", takes the account off its friends' boards (`qorpera_set_leaderboard_hidden`). A database before the migration shows "The leaderboard isn't available yet." and no online marks.
- The company hub draws each company's figure in a colour of its own instead of all in charcoal (Jonas, 8 Nov 2026: "the company agents should be generated with a random color so they are not all black"). The colour is one of the palette's nine bright colours (never Charcoal or Gray), derived from the company id with a hash of its own, so it stays the same on every reload and in every client and does not follow the figure. The same logo shows on invitations and in Friends. A right-click (or the menu key) on a company tile in the hub opens a menu with Delete company for a founder, or Leave company for anyone else; it asks exactly as the company's settings do (Delete asks for the company name typed) and calls the same cloud functions, and the tile goes once the cloud says yes. A refusal shows on the hub. Shift+right-click keeps the browser's own menu. No migration.
- The open page in the rail's apps drawer is marked like an open agent: its tile takes the selection colour with white text, instead of a coloured name and a dot (Jonas, 8 Nov 2026: "it's cards background should use the highlights, like it does with any other agent"). The emblem turns white with its cuts in the selection colour, so the green Bank emblem still shows on the green tile.
- A Concepts viewer, `#/c/<id>/concepts` and `#/c/<id>/concepts/<name>` (Jonas, 8 Nov 2026: "a fullscreen view of the selected concept, with a sidepanel for the different ideas/designs you could select to view"). An app whose app.toml says `concept = true` is a concept of the company's product. The viewer is full screen, with no rail and no top bar: a slim side panel in Qorpera's light look has the way back to the company, a Concepts heading and one item per concept (cover or emblem, title, about, who made it), with Reload and Show in Storage for the open one, and a strip that never folds away (under the list, along the folded strip, under the phone's top bar) saying who made the open concept and "Qorpera never asks for passwords here"; a cover shows by its file's version, so one made or replaced shows at the folder's next change; the concept's frame fills the rest with no header over it (`AppPanel`'s `concept` placement, same loader, frame and bridge). Without a name it opens the most recently changed concept. An item switches the concept in place. The panel folds to a strip (kept per viewer in `localStorage`, `qorpera.web.concepts-collapsed`); at phone width it is a top bar with a menu. The apps drawer shows one Concepts entry when the company has a concept, and a concept's own `#/c/<id>/a/<name>` link and its `app:` links in chat open the viewer at it. Other apps keep their page.
- The app shows its first screen as soon as the stylesheet is in, not an empty page until `main.js` has run (perf sprint round 2, measure T8, 8 Nov 2026). `index.html`'s `#app` now holds a static copy of the app's own loading shapes for its three first screens: the sign-in page, the company list and a company's thread (`ui/skeleton.ts`'s markup and classes, no images, nothing downloads). The head script (`theme.js`, via `ui/boot.ts`) picks the one the app will draw before the first paint, from whether a session is stored (never what it holds) and the address, and the member's rail width; a sign-in callback, the new-company box, the account page, a founding table, a company page other than a thread and a compact rail show none, as before. The app's first draw replaces it, and a redraw before Auth has answered keeps it rather than blanking the page. Its shapes breathe on the document's clock, as the app's own do, so the swap shows no blink. It is hidden from assistive technology and inert. On a slow phone line (1.6 Mbit/s, 150 ms, CPU 4x slower) the page stayed empty for about 3.2-3.8 s; on local builds the shapes stand from about 1.3 s (measured; live expected about 1.4 s).
- An agent's app opens as a page of its own, `#/c/<id>/a/<name>`, in the whole main area as Mailbox and Bank do (Jonas, 8 Nov 2026: an app is a whole product prototype, not a side panel). Its slim header has the way back (to the company's last open conversation, else the company), the app's emblem and name, "Made by <agent>" and "Qorpera never asks for passwords here", Reload and Show in Storage. The apps drawer's icons are links to the page, and an app's link or card in a message, on the wall or in a choice opens it. A deep link or a reload opens the company, waits for its first read, then the app; an app that is gone says "This app isn't in the company anymore." The panel beside the chat now shows only a `folder:` link to an `.html` file. Danish too.
- A change reaches an open company tab by push (perf sprint T3, 8 Nov 2026; migration `20260930653000`): the database sends it on the member's own private topic `qorpera-live-<company>-<member>` at commit, only to the members who may read the changed row then. On a test machine, side by side, it arrived 4 ms after the write (median; p95 16 ms) instead of 278 ms (p95 450 ms), and Realtime no longer polls the database for it, which was half of the database's time on 8 Nov. A database without the topic refuses the join; the tab then listens by `postgres_changes` as before, and tabs from before this change keep doing so until they close. The sidebar's hint comes on the same topic.
- The line above the composer, "No progress for 15 minutes. If it looks stuck, press Stop.", is gone (Jonas, 7 Nov 2026: "dont like this message btw - get rid of it"). A long tool call or a listener can be quiet for longer than that. The composer's Stop stays.
- A run or coding session whose save did not finish now says so in plain words and gives the next step: "Send a message to carry on" (Jonas, 7 Nov 2026). It used to say a person must check it, which a founder can't do.
- A tab left open costs the cloud far less, and a thread's composer needs fewer round trips (perf sprint A6, 7 Nov 2026). On production one tab with nobody looking sent 19 requests a minute all night (06 Nov 18:20Z to 23:20Z), and 8,183 of 30,064 browser requests in 11.5 hours were CORS preflights. A hidden tab whose live hints work now reads on no clock: the 30 s heartbeat, the work watch and the apps' minute poll wait, and hints still read what they name. Its first moment back reads, if the clock came due or it was away a whole clock period (a frozen tab's timers never fire). A channel that is down keeps its clock, also one that failed while hidden, which the badge does not show. The account read has no hints: it waits while hidden and reads on return. The usage refresh after an account read takes the logins that read just found, instead of reading them again. The frequent calls (the accounts and a send's turn request, the company folder and its objects, notices, a run's control, a thread's model) no longer ask for `cache: "no-store"`: Chrome skips its preflight cache for such a request, so each cost a second round trip, and no browser stores the answer to a POST (the objects' route answers `no-store` itself). Nothing changes on screen.
- An agent's message shows as soon as it is saved (perf sprint 7 Nov 2026, measure A1): in a tab someone is looking at, a live hint is read at once instead of after 250 ms and then another 400 ms, and the rest of a burst is one more read at the end of the interval. A hidden tab keeps the wait, so it reads no more than before.
- Small fixes from the Rumlig test (Jonas, 7 Nov 2026: "fix the issues first"). Where the chat and the panel beside it don't both fit, the panel covers the chat, with Close, instead of running off the window. An app says "Opening…" until its page is there. An app's link in the middle of a sentence keeps the sentence whole, with its name as a link and the card under it. Enter pressed a moment before Send opens is sent when it does, within two seconds, if the text is the same.
- The Mailbox and Bank pages show from the start (Jonas, 7 Nov 2026; migration `20260930583000`), each with its own empty state: "The company has no email address yet" and "No bank yet". An owner or admin who switches one off keeps it off.
- An app can show videos: `.mp4` and `.webm` in its folder, in `<video src>` or `<source src>`, each at most 12 MiB and 48 MiB in all.
- Answer on the agent's wall now opens a reply in the message box (Jonas, 7 Nov 2026: "in the chatcomposer you should see some form of replying/answering visual"). The wall closes and the box shows "Answering" with the question, which × or Escape takes back. The message opens with the question as a quoted line (`> …`), so the agent reads what is being answered and the thread shows it above the member's words. Sent, the question says "Answered" on the wall (a press answers again) and no longer counts as waiting, until the agent takes it off. That mark stays in the open page: after a reload the question shows Answer again until the agent has taken it off.
- The rail's Co-Founder stands closer to the top row: half the space above its card (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026). The figure stands into a 16px band the list reaches up under the top row, which stays above it and keeps its buttons; scrolled, the band hides the list. Opening the apps drawer pushes the Co-Founder and the list down to make room, as before.
- A founder whose ChatGPT logins are all used up is still connected at the founding table (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026: "isnt it already connected?", then "Yes, fix it"; migration `20260930579000`). Their seat says "ChatGPT used up · resets <time>" instead of "ChatGPT not connected", Start works, and a line under it says the Co-Founder begins by itself at the reset. Once started, the page says when the waiting first run goes on. Danish too.
- The agent's wall shows headlines only (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026: "that's still too much, I wouldn't want that much text per healine. instead they should be clickable rows you can click to expand details"). An item with more to it is a row with a chevron: a click opens its detail (a sentence or two), its links and a preview of the picture (an image of the company folder, an SVG sketch included) or the app it links; it stays open across redraws, and the picture is read only when it opens (`app/folder-images.ts`, the member's own folder client). A question card whose options carry a `preview` shows them as big boxes with the picture, the option's name and its short line; a click picks it, and once answered the boxes stay, inert, the chosen one marked ("I would instead of a long text want a short headline and two big visual boxes for the options").
- The agent's card (a right-click on the agent in the rail, or its name at the top of the chat on a phone) has a **Latest notes** row once the wall has something on it, with "N new" for what the member has not seen open (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026). It closes a sheet over the thread, opens the agent's company thread, opens the wall, marks what is new and gives it focus. What was seen and the fold state are kept per member and agent in browser storage, no migration.
- A conversation at its login's usage limit takes a message again: it waits and goes on by itself, on the same login, once the limit resets ("A message you send now waits and goes on by itself when it resets"; Jonas, 5 Nov 2026; migration `20260930575000`). Nothing moves to another login by itself; "Or use another of your logins now" stays as the member's own pick. The member whose login reached its limit reads one line above the composer ("Your login a@… reached its usage limit. Work will go on by itself when it resets (about 4 h)."). A routine waiting for its limit and a coding session's follow-up say they go on by themselves.
- A good will invoice that could not charge the card shows a line above the composer for the company's founders and the card's member, with Stripe's invoice page (member notification `good_will_card_failed`, migration `20260930571000`); Stripe's paid retry takes it away.
- Agents' interface apps (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026: "the interfaces is where you see the company"; deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACE-APPS.md). A link `[Title](app:<name>)` in a message draws as a card in the bubble (the app's emblem and line once the company's apps are read); a press opens the app on its own page (see the app page above). A `folder:` link to an `.html` file opens in a side panel that stands beside the thread, which stays as it is and usable (the agent sheet's shape, not over the chat); its header, drawn outside the page, names the page, "A page of the Company File" and "Qorpera never asks for passwords here", with Reload, Show in Storage and Close (Escape closes it too). Posted apps join the apps drawer after Mailbox, Members and Bank. An app is put together from its folder with the member's own reads (its stylesheets and scripts inline, its pictures as `data:` URLs, at most 64 files and 4 MiB) and runs in the Company File's locked frame, handed over once with a private message port: `qorpera.read/list/table/todos/agents` read what the manifest's `reads` allows (never the platform's folders), read only, bounded per app; `qorpera.onChange` follows the folder. The frame document (`public/page-preview/index.html`) gives a page the port's frozen `qorpera` object only when one is handed over. One folder-hint channel now serves the pages' reader and the apps'. Danish too.
- The rail's top row is the company's name, with no figure: a press opens the member's companies (this one checked) and New company (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026). The Agents | Interfaces tabs are gone. A four-windows button beside the name opens the company's pages (Mailbox, Members, Bank and the pages agents build) as icons under the top row, four to a row; they spring in one after another and push the agents down, and the drawer stays open until the button is pressed again (kept per company on this device). An interface's page opens it once and marks its icon. While it is closed, a red dot on the button says a page has something waiting. A company with no pages has no button. Search looks through the agents only and folds out over the name. The collapsed rail shows the four windows and the icons without names.
- Two agents' conversation opens as a sheet that slides in from the right over the thread, which waits dimmed behind it (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026). The sheet's header names both agents with their figures and how many messages there are, and has a close button; Close, Esc or a press on the dimmed thread slides it away. Its foot says "Only the two agents write here". The old cream header bar and the Close Chat button are gone.
- Good will replaces Plan & usage (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026; design direction A, canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/YGQEiStXM956UZd8gnr4Pq; migration `20260930566000`). Settings → Company has a Good will section under Staff at work (Danish "Goodwill", one string in `ui/danish/settings.ts`): what the company pays Qorpera each month (10 % of the first 100,000 kr of sales, then less), its invoices with Stripe's invoice page, and the card that pays them; a founder adds or changes the card on Stripe's own page (`computer-billing/good-will-card`). Settings → General with no company open, and the account page, list "Good will in your companies": what each company sold and paid this year, whose card pays, and what the member's own card paid. The Plan & usage tab, its plan offers, plan checkout, computer-hour packs and the computer hours card are removed (`views/plan.ts`, `plan-payments.ts`, `cloud/computer-billing.ts`, `cloud/computer-hours.ts`); storage stays on the Company tab's Storage card.
- Code in chat messages now wraps inside the bubble: a long line (for example a tool's JSON after "machine_start returned verbatim") no longer scrolls sideways (Jonas, 4 Nov 2026). Inline code breaks anywhere too. The same rule covers everything drawn through the message markdown, so code in Storage's Markdown previews and in interface text blocks wraps the same way. CSS only.
- Chat images take the member company gateway (the public bridge) as Company File does, once the build names `memberCompanyGatewayUrl`: their upload and their read (`/v1/member-session-attachments/…`) went straight to the object gateway, whose name resolves to a private tailnet address on a member's tailnet machine, where the browser refuses it (4 Nov 2026). The bridge function gains the attachment route (PUT of one image up to 8 MiB, GET of an input), and both transports share `memberRouteTarget` (`cloud/deployment-config.ts`). Without the field nothing changes.
- Claude sign-in works (coding sessions only, migration `20260930564000`): AI subscriptions → Add account → Claude opens Anthropic's own sign-in, and a field takes the code Anthropic shows ("Paste the code from Claude"), sent once. The member's Claude login then shows as "Claude · coding only" with Sign out. Claude is offered only while the member may sign in with it (`qorpera_list_claude_logins`).
- Settings → My connections is now **AI subscriptions** (Danish "AI-abonnementer"), in the nav, its heading and every "Settings → …" hint (Jonas, 3 Nov 2026). Settings opened on the old tab name (`my-connections`) show AI subscriptions. With no company open (Settings from the company list with several companies), AI subscriptions now works: it lists the member's account logins (`qorpera_list_account_model_logins`, migration `20260930548000`), each "Used in all your companies" with its usage where it can be read and "Sign out everywhere", and "Add ChatGPT subscription" starts the sign-in at once (no provider picker, no Claude, no ChatGPT plan). A sign-in always begins in a company: the page reads and signs in through the member's first company, by company id, whose sign-ins are account sign-ins (the list's `enabled`) and where the member's identity is not retired, so the cloud keeps it as an account login and links it into every company of theirs. Nothing is enabled from here, and that company's own logins are neither shown nor refreshed; a sign-in the cloud kept for that company only, or one that ended unfinished, is said so. A member with no company reads "Create or join a company first"; one whose companies can't share a subscription is told to open a company. Inside a company the page is as before (#1396's "Used in all your companies", "Stop using here", "Sign out everywhere"). With no company open, Settings show only the account's pages (General, Appearance, Friends, Plan & usage, AI subscriptions) and no Storage switch; Company or Company connections asked for shows General until a company is open. No migration or vault change.
- Quotes of an email travel as quotes, never as the member's own words (Interfaces step I5d-4; migrations `20260928459100`, `20260928469160`). A message button puts each email value in the composer as one tinted piece in «» marks, with the email's thread as its record, and a line under the field says agents read it as quoted. The member keeps a quote or removes it whole (the caret steps over it; Backspace at its edge takes all of it) and writes around it. Messages send their `quotes`, shared drafts save and restore them (an empty list once the last one is gone), the thread shows them as quoted, and a name inside one names nobody. A row without its thread id gets no message button. A quote pasted over another stays a quote, shared drafts merge each quote whole, and a draft the cloud won't take as it is (a refused quote, more than 16 quotes) says so instead of retrying. The composer keeps its own undo history, since the browser's can put back a quote without its record.

### Added
- Added Default models settings above AI subscriptions (`settings-default-models.ts`, `cloud/default-models.ts`), subscription API credits display (`settings-connections.ts`, `cloud/native-subscription.ts`), Gemini remaining budget + live key health + zero-token Check key + AI Studio billing link (`settings-gemini-key.ts`, `cloud/gemini-key.ts`), and specific `GEMINI_API:*` run-failure messages (`ui/run-failure.ts`), with Danish translations (needs migration `20260930858000_member_default_models_and_gemini_health.sql`).
- Two thread pieces for projects and sites (Jonas, 15 Nov 2026: "chat widgets if there are UX components we need to add"). A change ready to go live stands as a card below the thread of the agent behind it (the Co-Founder's when no agent made it, or it left), one per project, a question's width: the agent's figure and "Ready to go live", the newest noticeable change's picture large and its line, "and N more changes" leading to What's new, and What's new's own Look first, Put it live and Not yet for owners and admins (Not yet puts "Not yet: " into that thread's composer). Once put live it settles like an answered question, "Version N is live." with the site's address as a plain link; a site Qorpera took down gets one quiet line instead of a card. Its read, writes, outcomes and Look first's tab are What's new's, moved into one shared desk (`app/views/release-desk.ts`), which What's new now draws from unchanged; the card shares the apps drawer's read. A message that links one of the company's sites (its own host, its old `sites.qorpera.com/<slug>/` link, or an app link's host, matched against the company's list) shows the site under its words as one row beside the app cards' (`app/views/thread-sites.ts`): the globe, its title and state, its address, a key that copies the password for owners and admins (never shown in the thread), and the whole row opening the site; a taken-down site's row is grey with Qorpera's reason and leads to Sites. The link in the words stays a link, in their own colour.
- A card's body is drawn as Markdown, as a message is (lines, lists, folder links), and a body past 5 lines or 360 characters as shown folds behind Show more / Show less (`ui/card-fold.ts`; the fold goes when nothing is hidden, and an opened card stays open when drawn again); a review card's long quoted message or file text folds the same way (Jonas, 5 November 2026: "approval cards are completely unreadable").
- An agent's own chat has a wall (Jonas, 5 Nov 2026: "a poster or wall of updates kept within rules, instead of many new messages"). It is the agent's file `QorperaOS/agents/<handle>/wall.md` (`@qorpera/cloud-client/agent-wall`), read with the member's own folder client (the handle from the member's state folders) and read again after each change hint of the company's folder, when the agent's run ends and when the tab gets focus. Items are one line of Markdown each: folder links and app cards (drawn as small chips) open as in a message. What was seen and the fold are kept on this device; an item that joins while it is on screen slides in, and the fold animates its height (none of it under reduced motion). No wall in group chats, or while the agent has no wall or an empty one. Danish too.
- A conversation whose model login has reached its usage limit (or is disabled) now offers the member's other logins that can pay ("Or continue with another of your logins"). The conversation still never moves on its own; only the member's click moves it (`qorpera_choose_member_session_account`).
- The composer's "Chat settings and more" menu has a Login row when the member has two or more logins that can pay for the conversation. It opens a page drawn like Model's: the member's own logins that can pay (the account's email, else "Model account N", numbered and ordered as "Choose a login to continue" numbers them), the conversation's current one ticked, and "Pays for your messages in this conversation. Applies from your next message." A pick moves the conversation the way the login choice above the composer does (`chooseSessionAccount` → `qorpera_choose_member_session_account` where the cloud chooses, else the local sponsor), so a member can leave a login that works badly, not only one that is used up or disabled. The row is disabled while a message is sending, a retry waits (it keeps its original login) or the member can't write. Nothing moves on its own; Claude logins are never offered for chat. Danish too.

- Connect the company's bank from the Bank page (Bank connection part 4). An owner or admin sees Connect the bank on the empty page (Connect again in the paused line, which replaces the company's connection): a dialog asks which bank (Danske Bank, Nordea, Jyske Bank, Sydbank, Nykredit Bank, Arbejdernes Landsbank, Spar Nord Bank, Lunar; with "The account is in my own name" for a personal account), says the member logs in at the bank with MitID, chooses the accounts and that agents can read but not pay, and goes to the bank's own page (`createWebConnectors(...).beginSignIn("bank", existing, signal, {name, country: "DK", psuType})`, the start body's `bank`). The bank sends the member back to the Bank page, which finishes the sign-in (`noteBankReturn`, the app routes a `bank` return there instead of Settings) and says the numbers arrive within a few minutes; a connected bank without a summary yet says so, and other members read that an owner or admin can connect it. Closing the dialog (or leaving the page) ends a sign-in still starting, so a late answer never leaves for the bank, and the start reads the page to come back to before the ticket is issued. How a sign-in went shows beside the numbers too (Connect again on a paused page); a connection that could not be read says so with Try again; the page draws again when the member's role changes. `takeConnectorReturn` accepts the `bank` connector; the Services cards are unchanged and leave the bank's return to the Bank page. `InterfaceWrites` gains `bankConnection`.

- A card whose work a member stopped says so (2026-10-22 amendment, item 4; migration `20260930533000`): the transcript reads `qorpera.chain-stopped/v1` and an open question, email or approval card shows "A member stopped the work that raised this card. Answering it starts new work." (to whoever may answer; others read the first sentence). The card stays answerable. Danish too.

- The company hub is rebuilt as hub round 3 (Jonas, 22 October 2026; design canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/CQTTymPUwNskh8QCkQKixf, page "Round 3"). With companies, the hub is a simple selector: "Welcome back, {name}. Pick a company." and one tile per company, founding table and invitation, each the company's figure on a stage and its name, nothing more (no role, date or status line); an invitation's tile carries the inviter's picture, and a company invitation asks "Join … as …?" with Decline on the tile's corner; the last tile is New company. New company (`#/new`, and the hub itself for a member with no company yet) is qorpera.com's hero in the app: "From idea to company.", one box for the idea with the Co-Founder's crown looking over its edge, the website's kinds of company under it (a kind fills an empty box with its example), an empty box writing out example ideas as the website does, and Invite friends in the box. The arrow opens a founding table (with no name yet unless one is typed, changeable at the table) with the idea as the table's text; Invite friends opens the table with the friends list open, so friends write the first message to the Co-Founder together. qorpera.com's box already links to `app.qorpera.com/?ide=…&kind=…` (and a link may say `#/new?idea=…`): the app now keeps the idea and the kind through a sign-in, takes them out of the address at once, and opens New company with the idea in the box ("Your idea from qorpera.com") and the kind pressed; a kind without an idea fills the box with its example. No migration; the table's rules are unchanged (a name to open, each founder's own ChatGPT before Ready).
- The founding table is drawn as writing the first message together: the company's name is the page's heading (editable), the box holds the idea, which every founder may edit, and each founder's own line (their role) in their own colour, and the people at the table look over the box's edge as the crown does. Under it, one chip per seat (Ready, Not ready yet, ChatGPT not connected, Invited, with Remove or Withdraw where allowed), then Ready and a centred Start. Alone at the table, Start is one press: Ready, then Start. Ready and Start (alone or together) never go ahead while words you changed in any field are not on the table (a save that failed), and your words stay in their field; an idea the new-company box could not save as it opened the table is tried again, only ever on a table that still has no text, kept in the tab through a reload, and shown in the box as your unsaved text until it is saved. The table still polls every 2.5 s; live presence and typing are not part of this change.
- Live presence at the founding table (migration `20260930531000`, SQL test 788; design canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/CQTTymPUwNskh8QCkQKixf, board "G · Writing the first message together"). Every seat of an open table, joined or invited, joins the table's private Realtime topic `qorpera-founding-<profile id>`. Over the box, the people here now have a ring in their colour (you always) and someone writing a pulsing one; their chip says "Writing" in their colour (a screen reader also hears "Here now"), and the box says "{name} is writing" ("{names} are writing"). A founder's words and caret go out as they write: at most every 200 ms, again every 5 s while they stay in the field, and their last words before they leave it. An idea box, name or role line you have not touched follows the newest writer's words as it follows the table's, their new words tinted in their colour, until their save shows (held 6 s after they leave the field); their caret and name flag stand where they write, in your own words too when you changed the box. Nothing of it is saved: Ready and Start still wait for your queued saves and check your own fields, and words the box only follows are not yours.
