Each tab registers a device with a signing key at the table and signs what it sends; the others look the device up, so the person comes from the table's seats, never from the tab (as company presence does). On a cloud without the migration the topic client meets PGRST202 and stops: the table reads itself every 2.5 s as before, with no rings and no writing line.
- Digital Post in the Mailbox and in Settings (part 4 of `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-DIGITAL-POST.md`; reads `qorpera_member_digital_post` of migration `20260930529000` and the folder's `digital-post/letters.json`, which part 3 writes). Letters from public authorities are lines of the Inbox among the email threads by when they came: the authority, the title, the start of the text, a Digital Post mark and "Legal effect" where it applies; search finds them. A letter opens to its flags, its text and its documents, which open in Storage, with a line to send a note about it to an agent the member picks (the Co-Founder at first). The note names the letter by its id and its year's folder only: an authority's words are never the member's. Addresses has a Digital Post row with how it stands, how many letters, and "Choose who reads it", which asks the Co-Founder for a routine on `digital-post/`. The Mailbox shows when the company has an email address or Digital Post, so a company without email gets it too, without Write. Settings → Company connections has a Digital Post card: how it stands, and for an owner or admin the steps in Administrativ Adgang (Qorpera's CVR and the vault's addresses), a field for the API key, which goes straight to the company vault, Replace key and Disconnect. A pasted key is checked before it is sent, and a new key whose answer was lost keeps its capture ticket for the next try (the coding variables' rule, now shared in `cloud/connectors.ts`). A letter too long to show (over 1 MiB) says so and opens from its folder. On a cloud without the call, the card and the Mailbox's Digital Post stay hidden. Danish for every new line.
- The agent and group chat cards have Pin (or Unpin) as the first icon in their foot, so a phone, where the rail can't be dragged, and a member without edit rights can pin and unpin: pins are the member's own. It is left out when the pinned row is full.
- Group chats get the agent treatment: a right-click (or long press) on a group chat opens its card beside the row instead of a menu (its name, subtitle and description edited in place, its participants, then Copy conversation ID, Hide and Delete as plain icons, shown even while the details load or when they can't be read), and a group chat has no header on a wide screen. Pin, Move to and Rename leave the group chat's menu (pinning and moving are drags; the name edits on the card). An opened routine offers Edit for every routine the member may change but ended ones, also those the agent schedules, windowed, webhook and two-trigger routines: their composer keeps how they run (When as words) and changes only name, instruction, Reply and Runs on.
- An agent's routines are a page of its card again, in the simpler design: the card widens for them, a back arrow returns to the details, and the composer opens in the card. Every routine row has its on/off switch; a done or ended routine's is off and locked, and says why. The card is a little wider, its description uses the card's full width and stops growing at five lines, and no scrollbar shows. The routines page over the chat is gone. In the card the page's top row is the way back, "Routines" and a + for a new routine. A routine opens to be read first, with Edit where the member may change it; the composer no longer shows the time zone (a new routine takes the viewer's, an edit keeps the file's) or the note about using the account while away, and the chosen Reply is a pill.
- A coding session's own chat has no header bar: a pill floats at its top (the agent's figure and the harness's mark, "Bo ⇄ Claude Code" and the session's title) with the transcript scrolling under it, and a labelled "Back to Bo" beside it returns to the agent's chat. On a phone the way back is an arrow and the pill drops the title.
- The agent card's foot is three plain icons at its right edge, named by their tooltips: Copy conversation ID (a check for a moment once copied; the card stays open), Hide from sidebar (or Show in sidebar) and Delete. No backgrounds, no words.
- HTML pages open live in Storage (Jonas, 21 Oct: "HTML opens live in the app: yes"). An `.html` or `.htm` file's preview shows the page, with a Page / Source switch, and a button shows it across the window in a dialog over Settings. The page runs in a frame that lets its own inline styles and scripts run and nothing else: the frame loads `public/page-preview/`, a fixed document with its own policy (its meta tag and a `/page-preview/*` rule in `public/_headers`), sandboxed with `allow-scripts` only, so it has no origin (none of the app's cookies, storage or sign-in), opens no windows or dialogs, sends no forms and never moves the app's page, and loads nothing from the network. No browser lets a policy stop WebRTC or link hints, so the frame document removes the WebRTC interfaces before the page runs and takes out any frame put into the page or into a shadow root it makes (a frame is a window of its own); it refuses the ways to make an unwatched shadow root (cloning one, `setHTMLUnsafe`, a declarative one written during the parse), and the app hands the page over by `postMessage` only after checking it brings no declarative shadow root, `dns-prefetch` or `preconnect` link, or `noscript` (whose content the frame parses differently from the check); a page that has some is handed over without them, and one that can't be made fit shows its source (`ui/page-frame.ts`). A hint the page's own script adds while it runs can still make the browser look up a name or open a connection; the frame holds nothing of the app. `scripts/test-web-page-frame-browser.mjs` checks all this in Chromium with the site's headers. A `srcdoc` or `blob:` frame would take the app's own policy, which blocks inline styles and scripts, hence the document of its own.
- Folder links: an agent links a file or folder of the Company File as `[name](folder:designs/sales-dashboard.html)`. A message draws it as a link-styled button, and following it opens Settings → Storage over the conversation at that file with its preview open (a page as a page), or at that folder. Only a plain company-relative path counts (`ui/folder-links.ts`, the Company File's own path rules after percent-decoding): no leading or trailing slash, no empty, `.` or `..` name, no other scheme; anything else stays text. Links in a document Storage shows open there too.
- Simpler agent screens (design canvas "Simpler agent chrome", approved by Jonas 21 Oct). An agent's own chat has no header on a wide screen; a phone keeps it. A right-click (or long press) on an agent in the rail opens its card beside the row instead of a menu: the figure, name, title and description, edited in place as before, then the agent's Computer and Routines as two lines, then Hide (Show for a hidden agent) and Delete as two square buttons. Pin, Move to, Rename and Copy conversation ID leave the agent's menu (pinning and moving are drags, the name edits on the card); group chats keep their menu. Computer and Routines open in the agent's chat, going there first when another page is open. Coding sessions still going sit in a pill above the composer, with a gap under it: the agent's figure wearing the harness mark, the title or "N coding agents", and who needs an answer; hover, focus or a tap unfolds it upward into a line per session. Routines open over the chat as a page of their own (styles/agent-routines.css): a figure and "Routines", rows with a name, the schedule and next run in words and an on/off switch, no cards; a row opens a plain composer (name, what to do, When as day circles and a time or "every few hours", Reply as Always / When there is news / Never, Runs on with Change, Save), read-only for routines the member can't change. The thread header's computer button and coding figures are gone.
- The Mailbox is rebuilt as a mail app's inbox (views/mailbox.ts, styles/mailbox.css; design canvas "Qorpera Mail" v3; migration `20260930522000`, SQL test 719). The page draws itself from the company's mail, not from its definition's blocks: Inbox, Sent and Addresses as plain tabs, a search field and Write, and no boxes. The Inbox lists the sends waiting for a person first ("Waiting for you"), each answered on its line with Send or Don't send through its card, then every other thread, one line each: who wrote (and the agent, once the company wrote last), the subject, the start of the newest message (the threads call's new `snippet`), the agent who handles it (grey while the thread is new) and when. Search narrows the lines read so far. A thread folds its older messages to one line each and opens the newest; a send waiting in it shows as the agent's draft with its answers; "Tell Ida what to answer" sends the member's note to the handling agent as their own message, with the email's subject and sender as quotes of the thread (or leaves it waiting in that agent's thread when something is drafted there or the send can't go). Account mail shows only its site, who used the account and when. Addresses lists each address, who answers it and how much came in the last 7 days; Change asks the Co-Founder to give it to another agent, and New address opens Settings → Company connections. Write asks the chosen agent to write an email from the address it answers. A viewer gets no Send, note, Write or Change. Lines and drafts slide away once answered, and nothing moves with reduced motion. Danish for every new line. The definition's `mail` blocks still draw as before where a definition is drawn directly.
- The Bank page: a third page a company may switch on in Settings → Company → Pages beside Mailbox and Members (migration `20260930520000`, SQL test 688; `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md` "Pages"). The web ships its definition (`app/bank-page.ts`, not the cloud client's library, so the interface format stays as it is) and draws it itself (`app/views/bank.ts`, `styles/bank.css`) from the company's bank summary, `bank/summary.json` in its folder (`qorpera.bank-summary/v1`, `app/bank-summary.ts`, parsed strictly), which the folder reader follows like any page's file. Centred tabs Overview, Money in and Money out with no rule under them, and no boxes. Overview: the Saldo with Disponibel and Reserveret, the saldo over a year or 3 months as a smoothed line with the expected weeks ahead dotted (hover names the day), and money in and out for the last 6 months, green and red, with the difference under each. Money in and Money out: the month by category as a ring and a list, each category opening to its items (how often, next, amount), money out with what repeats every month and costs that come later, and every month; both sections fold away. Put in money explains putting the member's own money in as a loan to the company or as share capital, with the account and the transfer text to copy; it moves no money. Without a summary the page says there is no bank yet; a paused bank is said and the page dimmed. The rail draws Bank with a new solid building emblem. Danish included.
- Agents and group chats outside every section can be put in any order in the rail, by dragging or with Alt and an arrow, where the cloud keeps that order (its sidebar says `unassignedOrder`, migration `20260930519000`). The rail writes Unassigned's order as drawn into `agents/sections.md`, as id lines before the first section: when something is reordered there, moved there from a section, or left there by a deleted section. An item moved into a section leaves that order. An id the file orders that the rail does not show (an agent that is gone) keeps its place after the others. On a cloud before it the rail says, as before, that agents outside a section stay in the order they joined.
- The Members page as C6d leaves it (Interfaces C6d, Members + departure; migration `20260930513000`, SQL test 604; `QORPERA-REBUILD-SCOPE-AMENDMENT-2026-10-17.md` §2). The shipped page draws no part, column or lookup that reads `table:cards`, so its board holds the areas only; the library still seeds the cards table, byte for byte as before. A member who left keeps every cell that names them: their area sits in a lane "{name} (left)" ("Someone who left" where no name is known), and the members who stay take it or offer it as an empty one. An offer a leaver made is not listed. A company whose folder has no `tables/areas/` sees the board empty, "No areas yet.", not a missing table; "Add an area" (a name, and what it is about) adds a row the member owns, and the first one writes `tables/areas/` with the library's `table.toml` and `rows.csv` as they are, reading the folder again when another writer made it meanwhile. An areas table with no `rows.csv` yet draws empty the same way, and its first area writes the rows file's first line, naming the table's columns, before the row. An area's card and detail show who supervises its agents, and the member takes, gives, pauses or lets go of that yes (step I9b's consents, `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md` "Supervision consent"). The detail lists its agents' controls by what the rules allow: Remove for anyone who may change the rows; Add an agent (the hired agents in no area) where no consent on the area is live or it is the member's, else whose it is. A save refused because the agent is in another area by now, or because only the supervisor adds agents now, says so in plain words; any refusal or changed area reads the folder again. Danish for every new line.
- A coding session's thread notes a stop for `MACHINE_NEW_DISK` as "The machine got a new disk" (Danish "Maskinen fik en ny disk"), with the commands it stopped (F3 5g-5 part 3; migration `20260930505000`).
- Settings → Company → Memory: the company's memory choice, "Agent notebooks" (the default) or "Shared context — Beta" (scope reset 17 Oct, C5 PR B; the RPCs `qorpera_company_memory_mode` and `qorpera_set_company_memory_mode` of C5 PR A, migration `20260930508000`; `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md` "Company memory (2) — C5"). Every member sees the two choices; owners and admins make one, everyone else sees them locked with a note. While the company is not offered the beta, Shared context shows as unavailable and can't be chosen; a refused choice goes back and the section reads the company's memory again. The section says that a change applies to new runs only, that a run already started keeps its memory until it ends, and that nothing is deleted or rewritten; it counts the runs that started before the change and still finish in the other memory, and says when the beta was withdrawn, so new runs use Agent notebooks. On a cloud without the RPCs the section stays hidden. Danish included.
- The Mailbox's Sent view asks the threads call for sent threads (`direction`), so it pages through every sent thread, not only the ones among the Inbox's pages, and a thread with more than its newest 50 messages offers "Show older messages" ("Vis ældre beskeder"), which reads the 50 before them, a page at a time (Interfaces C6e; migration `20260930512000`). The thread's 20-second read keeps the older messages read, and when more than a page came since, it reads back to the newest message shown, so none is skipped. Each list's 20-second read reads as many pages as the list shows, so a thread whose newest message went the other way leaves Sent or Inbox on an older page too. A page that comes back after the thread moved on is dropped, and a failed read says "Older messages can't be read." with Try again. A server that names no `nextBefore` shows no button. It replaces the note that only the newest messages are shown.
- An owner or admin can erase a message from the thread for the whole company (summary trees M3e; the member erase route of M3c, `POST`/`GET /v1/member-message-erases/`; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §10). A right-click on a message (or Shift+F10 from a link in it) opens its menu with "Erase message", also on a message an earlier page brought; the thread offers it only to the roles the route takes, and never on a message still on its way. A right-click or a long press on a link, an image or attachment keeps the browser's own menu, and so does a mouse or pen right-click on text selected in the message; the keyboard (Shift+F10 or the menu key, told by the key, not by where the browser places the event) still opens the message's there. On Android a long press on the message's text opens the message's menu, with a Copy in place of the browser's: Chrome selects the word under the finger before the menu comes, and Copy takes what is selected; Firefox would select it only after an untaken menu, so with nothing selected the menu offers "Copy message", the message's whole text (an erased message offers no Copy). A Copy that fails says so above the composer. iOS Safari sends no menu for a long press, so on an iPhone or iPad the menu needs a pointer or a keyboard. On a message already erased the menu offers "Show how the erase went" instead, which reads its erase again without asking (the route answers an erased message with the erase that did it), copies included. Escape, Cancel or a redraw give the keyboard back to the link or the message the menu was opened on (the thread, if the message is gone). After a confirm in the page, a line of its own above the composer says how each erase goes in plain words (an erase started while another is still going gets its own line, and both are read until done; a new line takes the place of those with nothing more to say), naming the message by its author and time: "Erasing…", then "erased from the thread" once the cloud blanked it (the thread reads itself again in full and draws "(erased)"), then "gone from the thread and from the company's memory" when it is done. From the erase's third step on, the line names each file at another path (in this Company File or another of the company's) that still holds a copy of the message, which the erase leaves and the member erases there by its path, and its last word then says the message is gone from the thread and the company's memory "but not from files that hold a copy of it". A refusal, a paused company or a message that can't be erased says so; a 401 (the member's sign-in) gets the "Try again" line, not the role line. The calls carry the member's own token and nothing else (no body), through the member company gateway once it is configured, and an answer counts only for the erase, conversation and message it was asked about. There is no per-member copy: an erase applies to everyone.
- The company folder page opens a link and restores any old version (folder links LK6, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-LINKS.md`; needs LK3's member route). A link's row opens its page, which shows its target and reads no bytes: History, no Download, and never followed. A path's history views a link's version as it views a file's, and every version but the one the path holds now has a Restore button ("Gendan"): a file's version is saved as a `write` of its file reference, a link's as a `symlink` unit, through the member commit route with the version there now as `expectedVersion` (none when the path is free). A folder at the path is left alone and nothing is sent. A conflict shows the history again, a refusal shows its problems, and a lost answer offers Try again, which sends the same change set; it stays through the page's syncs until the restore has an answer, and Restore on the same version sends that change set too. The recent changes list opens a changed link as it opens a file.
- The company folder page shows links and never follows one (folder links LK2; LK1's migration `20260930380000`, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-LINKS.md`). A link's row has a link icon, its name and `→ target` (the whole target in its title), sorts after the folders with the files, and opens nothing. A path's history names a link's write as "Link to …" with its target ("Link til …" in Danish).
- A coding session's thread notes a stop for `MACHINE_STOPPED` as "The machine stopped" (Danish "Maskinen stoppede"), with the commands it stopped (F3 5g-1b; migration `20260930431000`).
- A coding session's thread notes a stop for `FOLDER_RESTART` as "The machine restarted" (Danish "Maskinen genstartede"), with the commands it stopped (F3 5g-5 part 2; migration `20260930501000`).
- Settings shows the last event a Stripe hook refused, when and why (folder core C4, E3c-2; E3c-1's migration `20260930344000` serves it; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-EVENTS.md` §5.8). `parseEventHook` reads `signingSecret.lastRefusal` `{receivedAt, reason}` into `EventHook.lastRefusal`, whole or not at all, and the hook's state line adds "Last refused event {time}: {reason}" (Danish "Seneste afviste hændelse {time}: {reason}"). The reason is the runtime's own English sentence, such as "The Stripe signature does not match".
- An approval card for an agent's save of an interface shows the page as it would look (Interfaces step I10; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §7, §10). While a `save_interface` call waits for an answer, its card shows each page the call saves under "How the page will look", drawn as the Interfaces tab draws it, from the exact call the card covers: from the folder as last read, with each table the save changes read by its new definition (its rows read again by it, so a cell that turns unreadable or readable shows so) and each table it makes empty. It stays live: what it reads is read with the company's pages, even a table no page reads yet, and drawn again when the folder changes (not for each key typed in the thread). Nothing in it can be used, its ids are dropped, and it is cut off at a fixed height and faded there. It goes once the card is answered or the thread closes. A page's mail blocks are not previewed, and a save that changes only tables shows none.
- Members set environment variables for coding in Settings (F3 step 5j-3b; the calls of migration `20260930438000`, PR #1108). My connections lists the member's own, which their coding sessions get in every repository and which leave with them; Company connections lists the company's, between the services and the webhooks, which a coding session gets only in a repository it was given to and which stay when someone leaves. Each card shows the name, what it is for and what changes: "Coding sessions in acme/web get it.", "No coding session gets it yet.", "Your coding sessions get it." or, where a company variable of the same name is given to a repository, "In acme/web, the company's NPM_TOKEN is used instead." Add a variable takes a name (capitals, digits and underscores, from a letter), what it is for and a value; the value goes to the company's broker with a one-time ticket, like a service token, and is never drawn again. The value's field takes several lines, so a pasted key keeps its line breaks (CR and LF as pasted), and Enter in it is a line break: Add and Save value send it. Its rules are checked first: at least 8 bytes (the broker's minimum from 5j-3), at most 32 KiB, and no NUL. A name already used by this owner replaces its value. A replacement that failed once its ticket was issued keeps that ticket, never the value, and the next try for the same variable sends with it while it has more than a minute left, then saves the value once more with a fresh ticket, since the earlier try may still have saved its own with the kept one; when the broker refuses the kept ticket, a fresh ticket tells whether the earlier try saved the value. A new variable asks for a fresh ticket on each try. A replacement the cloud refuses because an earlier try's is still open says "An earlier try to save this value is still open. Try again in a few minutes.", and a failed add reads the list again, since its answer may have been lost. Replace value and Remove act on one variable; Remove and taking a repository back ask first. An owner or admin gives a company variable to a repository (owner/name, stored in lower case). A name kept for the coding machine or a provider's sign-in, and a repository that isn't owner/name, are refused in plain words. Company variables are read-only for contributors and viewers, and a viewer's own are read-only too; the page redraws when the member's role changes. My connections reads its variables again after each change on Company connections, so what a member's own variable says stays true. The secret capture of the services (`createSecretCapture`) is shared by both, with the same requests as before.
- A message a member erased keeps its place in the thread as "(erased)" ("(slettet)" in Danish), in italics (summary trees M3d; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §10; migration `20260930441000`, PR #1093). The cloud blanks an erased message, question, answer or authorization to exactly `{"erased": true}`. A member's input, answer or decision stays theirs; the rest stays the agent's, with its speaker in a group chat. Before, such an event was hidden. A question whose answer was erased shows unanswered, since the answer no longer says which question it answered. The cloud rewrites an erased event in place, under its sequence, and raises its conversation's `memory_generation` (migration `20260928509140`): each full read of a thread (the open thread's at least every 30 seconds) also reads that generation from the session row, and when it differs from the one the thread's events reflect, reads the events it holds again, so a message on screen turns "(erased)" without a reload. A first read that finds a generation above 0 reads them again once, since an erase may have landed between the two reads. A copy read before an erase never replaces the erased one, and a Show earlier that started before a generation change and landed after the events were read again leaves the generation unknown, so the next full read reads them all again.
- An agent's change to the company's interfaces and tables shows in its thread, with Undo (Interfaces step I10; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §7, §9 decision 2; the runtime's notes of migration `20260930370000`, PR #1042). Each note (`qorpera.interface-change/v1`, a `session_info` event) is one centered line in plain words, never its files: "Made the interface “Leads”, changed the table “Clients” and the order of the interfaces", each by its title as the folder has it now (its folder's name when it has none), four by name and the rest counted. A member who may write gets Undo on a change that made folders and wrote files (a delete, move or copy by the agent has none). Undo is the member's own change set through the member commit route, based on the versions the agent's commit left: what it made is deleted (a folder as of the commit, so the core refuses it when anything under it changed since), and a file it rewrote gets its earlier text back. When anything it made changed since, nothing is sent and the line says "Not undone: it changed again since."; pressed again once undone, it sends nothing. A change too large for one change set (16 units, 64 KiB a file, 512 KiB in all), or whose earlier text can't be read, says to ask the agent; a unit the core did not save says why ("Undone in part." when others were). While it is sent the button says "Undoing…" and the keyboard stays on the line; a failed send says "Could not undo. Try again." with Undo back.
- The notices say when an area's supervisor no longer supervises its agents (Interfaces step I9b; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §5.1; migration `20260930371000`, `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md` "Supervision consent"). The member notification `interface_supervision_ended` reads as "{member} stopped supervising the agents in the area {name}." (withdrawn), "… no longer supervises the agents in …" (released), "…: the area is gone." (gone) or "{member} left the company, so the agents in … have no supervisor." (left), in English and Danish. The area is named by its row key, or "an area" when the notice carries none. A notice of this kind with a malformed subject fails the read like any other.
- Members offer cards to each other (Interfaces step I9c; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §3.3, §5; the calls of migration `20260930105000`, `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md` "Offers of a claimed cell"). On a members board whose block lists `offer`, a card no one holds or the member's own has Offer…, which opens the members it may go to: each other member who may change the table's rows (all members, or owners and admins), but those it is offered to already. Picking one offers it, and the focus waits on Offer… until the offer's Withdraw takes it; Cancel or Escape closes the list and returns focus to Offer…. Dragging the card onto that member's lane offers it too. The card then says "Offered to {name}" with Withdraw; to the member it is offered to, "{name} offers it to you" with Accept (where they may take it) and Not now; to everyone else, "{from} offered it to {to}". An offer the cloud refuses says why. Accept saves the member into the card's holder cell while it is as the offer read it, with the same write as Take; a card that changed meanwhile says "That changed while you were looking.". Pulse's "Needs you" lists each offer to the member ("{name} offers you “{row}” in {table}") with Accept and Not now. The offers are read when the company opens, again after each change to the folder the pages read (a save settles them), when the window comes back, and each minute (`qorpera_member_interface_offers`). Until the cloud has the calls, or for a member they don't answer, nothing of it shows.
- The Mailbox page draws (Interfaces step I5c; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §3, §3.2, §3.3, §4, §10). It shows once the company's mail is on, from the member mail read (I5a). Inbox, "Waiting for you" and Sent list the threads 50 at a time, newest first, with Show older, and keep fresh every 20 seconds while on screen. An unread thread is marked until it is opened. An open thread shows each message's From, To, Cc, Subject, attachment names and plain text (never its HTML), with the outside-content mark on what came from outside. Account mail shows as "Account mail" without its text. A send that waits for a person shows its email card, with Send email and Don't send for a member who may write; an answer uses the conversation's model account, as its thread does, and one refused for want of an account links to the agent's thread. "Ask {agent}" and the Addresses view's "Ask to change…" open the agent's or the Co-Founder's thread with the text in the composer, an email's values as quotes (none for account mail). The Mailbox's rail row counts the sends that wait. An edited page keeps the mark in every block: a list's status, a table's cells (the one that opens the thread too), a detail's title and fields, and a button's label are marked where they fill in an email's words. A `mail` block is drawn only from `mail`, and a table from `mail` reads as a list does: it says while it reads or why it can't, and has Show older. A text, numbers or calendar block that names `mail` is not drawn, even beside other sources: the threads are read a page at a time, so a tile or a day would count only those read so far, and neither marks an email's words. A thread's `last_at` and a waiting send's `due` are to the second, as every row's time is, so `since` reads them.
- Interface pages draw the company folder's own sources, live (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §3.2, §4, §10 "Built: folder pages"): `vision`, `agents`, `groups`, `todos`, `routines` (with each routine's next run), `connections`, `contacts`, `human_tasks` and `interfaces`, read with the member's own reads and read again when the folder changes; a file that doesn't parse is listed under its block. The `text` block, and a detail's text, draw markdown with no HTML, links as their text and images as their alt text, and leave out a file's settings block; `numbers` draws tiles with a change, a target and a trend line; `calendar` lists the days ahead from routines, human tasks and tables. Pulse now draws all its blocks but "What happened", whose `activity` source waits for a read of past runs.
- Changes an agent saved that wait for review show in the agent's thread (folder core P40c, migration `20260930102000`, SQL test 937; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C2.md` §8). The agent's own thread reads the member's open proposals of that agent with the thread (`qorpera_member_folder_agent_proposals`, 4 at a time) and shows each as an approval card after the messages: one change as its headline, or "{count} saved changes" with the first 8 and the rest counted, then where it was saved ("From a computer. Not in the Company File yet.") and Discard. Changes are worded as the runtime's card words them ("Hire “Mira” as Bookkeeper", "Delete “Q3 plan” from “Old”"), never as paths. More than 4 adds "More saved changes wait after these." Discard (`qorpera_member_folder_discard_proposal`) says "Discarding…" while it is sent and keeps the keyboard on the card; the card then goes, even from a listing read before the discard landed, and focus moves to the messages. A proposal that is already gone counts as discarded; any other refusal keeps the card and shows why. A routine, a room, a group chat and a sub-session list nothing, and a listing that fails keeps the cards shown last. Applying stays the agent's, through its next run's review.
- The Members page draws (Interfaces step I9a; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §3.2, §3.3, §10). Its board shows the areas and cards in lanes: the member's own ("You"), the other members by name, Shared, the open pile, then "Someone who left". An empty lane keeps its title and says "Nothing here.", and on a phone the lanes stack. An area's card opens its detail. A member who may write the table takes a card no one holds and releases their own, with Take and Release, in any lane (a Shared card too), or by dragging a card between the open pile and their own lane. While a card is dragged, only the lane it may land in is marked. The write changes only that holder cell in `rows.csv` and names the version it read, and the card stays where it is, saving, until the save has landed. A card someone else took meanwhile says "Someone else took it first." (or "That changed while you were looking." for a release), and a refused save lists its problems. Offers came with step I9c (above). The People view lists the members (the new live `members` source: name, picture, role, here) with what each owns and supervises, and how many cards each holds (lookup columns).
- A folder-v2 company's interfaces come from its folder, live (Interfaces step I7; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §10). The Interfaces tab lists what `interfaces/order.md` says, in its order, then the interfaces it doesn't list, by name (§8). Each is read from `interfaces/<name>/interface.toml` and parsed in the browser from its text, never from a stored parse. An entry whose folder or file is missing or can't be read is left out, with no error. A text that doesn't parse is a page that says "This page has a problem." with the parser's problems, line by line, and draws no block or button. The main interface is the one whose text says `main = true`; with none or two, none stands on top, and owners and admins see a quiet line on the tab that says so. A `table:<name>` block reads `tables/<name>/table.toml` and `rows.csv`, shows the rows that fit the definition (agent and member ids as names) and lists the rows that don't; a `file:` source is read unless its text is outside content. The folder's hint asks the feed what changed, at most once a second, with a one-minute poll behind it: only the changed files are read again, `resync` or a change in what the member may read reads everything again, and an open page redraws in place with its open row and scroll position. Until the first read lands, when it fails, or for a folder without `interfaces/order.md`, the tab keeps the library's definitions
- A routine's payer never changes without permission (migration `20260930095000`, PR #827). An open routine card says who pays: "Paid by you / {member} / the company since {date}" (`qorpera_get_routine_payer`, read once per routine after each list read). When that login can't pay, a member who may write (contributors and up) gets "Choose a login": their own logins that can pay for the routine (never a Claude login; a ChatGPT plan only once its "Work while you're away" is on, and never for a file-change or webhook routine), or what to do when none can. Company accounts stay owner and admin only, and the web lists none. Using one gives it a save's consent and binding first, then calls `qorpera_set_routine_login`; a refusal says why in plain words and keeps the choice open, and "Routine is unavailable" (no write access to the routine's file) reads "You can't change who pays for this routine." While the logins are read, Edit, New routine and the switches wait; while an editor opens, the list's controls wait too, the choice's Cancel included. The routines page now has one rule for answers that come back late: the list never draws over an editor that is open or opening, and a step that waited (a switch change, a pick, the editor opening or saving) moves the member or says why it failed only if they have not moved elsewhere since. A failed re-read of who pays keeps what the last read said, so an open choice stays. A login the routine keeps but that can't pay now says why under "Paid by" (from the read's `waitsFor`: "Runs wait until this login's usage limit resets.", or for a new sign-in "Sign in to this login again in Settings → My connections to let it run." to its member and "Runs wait until {member} signs in to this login again." to others), and offers the choice; picking a login at its limit, or one that needs a new sign-in, is refused in those words, and a pick while the company is stopped reads "The company is stopped, so its routines can't change now." Such a routine's switch still resumes it on that login (wording and switch from Interfaces). Turning on the switch of a routine that waits for a login opens its card on "Choose a login" instead of a resume that would only wait again (without the read, the switch resumes as before). The notices line says "{agent}'s routine no longer runs on your login. {member} moved it to their login." (or "to the company's account.") for a `routine_payer_changed` notice, dismissed like any line. Until the cloud has the migration, nothing of it shows, and the web stops asking after the first answer.
- An agent's routines say why a paused routine waits, under its schedule: paused by a member, paused by its agent, "it could not start with the access it had", "no model account pays for it", or "when its agent was archived" (the routine index's `pause_reason`; `agent_archived` arrives with F2's P15 and stays until a member resumes the routine). While the agent is archived, a paused routine's switch is locked with "Reactivate the agent first": a routine never runs under an archived agent.
- A folder-v2 company's Company File opens as the file browser approved for #386, read only until member writes (folder core C4, slice P33; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C4-MEMBERS.md`): a breadcrumb, folders first, a search of the open folder, a preview of text, Markdown and pictures with Download, a file's history (who, when, what) with any earlier version shown in place of the latest, and the company's recent changes on the clock button. The folder's hint asks the feed what changed, at most once a second, with a one-minute poll behind it, and redraws the folder and the open file it touched.
- Let members stop a worker's own run, which has no conversation (session-buildup audit gap 4b, migration `20260930002800`). A `run_no_progress` notice of a worker's run (`worker: true`) offers Stop beside Dismiss to a member who may stop it: an owner, an admin or the member whose work dispatched the worker (`qorpera_get_worker_run_control`, read once per notice). Stop calls `qorpera_cancel_worker_run`, the thread run's stop by run id; once the run has stopped the notice is dismissed, and a failed Stop says "The work could not be stopped. Try again." and keeps the button. The agent that dispatched the worker hears that a member stopped it.
- Say when an agent's run has made no progress for 15 minutes (session-buildup audit gap 4, migration `20260930002600`). The run goes on and nothing stops it. The number is the cloud's (`qorpera_run_stall_minutes()`, sent with each stall), never the web's own. In the agent's thread, "No progress for {minutes} minutes." stands above the composer while `qorpera_list_staff_execution_activity` names the stall (`no_progress_since`, `no_progress_minutes`), with "If it looks stuck, press Stop." where Stop is offered. The Working now rows say "No progress" since the last progress. The company notices line says "{agent} has made no progress for {minutes} minutes." for a `run_no_progress` notice (subject `qorpera.member-notification.run-no-progress/v1`), one per run, dismissed like any line; the cloud closes it itself once the run makes progress or ends. Until the cloud has the migration, nothing shows.
- Interface pages draw more of the fixed blocks (Interfaces step I3a; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §3):
  - **Views** are tabs under the page's head. A view built from another (`from_view`) shows that view's blocks narrowed by its own `where`: rows must match both. A block narrowed by a column its source doesn't have, and a view left with nothing to draw, are left out.
  - **`table`** sorts by its headings (an agent column by the name shown) and filters by its text. The filter keeps focus and the caret while the table redraws, and a letter being composed isn't cut off.
  - **`opens` and `detail`:** a list or table row that opens shows that record's fields and text in its `detail` block, beside the list. On a phone it is the next screen, and focus moves to Back and returns to the row. A row whose target isn't drawn keeps its own link. An open row follows its own item, never another item of the same agent.
  - **Buttons** are drawn only where the member can use them:
    - `answer` appears where a platform card waits for the member, and takes them to its thread.
    - `open` leads to an agent who still works here, or to another interface the member can open.
    - `message` opens the agent's thread with the text in its composer. Nothing is sent until the member sends it. A value from outside content, such as an email's subject, goes in «» on one line (it can't close the marks or start a line of its own), as a quote since I5d-4 (above).
    - Answer and message need a member who can write.
  - Every control keeps keyboard focus through live redraws. The platform's column names and the kinds of what waits are in the member's language.
  - Pulse's "Needs you" cards show their Answer button.
- The rail splits into **Agents | Interfaces** (contract, 2026-10-02 interfaces amendment, item 8; Interfaces steps I1 and I4):
  - **The top row** (rail top option A, Jonas, 2 Oct): the **Agents | Interfaces** tabs fill the row, then a search button and the add button.
    - The tabs are real tabs (`tablist`/`tab`, `aria-selected`, each list a `tabpanel`). The arrow keys move between them, and Home and End pick the first and last. A tab changes the list and leaves the page where it is; the list taking over slides in from its tab's side.
    - A new route picks its tab: an interface's page shows Interfaces, and a conversation or the Co-Founder shows Agents. The choice is kept for the browser tab (`sessionStorage`).
    - **Search** folds out of its button, leftward over the tabs. Its placeholder says what it searches ("Search agents" or "Search interfaces"). Esc or its ✕ folds it back, clears it and returns focus to the button. An empty field also folds back when focus leaves it, and text keeps it open. Without the tabs, the field keeps the row open.
    - The collapsed rail stacks the tabs as icons above the add button. The add button stays on both tabs.
    - Motion uses the motion system's tokens (`styles/motion.css`, `ui/motion.ts`): the thumb springs across, the field widens with a spring and its text box fades in after it, and the tabs fade and shrink away while it is open.
  - **The Interfaces tab** lists the interfaces this client can draw today. Their definitions come from `@qorpera/cloud-client/interface-library` until a company keeps its own files (I7). An interface is listed only when its `needs` hold and at least one of its blocks has a source the client reads. Today that is Pulse, drawn as the big card on top with its emblem. Search filters the list by name.
  - **The page.** `#/c/{id}/i/{name}` draws an interface from the fixed blocks. Today that is the `list` block over two sources from the company's live state:
    - **Needs you:** agents waiting on a decision, runs that did not finish, unread mentions;
    - **Working now:** agents working or writing, with writing dots.

    Blocks without a source are not drawn. A part-width block left alone takes the whole row. An unchanged page keeps its elements.
  - The emblems are drawn from fixed shapes in the interface's agent colour (`ui/interface-emblem.ts`; `colourNameHex` in `ui/avatar.ts`).
- The app moves. One set of timings and springs (`--motion-*`, `--ease-*` in tokens.css; styles/motion.css, ui/motion.ts) runs every animation, and all of it stops under reduced motion. Your message rises out of the composer; an agent's reply arrives whole and opens bubble by bubble from the corner where its working figure stood; a conversation fades up as it opens. In the rail the blue highlight slides to the conversation you open, taking each row's shape; rows glide to new places (a drop, a search, room for a new row); an agent that starts work gets its dot and its line; a mention's badge pops in; a new hire's row is placed like a card (it slides in lifted, lands and settles into the rail, and the name is written in); a new chat drops in. Menus, dialogs and Settings open out of what opened them and leave quicker; the agent's computer opens out of its header button, zooms between preview and full size and closes back into it; an answered card folds to its outcome while the old card fades over it, and its check draws in. A new theme, highlight colour or size spreads over the page as a circle from where it was chosen. Buttons and rows give under a press.
- A usage limit on a member's own ChatGPT plan is told in OpenAI's words with "Manage usage" (Sign in with ChatGPT switch-on gate 2b; OpenAI's UI guidelines): the failed-run card above the composer reads "ChatGPT · Usage limit reached. Review your plan or this app's limit in ChatGPT settings." with a "Manage usage" link to `https://chatgpt.com/settings/usage` (new tab) and Dismiss, and a coding session's failed turn says the same, with the link, to its owner. It needs the cloud's `paid_by_chatgpt_plan` on the run recovery and `usageLimit`/`paidByChatgptPlan` on a coding turn's failed event (migration `20260928379930`); without them, nothing changes. Anyone else, and a Codex login, read the plain sentence. A coding turn the cloud says stopped at a usage limit now reads as one, never as "Something on Qorpera's side refused one of the session's steps".
- A ChatGPT plan's “Work while you're away” switch that is busy says so: "Busy right now. Try again in a moment.", in Settings (and the founding table, which shows the same accounts) and on the first-routine card, instead of "could not be changed" or "could not be turned on" (Sign in with ChatGPT). The kernel answers 55P03 when the switch waited too long for a lock or lost a deadlock (migration `20260928369985`); nothing changed, and a second press works.
- A routine that started again when its member turned on “Work while you're away” says so on its card: "Resumed: When Anna turned on “Work while you're away” · <time>" (Sign in with ChatGPT; the routine list's `resume_reason`, `resumed_at` and `resumed_by_user_id` from migration `20260928369985`; nothing shows without them). The first-routine card's Turn on reads the routines again after the switch: when the kernel has started them (20260928369985) it says nothing more and saves no routine (it still acknowledges the notices), an owner or admin still starts any routine that waits, and anyone else is told to ask the agent only about a routine that still waits. It never says a routine started that did not, and after a failure it says only what is known: the switch failed, the routines could not be checked, or these could not start.
- "Continue with ChatGPT" in Settings → My connections → Add account, shown only while the company may use ChatGPT plan logins (`qorpera_chatgpt_plan_available`, asked when Add account opens; Sign in with ChatGPT slice 5b-3) and not beside a signed-in plan. The founding table does not offer it. It begins a plan sign-in for the member's own identity (`qorpera_begin_chatgpt_plan_sign_in`) and sends the browser to OpenAI. This tab keeps the sign-in (the member, the page it began on, and a capability) in session storage for at most 15 minutes. When OpenAI's callback sends the member back (`#?chatgpt_plan=confirm&confirmation=…`), the page goes back to where the sign-in began and opens My connections. The confirm (`…/chatgpt-plan-confirm`, with the capability, dropped first) belongs to the member, not to the page: it finishes while Settings closes or the route changes, and My connections shows the outcome once. Plain words for each outcome: connected, a second plan ("Disconnect your other ChatGPT plan first."), switched off, cancelled, too slow (also a return after 15 minutes), too many sign-ins, or not finished. A sign-in another account started in the tab is never finished.
- A ChatGPT plan's member is asked once to turn on “Work while you're away” (Sign in with ChatGPT slice 5b-2; founder decision 2026-10-01; shown only while plans are on, to a member whose plan is not turned on). When an agent's routine for the member waits for their account (notice `routine_needs_model_account`; the routine list's `pause_reason` and `runs_for_user_id`), the notice line above the composer says "Morning brief can run while you're away. Turn on “Work while you're away” so your ChatGPT plan can run it." with Turn on and Not now. Showing it on a page the member can see records it (`qorpera_record_chatgpt_plan_routine_offer`), so it never comes back. Turn on turns the plan on; an owner or admin's waiting routines start on it again, and anyone else is told to ask the agent to set the routine up again (saving a routine is for owners and admins). Not now leaves the routines waiting. The plan's switch, in Settings and on the card, now goes through `qorpera_set_chatgpt_plan_work_while_away` (the kernel guard, migration `20260928369980`). A suspended routine's notice is still skipped.
- A ChatGPT plan login keeps its own rules in the web (Sign in with ChatGPT slice 5b-1; shown only for a member who has a plan login, which needs the platform switch and the reads of migration `20260928369970`). Settings → My connections names it "ChatGPT plan" and gives it a "Work while you're away" switch, off until the member turns it on (`qorpera_set_chatgpt_plan_work_while_away` since slice 5b-2, and on: the consented version bound). Connecting a plan binds it for the member's own messages only: the web never consents a plan for work while away, not in Settings and not when a routine is saved. The routine editor names a plan "ChatGPT plan", lists a plan without work while away last, says to turn it on in Settings first, and keeps file-change and webhook routines off a plan. Codex logins behave as before. Pausing or resuming a routine falls back to an account that can pay for it. The founding table, which shows the same model accounts, shows the switch too.
- A routine that listens to one of the company's hooks shows "When a hook receives an event" (or the event types it takes) on its card, in English and Danish. It can be paused and resumed; the routine editor does not edit it. Before, such a routine would have been left out of the list as unreadable.
- Plan & usage shows the company's computer hours left once payments are live (`hoursLeft` in `qorpera_read_company_computer_hours`, migration `20260928259330_company_hours_left_read`): "Company A · 25 h left" instead of "Measured, not charged yet", and what they come from ("Free hours 2 h · Plans 5 h · Packs 20 h", with "Owed 1 h" once past zero). A founder also sees each founder's share ("Anna: packs 20 h", "Bo: plan 5 h", "Former founders: packs 2 h", with "A founder's plan is shared by every company it pays for."); other members see the numbers, never who paid. Amounts under three minutes are left out. A complimentary company reads "Complimentary: no limit". Before the live switch the card is unchanged.
- Say when a company is out of computer hours (the zero rule; switched off until live payments). The company's hours card in Settings → Plan & usage says "Out of computer hours. New work waits and starts by itself once hours are added." while the cloud reports `waitingForHours` in `qorpera_read_company_computer_hours`. A run the cloud ended because the company ran out (`[code=COMPUTER_HOURS_EXHAUSTED]`, an hour after it ran out) shows "The company is out of computer hours. New work starts again once hours are added." on its card, with Dismiss only, instead of an internal error. In agent threads and group chats, the same sentence stands above the composer while the company waits, read with the company's other periodic reads from `qorpera_company_waiting_for_hours` (until the cloud has it, nothing shows); what is sent is sent and shown as usual, and starts by itself once hours are added. Showing that work waits in a routine, a coding session or on a computer comes in a later change. Until the rule is on, nothing changes.
- Buy computer hours in Settings → Plan & usage (and on the account page) once payments open (migration `20260928248910_runtime_computer_hours_payments`, Edge Function `computer-billing`). A plan on sale says "Choose Basis" or "Choose Standard" and opens Stripe's checkout page; a member who pays switches plan ("Switch to Standard"), changes the card and finds invoices on Stripe's billing page ("Payments and invoices"). Plan prices and hours come from the cloud's catalog, which is what Stripe charges (qorpera.com's prices until the cloud has one). The plan card then shows the paid plan, when it renews or ends, that it is paused, or that its payment failed; the hours card shows the plan's hours used of the period's hours, by company. Founders get "Buy hours" on the company's hours card: packs of 20, 100 or 500 hours for the company, valid for 12 months. Stripe sends the member back to the account page, which thanks them and reads the plan again a moment later; the note goes when they leave the page. Card data never passes through the app, and only Stripe's own checkout and billing pages are opened. Before live payments only test accounts an operator enrolled see anything for sale, marked "Test mode: nothing is charged", and a plan's hours stay hidden, since members see only measured hours until then. Members who are not founders now read "Computer hours are paid by the founders. You are never charged." on the company's hours card.
- Settings → Plan & usage shows the open company's computer hours this month, beside its storage: the hours measured so far (one computer hour is an hour of a standard machine of 4 vCPU and 8 GB), "Measured, not charged yet", and what they went to (company machines, agent runs, coding machines, agents' machines). It reads `qorpera_read_company_computer_hours` (migration `20260928248000_runtime_computer_hours_meter`); until the cloud has it the card says "Not measured yet", and a failed lookup says "Computer hours unavailable" without asking again. The plan's own card is now "Computer hours in your plan" and reads "Counted once plans open", and Basis offers "74 computer hours a month" instead of "a worker computer for every agent".
- A coding session's status line says where it is between turns: standing by, running N commands, or asleep; the owner can archive it (the Archive pill beside the status), and a message reopens it. Archived, reopened and "the machine went to sleep" show as notes in the session's thread with how many commands were stopped. An archived session no longer counts as going in the thread's header. Until the cloud has the standby migration, sessions show as before and Archive is not offered.
- Group chats work like agent chats (migration `20260928030000_company_sidebar_group_chats`). Right-click a group chat in the rail for the same menu as an agent: Pin, Move to (a section, Unassigned or a new section), Rename, Copy conversation ID, Hide from sidebar and Delete. Drag it to reorder it, move it to another section or pin it; pinned group chats stand as square cards with their figures beside the pinned agents, and hidden ones fold into "Hidden" (renamed from "Hidden agents"). Group chats and agents share one order in each section; placement is the company's, pins and hidden are yours, as for agents. Rename and Delete are for owners and admins, as every group change is; Delete removes the group chat for everyone after a confirmation. The composer takes images in group chats (up to 5, 8 MiB in all, as in an agent's thread), and ··· → Model sets the model, effort and speed (Fast, on by default) for every agent in the group chat, from the next message (until one is chosen it reads "Each agent's own": each agent runs its own model). A post into a room, an agent's thread included, needs a message: images without text keep Send greyed out ("Add a message to send with the images.") instead of uploading and failing. Until the cloud has the migration, a group chat's menu offers Rename, Copy conversation ID and Delete, the group chat stays after the Unassigned agents, and images stay off.
- Mention people and agents with "@". Typing "@" in a conversation lists who can be named: in a group chat its agents (with their figures) and its other members, in an agent's thread the other members. Arrows choose, Enter or Tab puts the name in, Escape closes. In a group chat, naming agents means only they answer, and the line under the composer says so ("Only Bo will answer"); a named agent always answers, and a message that names no agent reaches every agent as before. A member named sees "@" on that conversation in the rail until they open it. Names in messages stand out, your own most of all. The cloud reads the names from the text itself (migration `20260928010000_runtime_room_mentions`), so older browsers and the agents themselves need nothing new. Until the cloud has it, "@" lists nobody.
- See who is here. The faces of the other members who have a conversation open right now stand in its header, before the computer button: up to three, then "+2" for the rest, with their names on hover ("Sofie Lund and Mads Holm are here"). A face grows in when someone opens the conversation and goes when they leave, switch conversations or hide the tab. It runs on the company's private presence channel: each tab registers a device key and signs what it announces, and names come from the company's member list, so nobody can appear as someone else. Nothing is stored. A company with one member joins nothing.
- See what teammates are writing and write it with them. In an agent thread or a group chat, another member's unsent message shows as a notch with their picture right above the composer; its dots move while they type. Point at it and the notch opens up in place to show the message as it is written; click it (tap on a phone) to open it above the composer and write in it. Both see each other's words as they are typed, and words typed at the same moment are merged, never lost. Only the member who started the message can send it, as their own message on their own account; the open draft has no Send and says so. The owner's composer names who is writing in it with them, and words a teammate adds while the message is being sent stay for the next one. A reloaded browser gets its own unsent message back. The cloud holds each draft under one revision and pushes changes on a private channel only the room's members receive (migration `20260928000500_room_shared_drafts`). Nothing is shared in a company with one member, or until the cloud has the migration.
- Call a skill from the composer: type "/" and a menu above the message field lists the skills of this conversation, the agent's own first (labelled with its name), then the company's, each with its description. Keep typing to narrow it, move with the arrow keys, pick with Enter, Tab or a click, close with Escape. The picked "/skill-name" shows in blue, as does any name of a known skill typed by hand or restored with a draft, and in written messages: blue in an agent's or another member's bubble, a light chip in the member's own bubble (whatever its colour), never inside code or links; the agent loads that skill before acting on the message (runtime skills, contract 15.2). Skills are read with the member's own Company File access from `skills/` and `agents/<agent>/skills/`, parsed by the same SKILL.md reader the agents use, when "/" is first typed and again after a minute. A group chat offers the company's skills. The field stays a plain text box: while it names a skill, a copy of the text with the names in blue lies over its transparent characters.
- Agent threads run Fast by default, and the composer's ··· → Model menu has a Speed section with a Fast switch that turns it off for that one thread (migration `20260927161500_thread_fast_mode`; the runtime release that declares fast mode). Fast is the provider's priority tier: about 1.5x speed on GPT-6 Sol and Luna, 2x on GPT-6 Astra, which uses more of the plan; the switch's caption says which. The menu row reads, for example, "GPT-6 Sol · Low · Fast". Routines and other threads keep their own speed. Until the cloud runs threads on the Fast tier, the Speed section stays hidden. The Model panel may now grow taller so the models, effort and speed fit without scrolling.
- Rules and approval cards for agents' actions (runtime bundle v130, Grok Bot's Auto-review; the rules now sit at the bottom of Settings → General). A rule is one sentence: "When an agent wants to …, it should: Allow automatically | Ask first". Every member writes rules for themselves; owners and admins also write the company's rules, which everyone else sees locked. Before an agent acts outside the company (sends, pays, deletes, hires, sets up a routine, clicks or types on its computer), it is checked against them; Ask first wins when rules conflict, and built-in safety checks always apply. When an action needs approval, a card in the thread shows what the agent wants to do, why it waits and the exact action: the member it waits for (a personal rule's owner, or any owner or admin for a company rule or a built-in check) answers Allow once or Deny, and on a built-in check's card Always allow, which saves the proposed rule (editable first) for the company. Everyone else sees who it waits for. Until the cloud has v130, the rules section says so and no card offers controls.
- Fit a new profile picture to the circle before it is saved: after picking a photo, a dialog shows it behind the circle others will see. Drag it (or use the arrow keys) to move it, and zoom with the slider, the mouse wheel, a pinch or + and −; the photo always fills the circle. Save uploads that square, Cancel changes nothing. Before, the picture was always the photo's centre square.
- Hidden agents fold at the foot of the rail as "Hidden agents" with their count, as Grok Bot's "Hidden Bots" do: open it to list them, then Show in sidebar, Pin, Move to a section, or drag one out to show it again where it lands. The open or folded choice is remembered in this browser; the collapsed rail leaves the group out.
- A profile picture for every account. Settings → General shows it at the head of the Account card: click it to pick a photo, and once there is one the same click opens a small menu to pick another or go back to initials. The photo is cut to its centre square and shrunk to 256 px in the browser, so only a small WebP (JPEG where the browser cannot write WebP) is uploaded. The picture replaces the initials on the rail's account button, in Settings → Company's member list, in Friends and in group-chat member lists, for everyone who shares a company with the member, their friends, and people they sent a friend request to (a request someone else sent does not reveal the picture until it is accepted). Pictures are private storage objects the cloud only hands to those people, and only the current one (migration `20260924150000_member_pictures`); an account's folder holds four objects at most, and uploads that never became the picture are cleaned up by the owner's browser; the cloud refuses to delete the current picture. The page shows pictures through `blob:` URLs, so its image policy is unchanged. Where the cloud has no member pictures yet, the disc stays initials with nothing to click.
- Choose the model and the thinking effort for one agent thread in the composer's ··· → Model menu: GPT-6 Astra, GPT-6 Sol or GPT-6 Luna (GPT-5.6 Sol and Terra are gone; a thread that used them goes back to the agent's default model) and Low, Medium or High effort (runtime bundle v91). The menu row shows both, for example "GPT-6 Sol · High". The choice belongs to that thread only and applies from the next message; routines and other threads keep their own. Choosing only an effort keeps the thread on its default model. Until the cloud honours per-thread effort, the Effort group stays hidden and only GPT-6 Astra can be chosen.
- The whole web app is offered in English and Danish. Choose the language in Settings → General (Follow system uses the browser's language); the screens redraw in the new language at once, a message being written and chosen images stay, and Settings stays open on the same tab. Every screen is translated: the agent list, conversations and the composer, cards and failed runs, new conversation, agent and group details, routines, coding sessions, Settings, sign-in, the Company File (also the live browse, edit and upload panel), organization and the computer screen's own controls. Names, messages, file names and server error text stay as written. Danish follows the wording on qorpera.com ("Virksomhedsfilen", "Co-Founder"). The tests fail when interface text has no Danish or English is left unwrapped where interface text goes.
- Settings → General → Language & region shows Language and a new Time zone as small buttons in one card, as the desktop reference does. Each opens a list with a search field and a check on the current choice: languages by their own name with the name in the interface language beside it, time zones by city with today's offset beside it (a zone is also found by region or by its name, such as "Central European Time"). Both start at Follow system. A chosen zone sets the clock for message times, the day breaks in a thread and the times in lists and cards, and new routines start in it; the screens behind Settings redraw at once and keep a message being written. Like the language, it is kept in this browser.
- Right-click an agent in the rail for Grok Bot's menu: Pin, Move to (the company's sections, Unassigned, New section), Rename, Copy conversation ID, Hide from sidebar and Delete (runtime bundle v90). Pinned agents stand as square cards right under the Co-Founder card; the company's sections follow, then Unassigned with the group chats. Right-click a section's title to rename or delete it, or an empty spot in the rail for a new section. Drag an agent to reorder it, move it to another section, or pin and unpin it; the place it will land lights up, with no border, and a thin line marks the spot. In the collapsed rail, where the titles hide, a short line stands between groups of agents (the pinned ones, each section, Unassigned); none under the Co-Founder, none around an empty section. Sections are the company's and agents move each other between them too; pins and hidden agents are yours. A touch screen opens the menu with a long press. Before the cloud has the sidebar, the rail stays one list and the menu offers Rename, Copy conversation ID and Delete.
- Connect Gmail, Google Drive and GitHub for the company in Settings → Connections → Services (runtime bundle v87). A connection belongs to the company: owners and admins connect it by signing in with the service (or, for GitHub, a pasted token) or disconnect it, every agent in the company can use it from its next run, new hires included, and the card names who connected it. A sign-in finishes only in the browser tab that started it, so a consent link passed to someone else connects nothing, and a crafted return link does nothing. Other members see the services read-only. The sign-in goes to the company's secret broker behind the shared broker origin (`?company=` routes it); no token reaches the web app or the database.
- Cards an agent raises in its thread (kernel bundle v70, runtime v71): a question with options, a computer takeover with Take over, I'm done and Skip, and the approval card's Approve and Decline now record the decision. A card shows who answered and what, stays in the thread once answered, and expires on its own time. Form and draft cards show their content and can be declined or discarded; filling a form waits for the vault fill step. A secret card takes the value in a masked field and saves it into the company's cloud vault straight from the browser (owners and admins for the company, other members for themselves; a saved secret with the same name is replaced), then marks the card provided; the agent only learns that it was saved. Group chats never carry cards.
- A coding session's thread notes when its agent was asked whether the quiet session can be archived ("Quiet for 3 days · asked Mark whether it can be archived"); nothing shows until the cloud asks.
- Start a company together at a founding table. Once the cloud creates new companies on the company folder (`qorpera_company_founding_available`), "New company" opens a table with your seat instead of creating the company at once; until then, and while the cloud has not said, the hub is unchanged. Friends take seats through the Invite Co-founder tile (your friends list, with Add a friend), write what they will do, connect their own ChatGPT account and press Ready; a change to the name, description, a role or who sits there clears everyone's Ready. One Start makes everyone at the table an owner, and the page shows who owns the company until it can be opened. The hub lists your tables and invitations to tables. There is no billing at the table (migration `20260928248200_company_founding_lobby`).
- Settings → Model accounts shows a login used in all the member's companies (account model logins, migration `20260930548000`): its card says "Used in all your companies", its Disconnect reads "Stop using here" (it stops using the login in this company only), and "Sign out everywhere" ends it in every company (`listAccountLogins`, `signOutEverywhere`; Danish texts too).
- Settings → **Sites** (Danish "Hjemmesider"), a company tab: the password-protected websites the company's agents published at `https://sites.qorpera.com/<slug>/` (`app/views/settings-sites.ts`, `cloud/sites.ts`; `qorpera_list_company_sites`, `qorpera_company_site_password`, `qorpera_set_company_site_password`, `qorpera_unpublish_company_site`, migration `20260930550000`). Every member sees each site's title, its link (open or copy), whether it is live or still publishing, when and by which agent it was last published, its files and size, and the Company File path it came from. An owner or admin also shows a site's password (to copy, then hide), changes it (one typed, 10 to 128 characters, or a new random one; every visitor then enters the new one) and takes a site offline after confirming (its link, files, password and visitors' sessions go; its source stays in the Company File). Other members read the list only. A role lost while the page is open clears any password shown.
- Settings → Sites shows a private app link (v195, `share_app`; `qorpera_list_company_sites` rows now carry `kind`, `machine` and `port`) as an app: its link on its own host (`https://<slug>-preview.qorpera.com/`), "App on machine <name>, port <port>" (Danish "App på maskinen <name>, port <port>"; "App on port <port> of a machine that was deleted" when its machine is gone) in place of its source, no file count, and its state Live or Off (not shared now, or its machine is gone). Show, copy and change password, and Unpublish, work as for a website; Unpublish says the app keeps running on its machine. `parseCompanySite` checks each kind's link, machine and port.
- "Download the code" in Storage (own code storage, step 2b; migration `20260930720000`). On the folder of a repository the company keeps in Qorpera (`code/<owner>/<name>` and below), an owner or admin has a button in the folder's top line, drawn as the selects beside it. Its menu, the app's own, offers "The files" ("Everything as it is now, in one file") and "With full history" ("For a developer"). Qorpera then makes the file in the repository's `downloads` folder; one line under the top line says "Getting the code ready…" while the web asks every 2 seconds, for at most 10 minutes, then "Downloading the code…" with how far a large file is, and "Downloading <name>." once the browser has it. Each other ending is one plain line: too large (over 200 MB), the agents working on the code, too many downloads today, not ready yet, could not be made ready, could not be downloaded. Cancel stops the wait; the same press finds the file again. None of the words is git's (`app/views/code-download.ts`, `cloud/code-downloads.ts`: `qorpera_list_code_repositories`, `qorpera_request_code_download`, `qorpera_code_download`). The button shows only where the cloud has the calls and says the member may download: a cloud from before the migration (`PGRST202`) shows none, so the web can ship first.
- A file in Storage downloads up to 256 MiB, a folder file's most (was 8 MiB). It is read piece by piece (`readFilePieces`) and handed to the browser as one `Blob` of the pieces (`offerPieces`); a file of more than two pieces says how far it is ("Getting <name>… 40%").

### Changed
- An agent's wall sits right above the composer as one small centred button ("1 waits on you · 1 working · 2 just done") that opens upward and pushes the chat up: flat rows with no card, done first, then working, and nearest the composer what waits on the member with an Answer button. It stays closed until the member opens it.
- Agent figures have crisp edges and stronger form (Jonas, 20 Oct: after #1317 they looked "more blurry and less striking"). The source renders' silhouettes fade over about 6 px at 512, so `avatar-appearance.js` steepens their coverage 2.5 times around its middle; `figure-motion.ts` no longer feathers the silhouette by half a pixel; and the rail's Co-Founder loses its drop-shadow halo (light and dark themes), keeping the ground shadow under it. The tone ramp gives light and shade more room (0.16 lightness per stop of shade, 0.12 per stop of light, up to 0.8 stop above the median, no longer scaled by 0.8), so a muted color such as Gray shows its form and Gold its highlight.
- Company storage opens inside Settings, with a Settings / Storage switch and one borderless search header above both panels. Documents and Recents share the storage navigation; Documents offers file-type icons, icon and detailed list views, type filters, and sorting by name, modification date, size or kind; a file's preview opens beside the files when there is room and in their place when there is not. Recents groups changes by day, and the storage column shows how much the company uses. Dates and sizes come from the folder, and partial listings say how many items are loaded. Existing file and link opening, downloads, version history, restoration and live updates remain available.
- A company shows two pages at most, Mailbox and Members, and only once an owner or admin switches them on (Interfaces C6a; `QORPERA-REBUILD-SCOPE-AMENDMENT-2026-10-17.md` §4; migration `20260930509000`, `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md` "Pages"). Settings → Company has a Pages group with a switch for each, off at first; other members see the switches locked, and a switch that fails says "{page} could not be changed. Try again." The Interfaces tab lists the pages that are on, drawn from the definitions the web ships, never from the folder's `interfaces/`: Pulse, a page an agent wrote and a folder file named `mailbox` or `members` are not shown. A link to any other page name opens "This page isn't available here yet.", and a button to one is not drawn. Switching a page off only hides it; its tables, offers and consents stay. The pages are read when the company opens (`qorpera_member_interface_pages`), on focus, each minute while it shows and right after a switch, and the folder is read only for the pages that are on. Gone with it: the order of `interfaces/order.md`, the "main interface" note, the "This page has a problem." page, the `interfaces` folder source, the approval card's page preview (step I10's "How the page will look"; in git history) and Undo of an agent's interface change (parked: with C6b and C6c agents make no pages, and the save check refuses their writes there). A cloud without the calls shows no pages.
- The offers reader keeps an offer's `agent` and leaves agents' offers to an area's supervisor out of what pages draw (Interfaces step I9b; migration `20260930372000`, `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md` "Supervision consent"). `MemberInterfaceOffer.agent` is null for a cell's offer. The Members board shows agents' offers when it learns Consent and Withdraw.
- Messages with coworkers in a row stand on one line in an agent's thread (Jonas, 5 Oct: "batched visuals"). Two or more `qorpera.peer-link/v1` lines with nothing between them read "Messaged (figure) Growth and (figure) Operations", "Messages from …", "Talked with …" when they go both ways, or "Left notes for …" / "Notes from …" when every one was held. Each coworker has one chip, in the language's own list order, that opens the two agents' view-only transcript as before; from three coworkers on, a chip is the figure alone, with the name in its label and title. "· N messages" follows when there are more messages than coworkers. A message, a coding session, a time separator or the start of the live log ends the line, and the next separator is measured from the line's last message. A single message keeps its own line. When a line takes in another message, a coworker new to it springs in and the count ticks over; a keyboard user on one of its chips keeps it, and closing a transcript returns to the chip that opened it.
- A conversation keeps its model account at its usage limit (founder decision 2026-10-04: the payer never changes without the member; migration `20260930059000`). Without the cloud's choice the web no longer moves a conversation to the member's account with room left either; it follows only the conversation's own binding (after the member signs in again); an account an owner or admin disabled stays.
