When the member disconnected the conversation's account, the composer says "Choose a login to continue." with a "Use {account}" button for each of their logins that can pay now; the pick is the member's own (`qorpera_choose_member_session_account`), and nothing moves the conversation before it. A kept account that cannot pay right now (disabled, needing a new sign-in, its computer recovering) is named as "This conversation's model account can't be used right now. Check it in Settings → My connections." While the conversation's account is at its limit, the composer says "This conversation's model account has reached its usage limit. Sending resumes when it resets." (Danish too); "Your model accounts have reached their usage limits." stays for a new conversation whose accounts are all at their limits.
- The Co-Founder is drawn by the same colour rule as every agent. Founding seeds its file with no colour (P15, #789), which draws it in its figure's own shade. The old rule drew a Co-Founder stored as `qorpera-blue` in its own shade, but on the company folder that is what a member who picks Blue gets, so the pick never showed. The web also no longer reads the sponsor hand-off notice (`sponsor_handoff`), which the cloud stopped writing when a run that reaches its login's usage limit began to stop on that login.
- The hub creates companies only at a founding table (lobby hardening, from #776's review). "New company" asks the cloud whether tables are on at every press, never from an older read; while they are off it says creating a company is paused, and the old "create at once" path (`qorpera_create_cloud_company`) is gone. A founding table that could not be set up says so instead of "Preparing" forever, and a company whose Co-Founder could not start on its own (no ChatGPT account could pay for the first run, or the run failed) says how to start it: connect an account and write to the Co-Founder in its thread.

- An agent's routines work on the company's folder again (folder core C4, slice P34r; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C4-MEMBERS.md` §6): a routine is its file `agents/<handle>/routines/<name>.md`, and New routine, Edit and the switch write that file as the member instead of the old save every folder-v2 company refused. A new routine's file takes its name from the title (made with the routines folder when the agent has none), with "always reply" and low effort written out. An edit changes only what the member changed and keeps the rest of the file (effort, end, runs, quiet hours, notes); with nothing changed it writes nothing and says "Nothing changed."; when someone else changed what the form shows meanwhile, it says "This routine changed while you were editing it." and opens the form again on the file. A save whose answer never arrived is sent again as it was on the next Save; when that save had made a new routine and someone changed it meanwhile, the form opens on the routine's file with the same message, and the next Save edits it. A routine's zone is its file's, also when an interval becomes days at a time, and its quiet hours keep theirs; the instructions may be as long as the file allows. Pause writes `status: paused` and needs no account; a pause or resume the folder refused says why. Resume, a new routine and an edit first switch on and bind the member's account that pays, as before; the editor says the routine runs on the member's own account that works while they're away, and shows the account picker only when none is switched on (never a Claude login). The editor offers every 5 minutes to 7 days or chosen days at a time; it no longer offers "when something in the Company File changes". A routine whose file the member cannot find (out of their reach, or a cloud that does not list routines' files yet) shows no switch and no Edit.
- A build given no cloud configuration (development) now talks to the live cloud the release build uses: project `phqlfrkcvstmxlebljcj`, its publishable key and the node's object gateway. The default still named the old project `wovsmjvghnbcbfazdtvr`, whose key every call refused. Release builds name their own configuration, as before.
- Agents, group chats and the composer's skill suggestions work on the company's folder (folder core C4, slice P34a-2; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C4-MEMBERS.md` §5). Since every company is folder-v2 they refused with "no governed File interface". Creating an agent makes its folder `agents/<handle>` with `agent.md` and its `memory/`, `skills/`, `routines/` and `todos/` folders; editing one rewrites `agent.md`, changing only the fields the member changed (a figure in its own shade is written with no colour, so it follows its figure's shade); deleting one removes its folder (never the Co-Founder's). Group chats are `agents/groups/<handle>` with `group.md`, made, edited and deleted the same way. Each change goes through the object gateway's member commit route as the member; a change that something else made meanwhile is read again and retried. A refused new agent or group chat is a new one on the next try, since the core keeps a refused change set's answer under its key. Skill suggestions list `skills/` and the agent's own `skills/` folder. A web-made agent gets its first run with P15; until then it starts when first messaged. The Company File's governed client, its mode check and the agent and room JSON documents are gone from the web.

- The Company File page is the company's folder, and only that: every company is folder-v2 (founder decision, 3 October 2026). The page opens the folder without asking the cloud which kind of company it is, and offers "Retry opening Company File" when the folder can't be opened. The governed page, its snapshot and proposal pages, and the switch between the two pages are gone. Old links to `#/c/{id}/snapshot/…` and `#/c/{id}/proposal/…` open the company home, and `#/c/{id}/file/history` opens the Company File. Review on a failed run opens the Company File.
- A failed run shows a red "!" in the rail in place of the agent's figure (rail rows, pinned cards and the Co-Founder card), instead of a red disc on the figure. The figure folds into it as the run fails and comes back once the failure is dismissed or work starts again; the link's name still says "A run did not finish". In forced colours the "!" is drawn in the text colour.
- A run stopped by a usage limit now says so in the thread, from the run's recovery read (`usage_limit`) rather than the saved error text: "The model account paying for this work reached its usage limit, so the work stopped. It does not move to another account. It can run again once the limit resets." (Danish too.) When the cloud answers the flag, it decides; only an older cloud's error text still counts, and a rate limit is never shown as a usage limit.
- Settings splits Connections into two tabs. My connections holds the member's own AI subscriptions (ChatGPT / Codex, Claude): they pay for the work that member starts, including their routines, and leave with them. Company connections holds the company's own accounts (Gmail, Google Drive, GitHub): every agent can use them and they stay when someone leaves. Each tab says that rule under its title and reads only its own list. Members who are not founders or admins see "Only founders and admins can connect or disconnect them." on Company connections, and a service sign-in comes back to that tab. The hints that sent members to Settings → Connections now say Settings → My connections. The Settings menu is 358px wide (252px in windows up to 1000px) so the Danish "Virksomhedens forbindelser" fits on one line.
- The agent's computer panel follows the machine its computer is on (a company can run several machines, each hosting up to six computers), names the machine by number once there is more than one, says why a starting machine is not up yet (being set up, or waiting for room on the servers) and moves when the agent gets room elsewhere. Panel copy uses the new words: an agent's computer (screen) runs on a company machine. Without the backend lookup it uses the company's one machine as before.
- An agent's approval card leads with what it wants to do and the change itself: who it reaches, what it is called and what it says. An email to send is shown as the thread's own email card, read-only (To, Cc, Subject and the whole text, read from the exact call), with Send email and Don't send. A Company File deletion reads "Delete “Old offer”" instead of a path. Why it asks is one quiet line; the call itself and the time of the request are not shown, and the answer buttons close the card. Once answered, the card folds to one line such as "Allowed once by Jonas. Send an email · anna@nordlys.dk".
- A coding session is one short line in the thread, like a message to a coworker: "Started a coding session in Codex" (or Claude Code), with the maker's own mark before the name. Click it to open the session as its own thread, where its steps, questions, answers and pull request live; the big block that unfolded in the thread, its status line and Show/Hide are gone, and so is the long "…'s coding session with you in … opened a draft PR" sentence. While a session is still going, the header shows it beside the agent: the agent's figure wearing the Codex or Claude mark, a little smaller than the agent's own and standing on the same line, with a thin divider between the figures; it works while the session works and waits while it asks for an answer. Hover or focus shows the session's title in a pill over the header (cut short with …); a click opens the session. The thread reads its sessions again every 2.5 s while one works (6 s while it only waits, 20 s in a hidden tab) and stops once they are all done.
- Codex is drawn with OpenAI's mark and Claude Code with the Claude spark, in the thread line, on the header figures and in the session's own thread; before, they had a hand-drawn prompt and a four-point star. The Claude card in Settings → Connections shows the Claude spark instead of Anthropic's "A".
- A coding session's own thread reads like a conversation. Codex or Claude Code speaks from the left behind its mark: its steps as quiet lines, what it says as bubbles (Markdown, as an agent's messages), its question as a bubble outlined while it waits, and the pull request as a card with Review changes, Open on GitHub and Ask … to review. Your follow-ups and answers stand on the right in your colour, the agent's in its figure's name. Day and time breaks come from the steps' own times. The composer is the conversation's, pinned under the transcript, which keeps the latest step in view unless you scroll up; the status line stands just above it and a question's choices and "Let … decide" sit between the two. Enter sends, Shift+Enter starts a new line, the caret lands in the composer when the session opens and takes input (on a touch screen it stays off, so no keyboard covers a session opened to be read), and a draft is kept when you go back and open the session again. The session's title, repository, branch and model open the transcript. A closing summary that repeats what the harness just said is shown once, and the "Draft PR opened" line gives way to the card. Older steps fold into one line only when at least three would hide, and new steps no longer push the oldest shown one under the fold; a long session is read to its end when it opens. A question's choices belong to the session's current turn only, so an older question's options are never offered for a new one. The footer ("Your answers and follow-ups go to Codex. Uses your Codex plan." with Close) is gone, as are the hint lines under the field; the back arrow and Escape return to the conversation. Other members get one lock line where the composer stands: "Only … can write to this session. Steer it through …".
- A new message no longer redraws the whole thread. Messages, cards and day separators that did not change keep their place on the page; only new or changed ones are drawn. With 1,000 messages a new message took about 270 ms (1.4 s on a four times slower computer) and now takes about 5 ms (25 ms). Screen readers read out only the new message, not the whole thread again. A card being answered keeps what the member typed and where the keyboard was, an open "Event details" stays open, and the working figure keeps moving.
- Opening a thread reads its history, its waiting messages and its coding sessions at the same time and draws it once, when all have arrived. Before, it read them one after another and redrew after every 500 messages. A read that fails keeps the history read before it, and the next read goes on from there.
- The app loads faster: the build splits `main.js` (esbuild code splitting, `scripts/bundle.mjs`). The figures' recorded motion (about 950 KB of frame data) and the computer screen renderer with noVNC are chunks fetched when first needed; a figure shows its still artwork until its motion arrives, then plays from the first frame, and a Computer panel fetches the renderer while it finds the machine. A tab that cannot load the renderer (a deploy renamed it while the tab was open, the network dropped, or its download stalled for 20 s) says so and offers Reload page, and asks nothing of the machine; browsers never fetch a failed module again, so Try again could not help. Figures in such a tab keep their still artwork. supabase-js and marked are a chunk of their own (`src/vendor.ts`) that `index.html` preloads and that keeps its name across deploys that change only app code, so browsers keep it cached. The render-blocking head script `theme.js` no longer carries the Danish dictionary: it is 1 KB instead of 83 KB (`src/ui/appearance.ts`). The sign-in screen downloads 287 KiB brotli instead of 427 KiB, a signed-in first load (with the figures' motion) about 358 KiB, and `web-deployment.json` now checksums every script and stylesheet, chunks included.
- A thread opens at its newest messages (migration `20260928279410_transcript_tail_read`, `qorpera_read_session_events_tail`): it reads the newest 200 events, a page further back while they show fewer than 150 messages (at most five pages), and "Show earlier messages" at the top reads the next page back, keeping the reader on the message they were reading. Older messages stand above the thread's live log, outside it, so a screen reader hears "Loaded 150 earlier messages." instead of each of them, and still hears every new message. Before, it read the whole history oldest first, 500 events at a time, and drew all of it: with 5,000 events a thread took about 1 s to open (2 s on a four times slower computer) and held 15,000 page elements; now about 75 ms (130 ms) and 700. What needs older history waits for it: a coding session started before the oldest message read, and a decision on an approval card not read yet, show once "Show earlier" reaches them; a message a running agent took before the oldest event read is read back to, so it never shows twice. The two agents' view-only conversation has "Show earlier messages" too. Until the cloud has the migration, threads read their whole history as before.
- An open thread with an agent working reads a third as much from the cloud. Every transcript event sends a live hint; a hint now reads only the conversations changed since the newest one read (their `updated_at` cursor, reaching a minute back), the open thread only when it changed (its new events at once, its waiting messages and coding sessions within 10 seconds, also without a later hint), the mentions at most every 5 seconds, and the agents' activity at most every 3 seconds. While hints are live the work watch reads the open thread's new events (not within 1.5 seconds of a hint's read) and the agents' activity on the same 3-second bound, which also shows a run that fails to start without any hint. When live hints come back after an error, what changed meanwhile is read once. In a thread that opened at its newest messages, all of these read only what came after the newest message it has; only "Show earlier messages" reads further back. The whole conversation list is read again every 5 minutes. For a thread whose agent writes every 1.5 seconds while another agent works elsewhere: 392 reads a minute before, 136 now. Without live hints the app polls everything, as before.
- Agent figures are no longer grainy, and their colors are brighter (Jonas, 20 Oct: "a much simpler vibrant look"). `avatar-appearance.js` no longer keeps a third of the source renders' paper grain: each body and face plate is read once into one smooth light field (an edge-aware smooth in stops of light that keeps Planet's ring and Acorn's cap sharp), then painted in the chosen color through an OKLab tone ramp. Shade keeps its color instead of going gray or brown, and the color moves part of the way toward the strongest sRGB chroma at its lightness and hue: grays stay gray, Brown moves little and Blue is capped. A figure's own color is painted the same way, so the original render is never shown as is. Baked eyes stay white. Preparing a figure is 3 to 4 times faster and a new color about 12 times faster. The unused `transform` and `tintPixels` exports are gone.
- The web reads the platform's files under `QorperaOS/` (Jonas, 21 Oct: one top folder for what is about the platform): the sidebar's sections are `QorperaOS/agents/sections.md`, agents and group chats are made under `QorperaOS/agents/` and `QorperaOS/agents/groups/`, the pages read their tables from `QorperaOS/tables/<name>/` (`table:<name>`) and a `file:` source names its whole path (`file:QorperaOS/rules/shared-areas.md`), the folder sources list `QorperaOS/vision/`, `QorperaOS/agents/`, `QorperaOS/connections/`, `QorperaOS/contacts/` and `QorperaOS/human-tasks/`, the composer offers skills from `QorperaOS/skills/`, and an agent's interface change and an offer's table are read below `QorperaOS/`. Storage shows `QorperaOS/` as a normal folder at the top, beside the company's own folders and `code/`. Paths are written through the cloud client's `osPath`, and paths the folder or the database hand back are read with `osRelative`. Needs migration `20260930534000`.
- A first visit reaches the API a little sooner (perf E2, 7 Nov 2026). `index.html` opens the connection to the deployment's API origin ahead of `main.js`: the first API answer comes about 20 ms sooner after DOM ready (headless Chrome, signed out, the line pasted into the live page, 12 pairs). The sign-in page marks its two lockups lazy, so that in Chrome only the one its theme shows downloads, about 90 KB less (one run; Safari and Firefox not checked).
- The composer's "+" menu looks like the app's other menus, and moves (Jonas, 10 Nov 2026: "Improve/redo the popup window when clicking + in the chatcomposer, and add a micro animation when it opens/closes"). It stands over the "+" that opens it, at a width of its own (264 px), with a thin edge and a soft shadow, in the system face at a regular weight (15 px rows, 13 px captions), with a hairline between Attach files and the settings under it and a small quiet arrow on a row that opens a page. It springs up out of the "+" and drops back into it when it closes: the menu stays on the page, out of reach, for the 140 ms that takes, and the "+" turns to a cross and stays pressed in while the menu is open. A page (Model, Login) comes in from its side while the menu takes the page's height, and the Model page shows models, effort and speed without scrolling on an ordinary window. With reduced motion all of it happens at once.

### Fixed
- A new website shows its first published page without a reload (seen live 24 Nov 2026 on a new "Go live test" website; Jonas: "Yes, fix them"). After the first approved change the page stayed on "Nothing is live yet" until a reload: the company's site list was read only when the company opened and when the window came back, so the site the first publish made was never in it, and without it the view draws neither the site, its address nor Go live. The app now reads the site list again once an interface's publish has ended while its site is not yet listed as served (`sitesAwaited` and `readSitesOncePublished` in `company.ts`, called from `application.ts`): once per such state, no loop and no press. The rule that a view writing by itself does nothing while the site list is unread is unchanged.
- A website's rail row no longer says "Live · <host>" while the site is not live. Its second line follows the bar's answer (`siteAnswer` in `company.ts`, from the site list and whether the site is public): "Live · <host>" only for a site everyone can open, "Not live yet" for one that is not or that Qorpera took down, and the host alone while either is not known (`views/surface-rail.ts`). Whether each site is public comes from the company's hosting read, taken with the site list while a website is served, and from every read and change of it the address card and Settings > Sites make (`hostingNotingPublic`); a read that started before such a change ended, or lands while one runs, is dropped (`startPublicRead`).
- Model accounts no longer looks stuck on "Refreshing…" (Jonas, 23 Nov 2026). The page read every login in the background, the Claude logins one after another at about 20 s each, and started again a minute after each round, with the button saying "Refreshing…" all the while. Now only the member's own Refresh says "Refreshing…" and greys the button; background reads stay quiet, and a Refresh pressed during one runs its own read once that one ends. In the background each Claude login is read at most every five minutes; Refresh still reads all of them.
- Teammates' faces on a surface are small again: the surface bar's presence faces were about 52 px because their style named `.avatar` while the faces are `.person-avatar`, and on a surface's rail row the faces sat on top of the surface's figure because the row had no place for them. The bar now shows up to three 22 px faces and "+N"; a surface row puts them at its right end (in the narrow rail, on the figure's corner, as for other rows).
- A run whose agent finished its turn without sending its answer no longer says "The run started, but one of its steps was refused." (seen live 10 Nov 2026: a Co-Founder's card under the founder's message, with Dismiss only). Nothing was refused; the agent's turn ended without its final `send_message`, so the member got no reply. The card now says "The agent finished without sending its answer. The problem is on Qorpera's side." and offers Try again wherever the cloud allows a retry. It reads the runtime's new code `AGENT_TURN_ENDED_WITHOUT_FINAL_SEND`, and `NATIVE_PUBLIC_COMPLETION:UNKNOWN`, which older runs carry for the same ending; any other category at that stage reads as before, and a run a member stopped still reads as stopped. Danish: "Agenten blev færdig uden at sende sit svar. Fejlen ligger hos Qorpera."
- A coding session's failed turn is told by its code (live company-building test, 3 November 2026; migration `20260930560000`). The cloud now gives a failed turn plain words instead of the executor's text with the bridge's code, and the code travels apart in the `failed` event's `code` (20260930551000); the card classifies by that code as it did by the summary, and a code no class names still reads as a step refused on Qorpera's side. The new reason `provider_outage` (ChatGPT overloaded or a server error, recorded for the run) says "ChatGPT was overloaded or had a server error while the session worked. This is usually brief, and the login is fine. The workspace is kept; a follow-up continues it." instead of asking to check the login. Older events keep their summaries' codes. The conversation no longer shows a coding turn's failed run as "An earlier run of <agent> did not finish" (the cloud stops listing it). Danish too.
- Fixes from the 3 Nov 2026 live test:
  - A table opened from an idea without a typed name has no name yet (the newest entry); the idea is no longer made into one.
  - The new-company box keeps focus and the caret when it moves between the hub and `#/new` or is drawn again in a new language, gives focus back when an opening fails, and is empty once a table opens from its idea (also through Resume), so a second idea is never typed after the first. Its example ideas are written in the placeholder only, and stop for good once the founder is in the box.
  - Escape pressed inside a page shown in Storage (its frame) works as Escape anywhere else: the frame document passes it to the app (`qorpera-page-escape`) once the page has left it alone, which closes the page shown across the window, then the file, then Settings. Settings opened from a folder link in a conversation gives the composer focus when it closes.
  - The composer no longer stays locked on "Model accounts are unavailable": a failed account read is tried again after 2 s, 5 s, 10 s, 30 s, then every minute, and at once when the tab is shown again or the browser comes back online. The line says "Trying again…" and stays up while it does.
  - Live updates no longer flash "degraded" when coming back to a tab the browser froze: a channel that failed while the page was hidden tries again at once when the page is back, and shows degraded only if it has not subscribed within 5 s (`RESUME_GRACE_MS`). Its return still re-reads what the hints may have missed.
  - A tab left open learns of a deploy: every 10 minutes and when the tab is shown or focused again it reads `web-deployment.json`, and when a file it loaded from `build/` (main.js, its preloads, the stylesheet) is no longer listed, a line says "A new version is available." with Reload. The files are compared, not `sourceCommit`, which changes with every merge (the site is built again for runtime-only merges too). It never reloads by itself, and Reload asks first when words are not sent yet.
  - The Company File's hint channel joins after Realtime has the member's token, so its first join is no longer refused ("Unauthorized") and live hints start at once instead of after the 10 s retry.
- "Choose a login" and a new conversation's first login follow the cloud's order (3 November 2026; migration `20260930559000`): a login whose provider refused its last three runs last, then one that completed a run this week with at least 20% left first, then the most room on one scale (each window's room divided by the share of the window still to run), so a 30-day window at 3% no longer beats a weekly one at 50% that resets tomorrow. The connection list's `recent_turns` is read beside the SDK's fields (`WebMemberConnection.recentTurns`).
- A coding session whose login cannot pay says so, and its owner can move it to another of their logins (live company-building test, 3 November 2026; needs migration `20260930551000`). The founder signed out the login a coding session was dispatched on, and its next turn read only "The session stopped because of an internal error." A failed turn whose event carries a login code (`login_removed`, `login_disabled`, `login_needs_sign_in`, `login_not_allowed_away`, `login_unavailable`) now says why the turn could not run and who can act: the owner is told to choose another of their logins and send a message, another member that only the owner can (`codingLoginReason`). While the session takes a message and its login cannot pay (the view's `loginState`), or its last turn stopped at its login's usage limit (`reason: usage_limit`) and it was not moved since, the owner's card lists their other logins that can pay for it (`qorpera_list_coding_session_logins`; email, else "Model account N") as "Use …" buttons, or says none can; a click moves the session (`qorpera_choose_coding_session_login`) and the card reads it again. The move shows as "You moved the session to another login". Nothing moves without the click. `parseCodingSession` reads `loginState` and `reason`, and `parseCodingSessionLogins` the list.
- A session the cloud no longer accepts signs the member out (3 November 2026). After the database move (new Auth signing keys) a browser kept its old session, and the new-company page showed "No suitable key or wrong key type" in red while other calls failed. Now, when Auth's `/user` or a token refresh answers 401/403, or PostgREST, a function or storage answers 401 with a JWT error (`PGRST301`, `PGRST303`, "Invalid JWT", "JWT expired", "No suitable key or wrong key type") for the token this browser holds, the app clears the session here (`signOut({ scope: "local" })`) and shows sign-in with "You were signed out. Sign in again.", once. The web's own direct PostgREST and function calls (notices, rooms, model, run control, cloud execution, computer billing) report their answers too (`cloud.noticeResponse`). A permission refusal, a network failure or a 5xx keeps the member signed in; a late refusal of an earlier token, or one a refresh replaced while the sign-out waited, is ignored; and a stored session Auth could not remove is forgotten here (`createCloud`'s fetch, `sessionRejection`, `AuthController`). Danish too.
- Live updates recover by themselves (3 November 2026). After Realtime restarted or dropped an idle connection, the rail showed "Live updates degraded" (channel error: transport failure) until the member opened a new tab. A failed company channel (`CHANNEL_ERROR`, `TIMED_OUT`, or `CLOSED` by the server) is now replaced by a fresh one after 1 s, 2 s, 5 s, 10 s, then every 30 s, with Realtime's token refreshed first (`realtime.setAuth()`); while the socket itself is down, a try reopens it and waits again instead of queueing joins for the reconnect; a subscription resets the waits, and the window regaining focus or the browser coming back online tries at once. The sidebar's channel recovers the same way (`keepSubscribed` in `cloud/realtime.ts`). Polling stays the recovery path while it is away.
- A failed run tells ChatGPT's refusal, a dropped connection and a login that could not be loaded apart from Qorpera's own failures (live company-building test, 3 November 2026; web only, no SQL). A ChatGPT workspace plan's login had its Codex turns fail (`[code=NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:PROVIDER_FAILED]`), and the card said "The run started, but one of its steps was refused. The problem is on Qorpera's side." The card now reads the whole code or its category (the part after the colon, from the runtime's closed list). `NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:PROVIDER_FAILED` (the turn failed with ChatGPT's own error, which Codex reads only once the turn is under way) says "ChatGPT refused or could not answer this run's request. Check the login that pays for it in Settings → AI subscriptions, or choose another login for this conversation." with Dismiss only. The same category at an earlier step (`NATIVE_CODEX_INITIALIZE`, `_CONFIGURATION`, `_SKILLS`, `_THREAD_START`, `_TURN_START`) is the pinned Codex refusing a request on the host, which never reached ChatGPT, and stays Qorpera's. The category `TRANSPORT_UNAVAILABLE` says "The run lost its connection while it was working. This is usually brief." with Try again (the words never ask for it, since the cloud refuses it once work took effect); and the whole code `NATIVE_CREDENTIAL_DOWNLOAD` (the host could not download the run's login before the model started) says "The run could not load the login that pays for it. Check that login in Settings → AI subscriptions, or choose another login for this conversation." with Dismiss only. The card is the agent's, so every member reads it: what asks something of the login is said so only to the member whose own login paid (the run's `sponsor_binding_id`, matched against their logins); anyone else, or a member whose logins are not read yet, reads "… The member whose login pays for it can check that login in Settings → AI subscriptions." (or "The member whose login it is can check it …"). A coding session's failed turn says the same, to its owner or to anyone else, then "The workspace is kept; a follow-up continues it." Unchanged: Qorpera's own codes (such as `NATIVE_COMPANY_CONTEXT:UNKNOWN`, `NATIVE_CODING_SESSION:UNKNOWN`, `CODING_*`, `NATIVE_HOST_CREDENTIAL`, opening, sealing or uploading the login) still say "The problem is on Qorpera's side."; the cloud's usage-limit flag still decides a usage limit, and a spent budget it did not record as one stays Qorpera's; a timed-out or cancelled step (also a login download that timed out) keeps its class; the runner's `PROVIDER_ACQUIRE` (its provider of computers) still reads as no computer; no words inside a code decide. No text says the work moves to another login. At a usage limit the card now also says when the limit resets ("It can run again when the limit resets ({time}).") when the login that paid is one of the member's own and one of its usage windows is full with a reset ahead, the rule the agent reads (`qorpera_worker_usage_limit_text`, migration `20260930549300`); never the cloud's estimate for a limit with no known reset. Danish too.
- A coding session's later turn no longer shows the session's age as its working time (31 October 2026). A session started the day before got a follow-up, and after about a minute its pill and its footer both said "Working · 22 h 11 min", because both counted from `startedAt`, which the cloud sets only at the session's first claim. The session's own thread now counts a later turn from that turn's first claim: its first `phase: preparing` event, which it reads with the rest of the stream, from the start (`codingTurnStartedAt`). The pill reads only the listing, which has no turn start, so on a later turn it shows no time ("Working · Workspace ready"). A first turn shows its time from `startedAt` in both, as before. Preparing and waiting for an answer are unchanged. No migration and no text change.
- A run whose completion the cloud refused says so, instead of reading as lost contact (30 October 2026). The runner now fails such a run at once with `[code=RUN_COMPLETION_REFUSED]` (the run left unsettled work, such as a save whose receipt is still executing). Before, nothing was said until the lease sweep failed it minutes later as lost contact. The card says "The run ended, but one of its saves did not finish. A person must check it before the run can be tried again." (a coding session's note likewise; class `completion_refused`, with Danish). Try again is as it was for lost contact: it counts as interrupted, and the card still hides it while the run has an unsettled operation (`canRetryFailedRun`).
- A failure no longer outranks the agent's newer work (stale failure card, 29 October 2026). A member sent the Co-Founder a new message after its earlier runs had failed; the new run did its work, but the conversation kept the old failure card, the rail showed the agent as failed, and there was no Working indicator, on reload too. The cloud now lists a failure from before the agent's newer run apart, as `superseded` (migration `20260930542000`, SQL test 469), and the web never takes the agent's state from it: while the newer run is queued or running the agent reads as Working, in the conversation and in the rail, and once it is done no failure comes back. The earlier failure keeps its card, titled "An earlier run of … did not finish" ("An earlier review did not finish" for a review), with Try again and Dismiss as before; a new message dismisses it as it did the agent's last failure. A run whose Company File proposal is open still asks for the decision first. A cloud before the migration sends no `superseded`, and the web reads it as before.
- A step cancelled from Qorpera's side reads as lost contact, not as refused (WB-001, 28 October 2026): a failed run whose error code's category is `CANCELLED` (e.g. `[code=NATIVE_CODEX_TURN_COMPLETION:CANCELLED]`, when the run's authority could not be read through a database outage) says "Qorpera lost contact with this run. The problem is on Qorpera's side." (a coding session's note likewise); a run a member stopped still reads as stopped. Only the code's category moves the class, as for a timeout (21 October). The card no longer offers Try again that the cloud refuses: not for a run with a completed operation or one of unknown outcome (`qorpera_resolve_failed_execution_run` and `qorpera_retry_room_delivery` refuse to repeat it; the recovery's operations now tell the card), and not for a session turn another member's ChatGPT plan paid for (20260929950000). No sentence changed, so no Danish change.
- An agent's new figure or colour, picked on its card, shows in the rail at once instead of after the save and the next roster read. The rail draws the choice while it is being saved and drops it once the roster has it (nothing is rebuilt then), when the save fails, or when the card's edits end (`CompanyState.looks`, `CompanyController.showLook`).
- Storage says what a company stores. The meter read `qorpera_company_files.storage_used_bytes`, which folder-v2 never fills, so it showed "0 B used" for every company. `qorpera_company_file_json` now answers a folder-v2 company's stored bytes as its folder quota counts them (`qorpera_folder_storage_usage`, or the object catalog's sum before that row exists; migration `20260930524000`, SQL test 972), so the meter, the Company card and Plan & usage show the real figure.
- A run whose lease lapsed no longer blames "the computer running this run": the lease can lapse when Qorpera's own database stops answering (21 Oct), so the card says "Qorpera lost contact with this run. The problem is on Qorpera's side." (a coding session's note likewise). A step that ran out of time (a code whose category is a timeout, such as `[code=NATIVE_PROMPT_COMPOSITION:TIMEOUT]`) says one of the run's steps got no answer in time, and offers Try again, instead of saying the step was refused.
- Agent figures have smooth edges again, at every size (Jonas, 20 Oct: after #1321 "the shapes edges aren't smooth at all anymore"). Drawing the 512 px surfaces several times smaller in one step skipped pixels wherever the browser resamples bilinearly, which GPU canvases do whatever `imageSmoothingQuality` asks; #1321's crisper edge and the dropped feather made that show as jagged outlines and eyes. `ui/figure-levels.ts` halves each surface to the power of two at or above its painted size (every 2:1 step averages exactly) and caches it; `figure-motion.ts` draws the body from it, and draws the eyes at 512 as the studio does (recorded eyes are a small raster, stretched) and halves that layer to the same size each frame through reused canvases; `group-avatar.ts` draws each member's figure and knockout from it. The silhouette's coverage is steepened 2 times instead of 2.5.
- A deploy that changes the vendored figure scripts reaches members at once (Jonas, 20 Oct: the grain-free figures of #1317 did not show). `index.html` loaded `assets/avatars/motion-model.js` and `avatar-appearance.js` by fixed names, and `/assets/*` is cached for a day, so the Cloudflare edge and the browser kept the old scripts for up to 24 hours after a deploy. The build now adds each script's content hash as a query (`?v=` and 12 hex digits of its SHA-256), and refuses to build without one.

- Moving and reordering agents and group chats in the rail works again on folder-v2 companies. Their sections are `agents/sections.md`, and `qorpera_manage_company_sidebar` refuses section changes and moves there (55000, migration `20260928249100`), so every drag, Alt+arrow and Move to ended in "The sidebar could not be changed. Try again." Section moves, new sections, renames and deletes are now the member's edits of that file through the member commit route, as the runtime's `organize_sidebar` makes them: the lines before the first section stay as they are, the version read is named, a changed file is read again, and the sidebar is read once the commit placed it. Pins and hidden items stay with the RPC; an item moved out of the pinned row is unpinned after the file is saved. The file keeps no order for Unassigned, so a reorder there sends nothing and says "Agents outside a section stay in the order they joined. Put them in a section to order them." A refusal from the folder puts the rail back (the Co-Founder rule in plain words). Danish included.
- Quotes in drafts (#860, after I5d-4): a quote whose words hold « or » is refused, as the cloud now refuses it (migration `20260930087000`). A shared draft that still holds one (written before; the migration deletes them) is treated as absent: the owner's composer starts empty and the next save replaces it, and a teammate's is not listed. A teammate's draft mended from the quote limit, from "too long" or from a broken quote is written though its panel closes within the write delay.
- An agent routine's notice no longer empties the notice band above the composer. Those notices carry no subject schema, so one of them failed the whole read. A suspended routine's notice (`routine_suspended`) is now skipped, and a waiting routine's (`routine_needs_model_account`) is read (a notice of an unknown kind still fails the read).
- A coding session that fails says why in plain words, never with the runner's own text ("Failed: Native provider bridge request failed (422) [code=…]"). A turn that ended without `coding_finish` or `coding_ask` (`[code=CODING_TURN_ENDED_WITHOUT_SUBMISSION]`, or the executor's "ended without a submission") reads "The session's last turn ended without saving its work or asking a question. The workspace is kept; a follow-up continues it." Other failures use the run card's classification (usage limit, account to connect again, no computer, a step with no answer in time, a refused step, stopped), each with what happens to the workspace, and anything else reads "The session stopped because of an internal error." The lost-computer and out-of-computer-hours sentences are unchanged.
- The rules list in Settings → General reads company rules that come from a company's `rules/` files (`createdVia: "folder"`, migration `20260928249190_folder_state_rules`). Before, one such rule made the whole list fail with "Rule is invalid.".
- A coding session past its 64th turn no longer fails to load: the web client no longer assumes a turn limit the cloud does not have.
- Settings → Friends only offers a friend request to an address that belongs to a Qorpera account. Typing a whole email looks it up once typing pauses: an account shows as a person under "Add a friend" and Send request lights up; any other address says "No Qorpera account uses …" and nothing can be sent, and your own address says so. Before, any well-formed address enabled Send request and the cloud refused it only after it was sent. An address counts once its owner has confirmed it (signed in with it), the whole address and never part of one, and a friend request follows the same rule (migration `20260928031000_friend_account_lookup`). Until the cloud has the migration, the search works as before.
- Closing an agent's computer before it has started no longer leaves it running for nothing. Opening the computer wakes it; closing the panel before the screen came up now takes that wake back (migration `20260927110000_runtime_office_wake_withdrawal`). The computer still finishes starting, since stopping a machine halfway through its boot is not safe, but once every member who woke it has closed it again and no agent or viewer needs it, it turns off one minute after it is up instead of after the usual five idle minutes. A wake whose screen connected, a first start and leaving the company send nothing. Until the cloud has the migration, closing works as before.
- Keep keyboard focus on Refresh in Settings → Connections while it reads usage. The button is greyed out and does nothing while a read runs, but stays focusable, so focus no longer drops to "Add account".
- A message still waiting for the agent now stands before the first message sent after it. Before, every waiting message sat below the whole conversation, so one the cloud never took (a "Queued" message from days ago) stayed at the bottom under every newer message and reply, with its old day break in the wrong place. A message sent while the agent is still writing stays at the bottom, below the agent's output, as before.
- A message the member stopped no longer shows the agent as failed. Before, Stop left a red disc and a "did not finish this run" line with Try again, and the composer lost its Stop for the agent's other work (such as a routine run in the same thread) until the line was dismissed. A stopped run now just ends: the agent shows what it is still doing, or idle, and Try again is not offered (to repeat the message, send it again). If Stop cut off Company File changes midway, the agent shows "Needs you" with Review, below any work still running or queued. The cloud side is migration `20260926192436_member_stop_settled_activity`; until it is live, the web marks such a stop as seen for the company once it has read the stop's details (members who can write only), so the work it hid, and that work's Stop, show again.
- A new stylesheet, script or picture that a Cloudflare edge did not have yet during a deploy is now answered with a "not found" that no cache keeps. Before, the edge kept the app page under that file's name, for up to a year under `/build/` (the app loaded without its styles on that edge until the file was renamed) and a day under `/assets/`. The build writes a small `404.html` into both folders, and fails if `_headers` lets caches keep files anywhere it cannot guard the same way. Every other path still opens the app.
- The app loads styled again where Cloudflare's edge had cached the page's HTML under the stylesheet's address (`build/app-QUCAFXYX.css`, requested while the #296 deploy was still spreading); the stylesheet now ships under a new name.
- Scrolling the rail or a thread no longer lays a tall fade over the Co-Founder card or the top message: the fade that marks more above is now 8 design px (about 10 on screen, was 36), close to the sidebar in the Claude app.
- A profile picture being saved is never deleted by a cleanup of leftover uploads that answered late; saving a picture after a very slow connection no longer fails and needs another try.
- Gmail and Google Drive in Settings → Connections → Services show their own logos instead of the Google "G".
- A right-click in the rail during the page's first 800 ms opens the agent or section menu; the long-press guard swallowed it before any press had opened one, which also made the rail menu tests fail at random in CI.
- Changing the figure of an agent that wears its figure's own colour now gives it the new figure's own colour, until a colour is picked in the same picker; a picked colour is kept through every figure. The first-run page keeps a colour chosen in the agent's details unless a different figure is picked there.
- Show grey placeholder shapes while a screen loads instead of a line such as "Opening company" or "Loading conversation". Opening a company or a conversation shows the rail, the thread header, message bubbles and the composer as placeholders on their real sizes, and each part is replaced where it stands as it arrives; on a phone the placeholder header keeps the button that opens the rail. The company list, Team, Friends, group details and Company File do the same. Placeholders stand in only for a first read: a refresh keeps what the page already shows. The shapes breathe together and stay still with reduced motion; a screen reader still hears what is loading. The Company File page now says when membership could not be read instead of waiting forever, and a start-up that cannot reach the cloud shows the error with Try again.
- Let the Co-Founder's colour be changed: its figure picker in the agent details offers the colours and Reset like any agent's, and the rail, header and group pictures draw the colour chosen. A Co-Founder whose colour nobody has picked keeps its figure's own shade.
- Keep keyboard focus in Settings → General when the account plan finishes loading; the page is redrawn only when the error or message it shows changes.
- Show a failed or stopped run as one line right above the composer, in the same band as the company notices, instead of at the end of the thread. The thread keeps only the conversation; the card keeps its reason, Try again and Dismiss. Opening a conversation while such a line, or the composer, appears no longer leaves the thread a little short of its last message.
- Show the composer's "Chat settings and more" button in group chats too. Its menu keeps Attach files greyed out with a note that images are not supported in group chats and to use Company File; the model choice stays with single-agent conversations.
- Entering an agent's computer shows the guest screen inside a thin frame in full screen, a computer inside a computer, with the agent's name on the left and one exit button on the right, the exit-full-screen glyph named "Back to thread"; the separate toggle is gone. If the browser refuses full screen, or never answers, the same frame opens over the thread and says "Full screen unavailable"; the next Enter asks again.
- Make thread and rail text sharper and easier to read, above all on the dark theme and on 1x screens: the thread and composer are set in the system face (San Francisco on a Mac, as Messages and the rail already are) at 16px on a 21px line with no sub-pixel letter-spacing, the page renders without macOS font dilation, dark bubbles carry white text on a darker grey, dark text tokens are brighter, and rail role lines and timestamps step up a size and weight.
- Show what an agent is doing in its conversation the way the desktop does: hovering or focusing the working figure at the end of the thread reads "Nova is working" or "Nova is writing" with the ink wave, and the row no longer relies on a browser tooltip. The rail card's "Working" and "Writing" carry the same wave, and every wave on the page runs on one clock, so the card and the thread caption move together.
- Preserve agent metadata drafts and retry state across details-panel closes, serialize saves, and retire confirmed edits when the roster catches up so later changes by other members are not overwritten.
- Keep keyboard focus on an available execution-notice action when refreshes remove the previously focused control.
- Keep the rail's "Needs you" for runs waiting on a member decision, including a failed run that left Company File changes. An agent with a failed or stopped run nobody has dismissed now rests idle with a red status disc, the opposite of the green working disc; the rail link names the state in words, also in the compact rail.
- Render room replies with immutable server-projected staff identity and preserve accepted human messages even when no agent is eligible.

- Use a length-neutral email code hint so sign-in instructions remain accurate with the configured eight-digit codes.
- Submit team-room messages through the public room-post contract, preserve room retry identity, and match room attachments/run displays to the supported interfaces.
- Keep the rail's search field, keyboard focus and scroll position when the rail refreshes: dead-key, IME and phone-keyboard typing is no longer broken, and a collapsed rail stops filtering by a search it hides until it opens again. A refresh rebuilds only the rows that changed, so moving between agents keeps their figures and an error notice is not announced again, and figures repaint sharp when the window moves to a screen of another pixel density.
- Make primary buttons inside Settings readable; they rendered white text on the light grey settings button.
- Keep dead-key and IME typing intact in the Settings Friends search: ´ then e gave "´é", and Pinyin and Android keyboard input came out garbled. The field now stays in place while the list below it filters, and the list is redrawn only when what it shows changes.
- Draw Markdown task-list items in messages with one checkbox; they also printed their `[x]` or `[ ]` marker as text.
- A failed run whose computer stopped answering no longer says "The run took too long and was stopped." Runs have had no time limit since 2026-09-24, so a lapsed lease means the computer running the agent crashed or lost its connection. The line above the composer now says "The computer running this run stopped answering. The problem is on Qorpera's side." and still offers Try again. A run where one request got no answer in time (the model, the secret broker or another cloud call) now says "One of the run's steps got no answer in time. The problem is on Qorpera's side." instead of "took too long", also with Try again. A run that no computer could start within a day now says no computer could be started, instead of "an internal error", and offers Try again where it offered only Dismiss before. Older runs that really hit a time limit keep the old sentence.
- A file preview opened from a message no longer closes when another message arrives.
- Screen readers read a member's own message once. Before, it was read again when the cloud took it, when the agent started on it and when it reached the transcript; now the message stays in place and only "Processing" is read. An answered card is read again once, when it turns from question to answer, not when the answer is confirmed. A message's day separator stays where it was when the message changes, and a new one is read just before its message.
- After midnight a thread no longer draws every message again at its next update. Only times that now name their day change, in place; older messages stay as they are, and a card being answered keeps what the member typed and the keyboard.
- The "Ready to go live" card in a thread keeps its three buttons on one row (seen live 15 Nov 2026 in a window of about 1000 px: beside the rail the card is as narrow as on a phone, and "Not yet" fell to a row of its own). The buttons now tighten by the width of their own row, not by the window's, and in a thread they always say the same short words: "Put it live" for a project's first version too (What's new's page still names the first version), and no other words while a press is on its way (the pressed button is marked busy and all three are off), so the row never changes width.

### Added

- Lay out the two agents' view-only conversation like a chat between two people: the coworker on the left, this thread's own agent on the right with the bubble tail mirrored; the chips in a thread are a small centered line, "Messaged" or "Message from" with the coworker's figure before its name, as the reference product shows them.
- Mark an agent's message that was left for a coworker without waking it: the pair conversation shows "Left for Helper's next turn" under it.
- Show an agent's coding sessions in its conversation (Order 9): a centered chip in the shape of a bot-to-bot handoff, "Bo started a coding session with you in ✳ Claude Code", with the agent's figure and the harness mark, and the live status under it ("Fix the delay after send · Working · 4 min · running vitest · 3 files changed"). The chip opens the session as its own thread over the conversation, with "Bo ⇄ Claude Code" in the header and Close in the footer; Show unfolds it in place. Both show the session's own steps, what the harness says, the question it asks with answer buttons and "Let Bo decide", the owner's input field, and the draft pull request with its checks and links. Only the session's owner can type into it; everyone else reads it and steers through the agent. Nothing shows until the cloud carries coding sessions.
- Show what agents say to each other. When one agent messages a coworker, the sender's thread gets a small chip "Messaged Helper" and the coworker's thread gets "Message from New Bot"; either chip opens the two agents' conversation over the thread, view-only, with both figures in the header, each message under the speaking agent's name, a lock line "This chat is view-only" and one Close Chat button. A line in that conversation says when an exchange has paused at its limit. These conversations never appear in the rail; members steer by writing in either agent's thread.

- Settings has a Plan & usage tab: the account's current plan (the freemium plan is now called "Free trial") with an Upgrade button that opens the three plans qorpera.com sells (Basis, Standard, Dedicated computer, with prices and what they include; choosing one says "Coming soon" until plan changes open), a Usage section with computer hours (a meter once the cloud reports them, "Not measured yet" until then) and the chosen company's storage, and a Billing card (price, next payment, payment method, invoices) that shows placeholders until billing lands. Nothing moved to General's loss except the plan card. The standalone account page keeps the plan below General.
- Appearance in Settings → General shows its choices as pictures instead of menus: Theme offers System, Light and Dark as miniature windows (System split down the middle), and Composer color offers Blue, Green and Grey as miniature conversations in the page's current theme. Each group is a radio group, so arrow keys move between cards.

- Resize the computer preview by dragging any of its edges or corners, from its resting size up to twice that, keeping its 16:9 shape and staying inside the thread. When the preview has keyboard focus, plus and minus resize it too.
- Present member-addressed execution notices inline in the workspace, with explicit acknowledgment and recovery after uncertain requests.
- Add a dark theme. Settings → General → Theme offers System default (follows the device's appearance), Light and Dark; the choice is kept per browser, follows changes made in other tabs, and a head script applies it before the first paint.
- Show a failed or stopped run in the agent's conversation with its error and a Dismiss action (the desktop's discard), so an old failure stops standing in for the agent's state; a run that left Company File changes links to them instead.
- Support bounded public JSON environment configuration for Pages release builds, with shared SDK checks and release artifact validation in web CI.

- Record the source commit and runtime bundle identity in web deployment artifacts; require clean source for release builds.

- Add provider sign-in through the exported native login SDK when an accepted public gateway is configured; keep login capability handling outside the UI and require explicit recovery after uncertainty.
- Submit conversation work through the shared sponsored-turn API with explicit member account selection, exact retry identity, execution history and observed-run stop controls.
- Discover, enable and revoke existing member model accounts through the shared client SDK, with refresh recovery after uncertain changes.
- Connect the product web app's Computer panel to shared member runtime discovery, status, explicit start intents and screen viewing.
- Bind member requests and viewer cleanup to the selected company and account lifetime.
- Check browser dependency boundaries and the provenance of the shared screen renderer during builds.
- Add explicit desktop/shell permission controls, owned browser profiles, private sign-in and separate browser consent to the product Computer panel.
- Allow managers to cancel the observed pending computer start, and fence viewer replacement and late connections against panel teardown.
- Connect the web composer to bounded member prompt submission, with account/company/conversation checks after token refresh and preserved request identity for explicit retries.
- Connect Company File to shared governed reads, versioned writes, selected-file uploads, verified downloads, exact proposal review, exports and optional activity hints.
- Preserve File drafts on cancelled navigation, clear content when access ends, and offer explicit reload recovery without replaying uncertain writes.
- Stage selected conversation images against the exact member input before submitting a message, preserve explicit retry identity, and verify canonical image previews through the shared attachment API.
- Add account-level browser consent cleanup and private sign-in closure, including after leaving a company, with account and route cancellation.
- Bind deployed cloud endpoints and CSP to one validated public build configuration, require a viewer origin for release builds, and ignore stored preview overrides in configured builds.

### Changed

- More room between the last message of a thread and the composer (16 → 40 design px).
- Routines show as cards in an agent's details, as Grok Bot's do: the name, when it runs in few words ("Weekdays at 8:48 AM", with the zone only when it is not yours) and a switch that pauses or resumes it at once. Click a card to open it in place: the whole instruction on the left, scrolling inside the card with a fade where more lies above or below, and when it runs (with its zone), the next run and the last run on the right; Edit appears by the switch. One card is open at a time and the list scrolls it into view. Ended routines show "Ended" or "Done" instead of the switch, and a concluded one says why. The agent popup is 880 design px wide on every page (was 720), so an open routine reads beside its facts; on a phone the instruction flows and the page scrolls. Members who cannot manage routines see the switch greyed. A switch that could not be changed moves back and the reason stays on screen; before, the list read after a failed pause or resume wiped the reason at once.
- Show your name on the rail's account button in larger type: 21px instead of 14px (50% larger), with the loading placeholder's name bar to match. The name outside a company, in the top bar, keeps its size.
- End the Settings side panel with a "Back to companies" row, drawn like the tabs, instead of the Company picker: it closes Settings and opens the company list, and is hidden when Settings are opened from the company list. Settings no longer switch companies. Opened over a company they show that company and no other; opened elsewhere they keep the company they show while the account lists it, else show the account's only company, and with several companies Company and Connections ask to open a company first ("Open a company to see these settings.") and Plan & usage "Open a company to see its storage."
- Rules move into Settings → General, under Language & region, laid out as Grok Bot's Auto-review rules: "When an agent wants to:", then "It should:" and (for owners and admins) "Applies to:" beside Add rule, which waits until a rule is typed. Every rule the member can see sits in one table (Action, Behavior, Applies to), the company's first; a pencil edits a rule's sentence and behaviour in its row (Enter or the tick saves, Escape or the cross leaves it), a bin removes it, and company rules show a lock to everyone who cannot change them. The Rules tab, its intro, the two "Company rules" / "Your rules" lists and the "Ask first wins" line are gone; one line remains under the table: "Built-in safety checks always apply." On a phone each rule stacks its sentence over its behaviour and scope. With no company open (Settings opened from the company list, with several companies), General says "Open a company to see its rules." in their place.
- Draw the web app at 130% by default, the size the browser's own 130% zoom gives: text, controls, the rail, dialogs, menus and agent figures are three tenths larger on screens used with a mouse or trackpad, and layouts change at the same window widths they would at 130% zoom. Phones and tablets keep their sizes, and so does the minimized computer preview (400 × 225, its buttons and text); the entered computer view follows the new size. The rail's default width is 369px before the scale, 480 on screen (was 456px), so three pinned agents share a row; every member starts once from it, and a width chosen after that is kept. Settings keeps its large side panel on windows wider than 1300 screen pixels (1000 before the scale, was 1100), so 13" and 14" laptops keep it too.
- Make the Settings side panel half again as large (20px text, 26px icons, 54px tab rows, with the search field and invitation count to match) and order its tabs General, Company, Friends, Plan & usage, Connections. The panel is 320px wide so every label fits on one line in English and Danish, and it scrolls when the window is too short for it. Windows up to 1000px wide (1300 on screen at the 130% scale; tablets in portrait, phones) keep the earlier, smaller panel, so the settings themselves keep their width. A search that hides the open tab selects the top matching tab, so "invitations" now opens Company.
- Settings → General → Appearance shows only its two rows of pictures: the "Theme" and "Composer color" titles and their hint lines ("System follows your device’s appearance.", "Send button, your message bubbles, and selected agent cards.") are gone. Screen readers still hear each group's name.
- Show the close × of the Settings window and the feedback window as a bare glyph in both themes: no grey square behind it, at rest, on hover or on keyboard focus. Hover and focus only darken the × (brighten it on dark), and keyboard focus still shows its ring.
- Lower the top edge of the rail's Co-Founder card by 10px, so the card is thinner and the figure stands higher above it. The figure, the name and the rows below keep their place.
- Show only the name on the rail's Co-Founder card, which makes the card a line thinner. Its role and any state ("Working", "Needs you") stay in the card's tooltip and link name, the status disc and the figure still show a run, and other agents' rows keep their second line.
- Put the rail's search field on the same row as the new-agent button, both half again as large (46px field, 27px plus), and widen the default rail from 304px to 456px. Rail widths stored before this change are not carried over, resetting the rail width forgets the stored width, and the agent computer panel covers the conversation whenever the thread is too narrow for both side by side.
- Make the Settings window 20% larger, give its sections icons, and set their labels in whole-pixel, medium-weight dark ink so they no longer read soft.
- Move Friends into Settings as its own tab, together with the company invitations waiting for the account. The profile menu's Friends item opens that tab and carries the invitation count, and the company invite picker's "Add a friend" switches to it.
- Settings → Company opens on its people without the company name as a heading. Member rows are 25% larger (70px rows, 52px pictures, 16px names), and the invite row that ends the list matches them. The Staff at work heading and figures are centred. Delete company (Leave company for other members) is a plain button right under the Company File row instead of hiding behind a "Manage company" fold-out; the standalone Team page shows the same button at its end. If deleting or leaving fails, the error shows right under the button instead of at the top of the page. Settings search matches each word on its own, so "delete company" finds the Company tab.
- Open an agent's routines and responsibilities as pages of its details popup, from two buttons beside the close button, each with a way back; routines left the Computer panel.
- Edit an agent's name, title and description in place, saving on Enter or when leaving the field, and change the figure from a picker under it that saves each choice, as on the desktop; the Edit agent button is gone and the description is taller.
- Replace the new-conversation page (agent cards, heading, Open/Cancel footer) with one list under the To: field: New agent, New group chat, then the agents. The field searches agent names, picking an agent opens its conversation, New group chat gathers agents as chips, and a name typed into the field carries into a new agent. Group conversations still cannot be started.
- The sign-in page no longer says it uses the same account as the desktop app, which is gone; it keeps the line about the session in this browser. The fonts and provider logos now live in `packages/web/public/assets` and ship at the same paths; the nine icons the app never used are no longer shipped.

### Removed

- Remove the Sites page (Jonas, 24 Nov 2026: "delete the sites interface"): the page (`views/settings-sites.ts`), its `#/c/{id}/sites` route (a typed or old address opens the company home, as any section the web does not know), its Sites tile in the apps drawer (a saved drawer layout or `apps/groups.md` that still names `sites` draws no tile for it), its styles, strings and tests, the web's `setPassword` and `unpublish` site calls, `CompanyState.siteVersions` and What's new's `liveVersions`. The pieces other views use moved to `views/site-shared.ts`. Nothing moved anywhere else. A taken-down site's card under a message and the taken-down line in a thread and in What's new no longer link anywhere; What's new's ten-site line and the card's copy fallback name no place. What can no longer be done in the app: list the company's sites; take any site offline (which also frees one of the company's ten sites, and for a project's code site resets its release so the newest change waits again); read the operator's whole takedown sentence with its Feedback hint; see the Webapp or Hybrid badge; for an app link, see its password on screen, change it, make it public or private, and add or remove its custom domains; for a website with no interface (an agent's `publish_site` site, a site from before interfaces, a deleted interface's site, a project's code site with none), go live or stop being live and add, check, make primary or remove a custom domain; for a website with an interface, pick its primary domain by hand, check DNS by hand, and open or copy its link from the address card while it is not live. The server functions behind them stay.
- Remove the "Live preview / Studio files" switch from the New surface window (23 Nov 2026). It showed only for Custom Surface and asked how the interface is built before the member had said what it is for. A Custom Surface made in the window is now always built and previewed live, as the switch's default was; the window keeps the name, the brief and the optional first change. Kinds with their own row (Identity, Database, Product Studio, Report & Analysis) still start as a folder of files, and `createSurface` still takes either kind.
- Remove the generic interface renderer's parts no shipped page reaches (#1949, 18 Nov 2026). Mailbox, Members and Bank draw their own views, and the Data page before its databases are read draws one static text block, which is all the renderer draws now. Gone with it: `app/interface-board.ts`, `app/interface-areas.ts`, `app/views/interface-mail.ts` and `app/views/interface-blocks.ts`; in `views/interface.ts` the views and tabs, the list, table, detail, board, mail, numbers and calendar blocks, the row buttons and `renderInterfaceFile`'s export; in `app/interfaces.ts` `interfaceRows` (the `working` source, the `waiting` rows, the `members` rows, the folder sources), `InterfaceRow`, `rowHasCard`, `isLiveSource`, `tableDefinition`, `idColumn`, `drawnParts`, `blockButtons`, `DrawnButton`, `memberIds`, `memberMayWriteRows`, `namesLeaver`, `leaverName`, `memberOffers` and `offeredRow`; in `app/interface-sources.ts` the folder sources (`isFolderSource`, `folderSourceColumns`, `folderIdColumn`, `folderSourcePlan`, `folderSourceState`, `folderSourceRows`); in `app/interface-folder.ts` their listing and reads (`InterfaceFolderState.sources`); in `app/interface-claims.ts` `claimTableCell`, `claimFailure`, `ClaimVerb`, `ClaimOffer`, `ClaimLostError` and `InterfaceWrites.folder/current/offers/reread`; in `app/interface-mail.ts` `mailRequest`, `mailRows`, `mailSourceRows`, `openedMailRow`, `rowThread` and `isAccountMail`; `CompanyController.noProgress`. The Mailbox's badge counts its waiting sends directly.
- Remove the offers and supervision consents reader (`app/interface-offers.ts` `CompanyInterfaceOffers`, `cloud/interface-offers.ts`, `cloud/interface-supervisions.ts`, `CompanyState.interfaceOffers`). The web no longer calls `qorpera_member_interface_offers` or `qorpera_member_interface_supervisions` (on opening, focus, each minute and every folder change); no page drew them.
- Remove reads nothing showed: `qorpera_get_account_entitlement` (`CloudApi.getAccountEntitlement`, `AccountViewState.loading/loaded/entitlement`) on Settings and the account page, and the conversation's turn history (`WebNativeSubscription.listSessionTurnProvenance`) on each account refresh.
- Remove code nothing called: `app/views/live-company-file.ts` (`LiveCompanyFileView`), `app/views/settings-coding-environment.ts` (`CodingEnvironmentView`, `CodingEnvironmentAccess`) and its CSS, `CloudApi.appendPrompt`, `WebConnectors.captureToken` and `HOSTED_CONNECTORS[].token`, `appLayoutChanges`, `atComputerSeed`, `appNoteText`, `QUIET_SCROLL_FADE`, and `SessionRunView`'s unmounted status element (`el`).

- Remove the Danish text of member-web's Company File panel, which went with snapshot-v1 (step C, C2). The translation test no longer reads `member-web/src/file-panel.ts`. "More files", "Open" and the paused-agents line, which the web app also writes, stay.
- Remove three lines from each account card in Settings → Connections: the "Connected <date> · <id>" line, the usage bar's name ("Weekly"), and "Last checked <time>". Each comes back only where it is needed: a card with two bars (5 hours and Weekly) names them, an old reading dims the bar and says when it was taken ("92% at last check · 02:15 AM", never "left", since the limit may be reached since), and two cards for the same provider and email say when each was added and end with the account's short id, so Sign out is not a guess. Hovering the percentage shows when usage was last checked, Refresh reads "Updated" for a moment once a new reading is in (pressed during a background read, it makes that read take a new one; a background read ends "Updated"), and screen readers still hear each bar's name.
- Remove Company execution from Settings → Company: the pause and resume buttons, the spend, token and wake status line, and the monthly spend, monthly token and hourly wake limit fields. Notices about a reached company limit or a paused company no longer show above the composer, and older ones are skipped when notices load; the "all company computers are in use" notice stays.
- Remove the Organization and Approvals cards from Settings → Company, and their pages: the Organization page with its project pages, and the read-only Approvals list (it only gathered the old authorization requests the cloud runtime never sends). Old links open the company home. Cards an agent raises in its own thread, such as an approval card, are unchanged.
- Remove the member notification inbox: the rail's Notifications row and its unread-count polling, and the Notifications page and route (old links open the company home). Execution notices and explicit acknowledgment remain available inline in the workspace.
- Remove the standalone Friends (`#/friends`) and Invitations (`#/invitations`) pages and the separate Invitations menu item; both live on the Settings Friends tab.
- Remove Stop sharing with agents, Share with all agents and Reconnect from a connected service in Settings → Connections → Services. A company connection is always available to every agent, new hires included, so a connected service shows that line and one action, Disconnect; a connection not yet marked for all agents reads the same.
- Remove the "Cloud endpoint (advanced)" section from Settings → General and the standalone `#/account` page: the web client version line and the project URL, publishable key and object gateway fields with Use these endpoints and Back to production. On release builds that button only failed. The browser endpoint override behind it is gone too: every build now uses the endpoints compiled into it and never reads a value saved by the old form, and the deployment manifest's `configOverrideAllowed` is now `false` for every build. The client version stays in `web-deployment.json`.
- Remove the Cloud check page and its RPC probe; `#/cloud-check` now opens the companies page.
- Remove the Companies section ("All companies — Open or create a company") from Settings → General, which now ends at Language & region. The company list still opens at `#/`.
- Remove the "Progress" line under an agent message written while its turn is still running. The message keeps its smaller bubble.
- Remove the web's snapshot-v1 Company File reader (legacy sweep step C, C1): the object reader (`cloud/objects.ts`) and its download grant call (`qorpera_authorize_profile_object_download`), the Company File snapshot and tree manifest types, and the snapshot-v1 fields of the Company File read (`canonical_workspace_id`, `state`, `head_snapshot_id`, `logical_bytes`, `retained_storage_bytes`, `object_backends`) and of a session (`company_snapshot_id`). Nothing read them: the Company File page reads the company folder, and Settings reads only the storage used and allowed.
